Bounds for Genus Zero Gromov-Witten Invariants.
Mark McLean

Abstract
. We give a bound for the norm of each primary genus zero Gromov-Witten invariant in any smooth projective variety. This bound depends only on the maximum degree of its defining polynomials, the norms of the differential forms representing each insertion, the number of marked points and the degree of the curves. These bounds grow factorially in the number of marked points and a multiple of the degree. This implies that the Borel transform of the genus zero primary generating function converges absolutely in a particular translation of the ample cone. To prove these bounds, we start with Siebert’s formula, expressing genus zero Gromov-Witten invariants in terms of the Segre class of the normal cone of the underlying moduli space inside the space of curves mapping to projective space and the Chern classes of the bundle whose fiber over a curve is the space of sections of the pulled back normal bundle. We then use this description to bound each primary Gromov-Witten invariant in terms of an intermediate count of curves called the D D -volume. A deformation to the normal cone argument is then used to compare such an intermediate count with one in projective space.



1.
Introduction

We establish upper bounds for primary genus-zero Gromov-Witten invariants of smooth projective varieties.
We will prove the following main theorem:

Theorem 1.1
.

Let
X\subset\mathbb{P}^{N}
be a smooth projective subvariety cut out by polynomials of degree at most
\delta_{X}\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
d\in H_{2}(X;\mathbb{Z})
be a curve class and let
\deg(d)\in\mathbb{N}
be its image in
H_{2}(\mathbb{P}^{N};\mathbb{Z})\cong\mathbb{Z}
.
Then for each tuple
\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{m}
of homogenous closed differential forms on
X
we have the following bound of primary genus
0
Gromov-Witten invariants:

(1.1)

\left|\langle[\alpha_{1}],\cdots,[\alpha_{m}]\rangle^{X}_{m,d}\right|\leqslant m!\left(4952{N+(4\delta_{X})^{2^{N-1}}+1\choose N}^{6}(\deg(d)+1)\right)!\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

where
|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}
is the
C^{0}
norm of
\alpha_{j}
with respect to the metric on
X
coming from the Fubini-Study form
\omega
1
1

1

We use the convention that the Fubini-Study form is Poincaré dual to the hyperplane.
for each
j
.

This is the first result of its kind providing a bound for all primary genus zero Gromov-Witten invariants in terms of the geometry of the target projective variety.
These bounds are very, very far from being optimal even in elementary examples
2
2

2

These inequalities say that there are at most
10
to the power of
600
million conics passing through five general points in the plane.
. For instance, we do not prove that the primary generating function converges, which is conjectured in
[
KM94
, Section 4.3]
,
[
CK99
, Conjecture 8.1.2]
and
[
Zin14
, Conjecture 1]
and known in many examples (See
[
Iri07
]
,
[
Zin14
]
,
[
CI15
]
and
[
Kot22
]
).
It is possible using the methods of this paper to improve the inequality (
1.1
), but we have chosen brevity instead.
A very different possible approach to giving bounds in the symplectic setting is contained in
[
Swa21
]
.

We have the following consequence of the theorem above. Let

H^{>2,\textnormal{even}}(X):=\bigoplus_{j>1}H^{2j}(X;\mathbb{R}).

Let
\textnormal{Amp}(X)\subset H^{2}(X;\mathbb{R})
be the ample cone of
X
.
We also let
H_{2}^{+}(X)\subset H_{2}(X;\mathbb{Z})
be the subset of classes represented by proper curves mapping to
X
.
We write
\Gamma
for the
\Gamma
-function.

Corollary 1.1
.

Let
\Omega_{X}:=19808{N+(4\delta_{X})^{2^{N-1}+1}\choose N}^{6}[\omega|_{X}]\in\textnormal{Amp}(X)
.
Then the Borel transform of the primary generating function:

B:(\Omega_{X}+\textnormal{Amp}(X))\times H^{>2,\textnormal{even}}(X)\times\mathbb{R}_{>0}\to\mathbb{R},

(1.2)

B(\eta,t^{+},s):=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N},\ d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)}\frac{\exp(d\cdot\eta)}{m!\,\Gamma(d\cdot\eta+1)}\langle t^{+},\cdots,t^{+}\rangle^{X}_{m,d}s^{d\cdot\eta}

converges absolutely and hence is analytic.

Proof.

We have

(1.3)

\frac{1}{2}(d\cdot\eta)\geqslant 4952{N+(4\delta_{X})^{2^{N-1}}+1\choose N}^{6}(\deg(d)+3),\quad d\cdot\eta\geqslant(N+1)\deg(d)+N-3

for each
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)-0
and each
\eta\in\Omega_{X}+\textnormal{Amp}(X)
.
Since
t^{+}
is a sum of classes of degree higher than
2
and by Lemma
5.2
, we have

(1.4)

m\leqslant(N+1)\deg(d)+N-3\overset{\textnormal{\eqref{eqncdotedt}}}{\leqslant}d\cdot\eta

if
\langle t^{+},\cdots,t^{+}\rangle^{X}_{m,d}\neq 0
.
Hence, by Theorem
1.1
combined with the fact that the sum of the
|\cdot|_{C^{0},\omega}
-norms of the homogenous components of a form on
X
is at most
2^{2\dim(X)}
times its
|\cdot|_{C^{0},\omega}
-norm, we get

(1.5)

\displaystyle\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\left|\frac{\exp(d\cdot\eta)}{m!\,\Gamma(d\cdot\eta+1)}\langle t^{+},\cdots,t^{+}\rangle^{X}_{m,d}s^{d\cdot\eta}\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{m=0}^{\lfloor d\cdot\eta\rfloor}\frac{m!\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}d\cdot\eta+1)(es(2^{2\dim(X)}|t^{+}|_{C^{0},\omega}+1))^{d\cdot\eta}}{m!\Gamma((d\cdot\eta+1))}

\displaystyle\leqslant(d\cdot\eta+1)\frac{((es(2^{2\dim(X)}|t^{+}|_{C^{0},\omega}+1))^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}d\cdot\eta}}{\Gamma(\frac{1}{2}(d\cdot\eta))}

for each
(\eta,t^{+},s)\in(\Omega_{X}+\textnormal{Amp}(X))\times H_{2}^{>2,\textnormal{even}}(X)\times\mathbb{R}
,
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)-0
.
So the sum (
1.2
) converges absolutely.
∎

It would be nice if the Legendre transform in the
s
-variable for
B
existed since this would produce an analytic function whose asymptotic power series at
0
would be equal to the primary generating function. Such an analytic function would satisfy the WDVV equation for instance.

We will now give a brief sketch of the proof of Theorem
1.1
.
The key idea is to try to compare Gromov-Witten invariants of
X
with those of projective space.
For each variety
Y
, we let
H_{2}^{+}(Y)\subset H_{2}(Y;\mathbb{Z})
be the subset of homology classes represented by morphisms of curves to
Y
.
We let
Y_{m,d}
be the moduli space
of stable genus zero nodal curves with
m
marked points representing
d
for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
d\in H_{2}^{+}(Y)
.
For any smooth hypersurface
E\subset Y
, we let
Y_{m+E!,d}\subset Y_{m+E\cdot d,d}
be the closure of the space of curves
u
transverse to
E
with
u^{-1}(E)
the union of the last
E\cdot d
marked points.
For any vector bundle
\pi_{W}:W\to Y
, we define
W_{m,d}:=W_{m,\iota_{*}d}
where
\iota:Y\hookrightarrow W
is the zero section inclusion map.
We let
W_{m+E!,d}:=W_{m+(\pi_{W}^{-1}E)!,\iota_{*}d}\subset W_{m+E\cdot d,d}
.
Note that these moduli spaces have some similarities with those in
[
CM
]
.

In order to get bounds for genus zero Gromov-Witten invariants we will use a description of the virtual fundamental class by Siebert (
[
Sie04
]
) as a homogenous component of the cap product of the Segre class of the normal cone of
X_{m,d}
inside
\mathbb{P}^{N}_{m,{\iota_{X}}_{*}d}

with the total Chern class of
NX_{m,d}
where
NX\to X
is the normal bundle of
X
in
\mathbb{P}^{N}

and
\iota_{X}:X\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{N}
is the inclusion map.
As a result, we first find bounds for integrals of the form:

(1.6)

\langle[\alpha_{1}],\cdots,[\alpha_{m}]\rangle^{s(C_{\bullet})}_{m,d}:=\int_{s(C_{m,d})}\prod_{i=1}^{m}\operatorname{ev}_{i}^{*}\alpha_{j}

where
\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{m}
are homogenous de Rham forms on
X
,
C_{m,d}\to X_{m,d}
is a subcone of
V_{m,d}
for some vector bundle
V
which is a pullback of a cone in
V_{0,d}
under the forgetful map and
s(C_{m,d})
is its Segre class.
This is done in terms of the
D
-volume

D\textnormal{-vol}(C_{m,d})
(Definition
4.13
).
To define the
D
-volume for a smooth divisor
D
we first define
C_{m+D!,d}
to be the pullback of the cone
C_{m,d}
to
X_{m+D!,d}
under the forgetful map forgetting the last
D\cdot d
marked points.
Let
\operatorname{ev}:X_{m+D!,d}\to X^{m^{\prime}}
and

\operatorname{ev}:C_{m+D!,d}\to V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}
be the evaluation maps where
V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}\to X^{m^{\prime}}
is the product vector bundle and
m^{\prime}=m+D\cdot d
.
Let
P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}:=\mathbb{P}(V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}\oplus\mathbb{C})
be the projective compactification of
V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}
.
Suppose
\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}}:P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}\to X^{m^{\prime}}
is the natural projection map and
p_{i}:X^{m^{\prime}}\to X
the
i
th projection map for each
i
.
Let
\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}:C_{m+D!,d}\to\operatorname{ev}^{*}V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}
be the natural evaluation map and let
P\operatorname{ev}:\operatorname{ev}^{*}P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}\to P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}
be the natural projection map.
We let
\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+D!,d})}
be the closure of the image
\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+D!,d})
in
\operatorname{ev}^{*}P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}
.
We let
D_{\sum m^{\prime},V}:=\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}}^{*}\sum_{i=1}^{m^{\prime}}p_{i}^{*}D+\infty_{V^{\boxtimes m}}
be a divisor in
P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}

where
\infty_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}
is the divisor at infinity.
The
D
-volume is then defined to be:

(1.7)

D\textnormal{-vol}(C_{m,d}):=\frac{1}{m!(D\cdot d)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+D!,d})}]}\exp(D_{\sum m^{\prime},V}).

More generally we define
D\textnormal{-vol}(C^{i}_{m,d})_{i\in I}
for multiple cones
C_{m,d}
,
i\in I
using a fiber product of bundles of the form
P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}
over
X^{m^{\prime}}

(See Definition
4.13
).

Next, we give give bounds for the norm of the integral (
1.6
) in terms of
D\textnormal{-vol}(C_{m,d})

(Proposition
5.9
)
and conversely bounds for
D\textnormal{-vol}(C_{m,d})
in terms of the norm of integral (
1.6
) for certain
D
(Proposition
5.14
).
Let us explain the key idea behind the proof of Proposition
5.9
in the special case where
m=1
,
X=Y\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
,
d=[\textnormal{pt}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}]
and we have a single cone
C
inside a vector bundle
V_{h,d}
over
X
where
V
is the pullback of a vector bundle over
Y
. In this case,
X_{m,d}=X
and
V_{m,d}=V
.
Let
\overline{C}
be the closure of
C
inside
\mathbb{P}(V\oplus\mathbb{C})
. Let
\infty_{V}=\mathbb{P}(V\oplus 0)
be the divisor at infinity in
\mathbb{P}(V\oplus\mathbb{C})
and let
\pi:\mathbb{P}(V\oplus\mathbb{C})\to X
be the natural projection map.
Suppose
D
is far inside the ample cone of
X
and linearly equivalent to
4D^{\prime}
for some
D^{\prime}
. Let
\omega^{\prime}
and
\omega_{C}
be Kähler forms Poincaré dual to
D^{\prime}
and
2\pi^{*}D^{\prime}+\infty_{V}
respectively.
The norm of (
1.6
) in our special case is
less than or equal to

(1.8)

\sum_{r}\left|\int_{\overline{C}}\pi^{*}\alpha_{1}\cup\infty_{V}^{r}\right|=\sum_{r=1}^{\dim(C)}\left|\int_{\overline{C}}\pi^{*}\alpha\cup((2\pi^{*}D^{\prime}+\infty_{V})-(2\pi^{*}D^{\prime}))^{r}\right|

(1.9)

\leqslant\sum_{r=1}^{\dim(C)}\sum_{k=1}^{r}{r\choose k}\left|\int_{\overline{C}}\pi^{*}\alpha_{1}\cup(\omega_{C})^{r-k}\cup(2\pi^{*}\omega^{\prime})^{k}\right|

(1.10)

\overset{\textnormal{Remark \ref{remarkcomparisonofnorms}}}{\leqslant}\sum_{r=1}^{\dim(C)}\sum_{k=1}^{r}{r\choose k}\left|\pi^{*}\alpha_{1}\cup\omega_{C}^{r-k}\cup(\pi^{*}\omega_{D^{\prime}})^{k}\right|_{C^{0},\omega_{C}+2\pi^{*}\omega^{\prime}}\int_{\overline{C}}\exp(\omega_{C}+2\pi^{*}\omega^{\prime})

(1.11)

\overset{\textnormal{Lemmas \ref{lemmaproductnorms} and \ref{lemmaaboutpositivedefiniteforms}}}{\leqslant}C\left|\alpha_{1}\right|_{C^{0},\omega}\int_{\overline{C}}\exp(\pi^{*}D+\infty_{V})\leqslant C\left|\alpha_{1}\right|_{C^{0},\omega}D\textnormal{-vol}(C_{h,d})

where

(1.12)

C=\sum_{r=1}^{\dim(C)}\sum_{k=1}^{r}{r\choose k}2^{2\dim(X)}{2\dim(X)\choose 2}^{k}(r-k)!{\dim(C)\choose r-k}\dim(X)^{k}.

In order for the proof of Proposition
5.9
to work in general, we need to embed our cone
C_{m+D!,d}
in
\operatorname{ev}^{*}V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}
where
\operatorname{ev}:X_{m+D!,d}\to X^{m^{\prime}}
is the evaluation map.
Lemma
4.19
does this showing that the natural map
V_{m+D!,d}\to\operatorname{ev}^{*}V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}
is injective for appropriate
D
. This lemma only works for moduli spaces of the form
X_{m+D!,d}
, rather than
X_{m,d}
, since there are many marked points on each irreducible component of each curve.

In the rest of Section
5
we show that the
D
-volume satisfies some other useful technical properties.
One of these technical theorems allows us to bound the
D
-volumes of cones of the form
V_{m,d}
for a convex vector bundle
V
using curves in
P_{V}
(Proposition
5.21
). This is important because the total Chern class of
NX_{m,d}
appears in Siebert’s description of the virtual fundamental class.

Now, one important cone is the image
C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}}_{m,d}
in
NX_{m,d}
of the normal cone of
X_{m,d}
inside
\mathbb{P}^{N}_{m,(\iota_{X})_{*}d}
.
In Section
6

we bound
D|_{X}\textnormal{-vol}(C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}}_{m,d},(C^{i}_{m,d}|_{X_{m,d}})_{i\in I})

by
D\textnormal{-vol}(C^{i}_{m,d})_{i\in I}
where
C^{i}_{m,d}
,
i\in I
are certain cones over
\mathbb{P}^{N}_{m,(\iota_{X})_{*}d}
and
D
is a generic divisor in
\mathbb{P}^{N}
of sufficiently high degree (See Proposition
6.5
).
Let us illustrate the proof of Proposition
6.5
in the case where
X=\mathbb{P}^{1}
is a line inside
\mathbb{P}^{2}
,
m=0
,
d=[X]
,
D
is a generic degree
2
hypersurface and
I
is a singleton set with the only cone being
0
.
In this case,
X_{0,h+D|_{X}!,d}
consists of two points. Its normal cone inside
\mathbb{P}^{2}_{0,h+D!,(\iota_{X})_{*}d}
is
X_{0,h+D|_{X}!,d}\times\mathbb{C}^{2}
. Hence,
D|_{X}\textnormal{-vol}(C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{2}}_{h,d})=\frac{1}{2}
.
Now,
\mathbb{P}^{2}_{0,h+D!,(\iota_{X})_{*}d}=D\times D\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
since each curve is determined by its intersection with
D\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}
and these intersection points are labelled.
The image of this space under the evaluation map is the product embedding
D\times D\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}
. Hence,
D\textnormal{-vol}(\mathbb{P}^{2}_{h,(\iota_{X})_{*}d}(0))
is one-quarter of the self intersection of this image which is
4
.
Let us describe the geometric mechanism demonstrating
D|_{X}\textnormal{-vol}(C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{2}}_{h,d})\leqslant D\textnormal{-vol}(\mathbb{P}^{2}_{h,(\iota_{X})_{*}d})(0)
in our special case.
Let
\chi
be the blowup of
0\times X
inside
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{2}
, let
E
be its exceptional divisor and
\widetilde{D}
the proper transform of
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times D
in
\chi
.
Let
\chi_{2}:=\chi\times_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}\chi

and let
\Pi:\chi_{2}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
be the corresponding projection map.
Define
\pi_{k}:\chi_{2}\to\chi
,
k=1,2
to be the projection maps to each factor.
Let
D_{2}:=\widetilde{D}\times_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}\widetilde{D}\subset\chi_{2}
.
In the proof Proposition
6.5
,
D_{2}
is the image under the evaluation map of a moduli space of curves.
Define
D_{E}
to be
D_{2}\cap E^{2}
minus the preimage of the diagonal in
X\times X
under the blowdown map.
Then since
\widetilde{D}-E
is nef, we have

(1.13)

D|_{X}\textnormal{-vol}(C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{2}}_{0,d})=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{P}^{2}\sqcup\mathbb{P}^{2}}\exp(\mathbb{P}^{1}\sqcup\mathbb{P}^{1})

(1.14)

\leqslant\frac{1}{2}\int_{(\mathbb{P}^{1})^{2}\sqcup(\mathbb{P}^{1})^{2}}\exp((\infty\times\mathbb{P}^{1}+\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\infty)\sqcup(\infty\times\mathbb{P}^{1}+\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\infty))

(1.15)

=\frac{1}{2}\int_{D_{E}}\exp(\pi_{1}^{*}(\widetilde{D}-E)+\pi_{2}^{*}(\widetilde{D}-E))

(1.16)

\leqslant\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Pi^{-1}(0)\cap D_{2}}\exp(\pi_{1}^{*}(\widetilde{D}-E)+\pi_{2}^{*}(\widetilde{D}-E))

(1.17)

=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Pi^{-1}(1)\cap D_{2}}\exp(\pi_{1}^{*}(\widetilde{D}-E)+\pi_{2}^{*}(\widetilde{D}-E))=D\textnormal{-vol}(\mathbb{P}^{2}_{0,(\iota_{X})_{*}d}(0)).

Using Siebert’s description of the virtual fundamental class we next describe the virtual fundamental class in terms of Segre classes of the cones
NX_{m,d}

and
C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}}_{m,d}
. Using this we then give upper bounds for norms Gromov-Witten invariants in terms of
D
-volumes of these cones (Section
7
).
In fact, we use cones coming from vector bundles
T
and
W
where there exists a short exact sequence of convex vector bundles

(1.18)

0\to NX\to T\to W\to 0

where
T
, later on, is the restriction of a sum of line bundles on
\mathbb{P}^{N}
. This is because the tools above compute Segre classes rather than Chern classes and
the total Chern class of
NX_{m,d}
is
c(T_{m,d})\cap s(W_{m,d})
and the total Chern class
c(T_{m,d})
can be computed more easily since it extends to a sum of line bundles over
\mathbb{P}^{N}
.

In Section
8
we give explicit upper bounds for Gromov-Witten invariants twisted by sums of line bundles over products of projective space.
This in turn allows us to give upper bounds for the
D
-volumes involving the bundle
T_{m,d}
. Since these bounds only need to be very coarse we just use the localization formula
[
Kon95
]
directly without needing any more sophisticated arguments (E.g. we don’t need to work as hard as
[
Zin14
]
).

Finally, in Section
9
we assemble all of these ingredients together.
Here is also where we use the constant
\delta_{X}
. Such a constant can be used to bound the Castelnuovo-Mumford regularity of the defining ideal of
X
as well as its square.
The bound is needed to embed certain projectivizations of vector bundles over
X
into products of projective space of bounded dimension and degree.
It also allows us to construct the short exact sequence (
1.18
) above.
Once we have this data we can use the theorems involving
D
-volumes above to bound the norm of our Gromov-Witten invariants (See Theorem
9.1
).

Conjecturally, bounds for genus zero Gromov-Witten invariants should involve an exponential of a multiple of the degree, rather than a multiple of the degree factorial
(
[
KM94
, Section 4.3]
,
[
CK99
, Conjecture 8.1.2]
and
[
Zin14
, Conjecture 1]
).
It is the techniques used to prove Propositions
5.9
and
6.5
which are largely responsible for this factorial, rather than an exponential bound, as seen in the calculations above.
At this time the author has no idea how to improve them to get the conjectured exponential bound.

2.
Notation and Conventions

The natural numbers
\mathbb{N}
start from
0
. We write
\mathbb{N}_{>0}
if we wish to start from
1
.
By
stack
we will mean a separated Deligne Mumford stack of finite type over
\mathbb{C}
with quasi-projective coarse moduli space and with the fppf topology unless stated otherwise.
The only exception to this rule is when we refer to the moduli stack of genus zero pre-stable curves
\mathfrak{M}_{m}
(Section
7.1
).
If
(Y_{i})_{i\in I}
is a collection of stacks over a stack
X
indexed by a set
I
, we define

\sideset{}{{}_{X}}{\prod}_{i\in I}Y_{i}

to be their fiber product over
X
.

Definition 2.1
.

Let
Y
be a smooth quasi-projective variety.
We define
H_{2}^{+}(Y)\subset H_{2}(Y;\mathbb{Z})
to be the submonoid of classes represented by morphisms from curves to
Y
.
For each
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
d\in H_{2}^{+}(Y)
,
we let
Y_{m,d}
be the moduli stack
of stable genus zero curves with
m
marked points
representing
d
.
We let
\operatorname{ev}_{i}:Y_{m,d}\to Y

be the evaluation map at the
i
th marked point for each
i=1,\cdots,m

and
\operatorname{ev}:=\prod_{i=1}^{m}\operatorname{ev}_{i}:Y_{m,d}\to Y^{m}
.
We let
\pi_{j}:Y_{m,d}\to Y_{m-1,d}
be the
j
th forgetful map for each
j=1,\cdots,m
.
Let
[Y_{m,d}]^{\operatorname{vir}}
be the virtual fundamental class
(See
[
BF97
]
and
[
Beh97
]
).

For any stack
M
,
CH_{*}(M)
will be the Chow group
of
M
with
rational coefficient
s as defined in

[
Gil84
]
and
[
Vis89
]
.
So Chow cycles will be rational linear combinations of integral closed substacks.
Now the stack
M
has an underlying coarse moduli space,
which is a variety
\underline{M}
.
Such a variety has an analytic topology since it is of finite type over
\mathbb{C}
.

All singular (co)homology groups have rational coefficients unless stated otherwise.
In other words,
H^{*}(A)=H^{*}(A;\mathbb{Q})
and
H_{*}(A)=H_{*}(A;\mathbb{Q})
for any topological space
A
.
We define
H^{\textnormal{even}}(A)
to be the even dimensional cohomology of
A
with coefficients in
\mathbb{Q}
.
If we talk about singular (Borel Moore) homology or singular cohomology
H^{*}(M)

of our stack
M
,
then we mean with respect to the analytic topology on
\underline{M}
.

If we have a singular cohomology cycle
\alpha
on
\underline{M}
with respect to the analytic topology above
and a class
C\in CH_{*}(M)
,
then we can pair them, giving a rational number.
This pairing is done first by pushing
C
forward to
\underline{M}

and then pairing with
\alpha
.
We will call this rational number the
integral of
\alpha
over
C

and write it as
\int_{C}\alpha
.
More generally, if we are given a power series expression with Chern class parameters
c
then we define
\int_{C}\alpha\cup c=\int_{C\cap c}\alpha
.

If
V\to X
is a vector bundle then we define
\mathbb{P}(V)
to be equal to
\operatorname{Proj}(\mathrm{Sym}(V^{*}))
(I.e. the Grassman bundle of
1
-planes in
V
rather than dimension
1
quotients).

We write
\operatorname{Coh}(M)
to be the category of coherent sheaves on
M
.
For any sheaf or complex of sheaves
\mathcal{F}
over a stack
Z
, we let
\mathcal{F}^{\vee}=Hom(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{O}_{Z})

be its dual.

3.
Systems of Classes

In this section we define Gromov-Witten counts for systems of fundamental classes satisfying a forgetful axiom.
Let
X
be a smooth projective variety.

Definition 3.1
.

A
system of classes
for
X
is a collection

(3.1)

[]^{\prime}:=([X_{m,d}]^{\prime})_{m\in\mathbb{N},\ d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)},

(3.2)

[X_{m,d}]^{\prime}\in CH_{*}(X_{m,d}),\ \ m\in\mathbb{N},\ d\in H_{2}^{+}(X),

of classes
so that the following property holds:

(Forget.)

For each
m,j\in\mathbb{N}
,
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
with
1\leqslant j\leqslant m
,
we have
\pi_{j}^{*}([X_{m-1,d}]^{\prime})=[X_{m,d}]^{\prime}
.

Example 3.2
.

[]^{\operatorname{vir}}=([X_{m,d}]^{\operatorname{vir}})_{m\in\mathbb{N},\ d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)}

is a system of fundamental classes for
X
.
The (Forget.) axiom is proven in
[
Beh97
, Axiom IV]
.

Definition 3.3
.

Define

(3.3)

\langle a_{1},\cdots,a_{m}\rangle^{[]^{\prime}}_{m,d}=\int_{[X_{m,d}]^{\prime}}\prod_{k=1}^{m}ev_{k}^{*}a_{k}

for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
a_{1},\cdots,a_{m}\in H^{*}(X)
and each
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
.
We also define

(3.4)

\langle a_{1},\cdots,a_{m}\rangle^{X}_{m,d}:=\langle a_{1},\cdots,a_{m}\rangle^{[]^{\operatorname{vir}}}_{m,d}

for each such
m,a_{1},\cdots,a_{m},d
.

4.
Systems of Cones

4.1.
Definition of Systems of Cones

Later on in Section
7.1
, we describe the virtual fundamental class in terms of Segre and Chern classes of various cones. As a result it is natural to define systems of cones.
Let us fix a smooth projective variety
X
and a vector bundle
\pi:V\to X
.

Definition 4.1
.

Let
D\subset X
be a divisor, let
\textnormal{supp}(D)
be its support and let
\textnormal{supp}(D)^{\mathrm{sing}}

be the singular locus of its support.
Suppose
u:\Sigma\to X
is a morphism from a nodal curve
\Sigma
.
Define
\Sigma^{\mathrm{sing}}\subset\Sigma
to be the set of nodal points.
We say that
u
is
transverse
to
D

if

(1)

the map
u|_{\Sigma-\Sigma^{\mathrm{sing}}}
is transverse to
\textnormal{supp}(D)-\textnormal{supp}(D)^{\mathrm{sing}}
,

(2)

\textnormal{supp}(D)^{\mathrm{sing}}\cap u(\Sigma)=\emptyset
and

(3)

u(\Sigma^{\mathrm{sing}})\cap D=\emptyset
.

Definition 4.2
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
.
Let
D\subset X
be a reduced divisor.
Define

(4.1)

\mathring{X}_{m+D!,d}\subset X_{m+D\cdot d,d}

to be the substack parameterizing flat families of curves where the image in
X_{0,d}
of each curve
u
corresponding to a
\mathbb{C}
-point in each such family is transverse to
D
and where
u^{-1}(D)
is the union of the last
D\cdot d
marked points.

We define

(4.2)

X_{m+D!,d}\subset X_{m+D\cdot d,d}

to be the closure of
\mathring{X}_{m+D!,d}
.
We have the following evaluation map

(4.3)

\operatorname{ev}:X_{m+D!,d}\to X^{m+D\cdot d},\quad\operatorname{ev}:=\prod_{j=1}^{m+D\cdot d}\operatorname{ev}_{j}.

Let
\pi:V\to X
be a vector bundle over
X
.

Definition 4.3
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
.
We define

(4.4)

V_{m,d}=V_{m,\iota_{*}d}

where
\iota:X\hookrightarrow V
is zero section map.
This has a natural projection map:

(4.5)

\pi_{V_{m,d}}:V_{m,d}\to X_{m,d}

sending
u
to
\pi\circ u
.
Define
V^{\boxtimes m}:=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{m}p_{i}^{*}V

where
p_{i}:X^{m}\to X
is the
i
th projection map for each
i=1,\cdots,m
.
We define

(4.6)

\operatorname{ev}:V_{m,d}\to V^{\boxtimes m}

to be the evaluation map given by
\operatorname{ev}_{1}\times\cdots\times\operatorname{ev}_{m}
.
We let

(4.7)

\operatorname{ev}^{*}V^{\boxtimes m}\to X_{m,d}

be the pullback of the vector bundle
V^{\boxtimes m}\to X^{m}

via the map
\operatorname{ev}:X_{m,d}\to X^{m}
.
Define

(4.8)

\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}:V_{m,d}\to\operatorname{ev}^{*}V^{\boxtimes m}

to be the pullback of the map (
4.6
).
Now, let
D
be a reduced divisor in
X
.
Define

(4.9)

V_{m+D!,d}:=V_{m+\pi^{-1}(D)!,\iota_{*}d}.

Define

(4.10)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}:V_{m+D!,d}\to\operatorname{ev}^{*}V^{\boxtimes m+D\cdot d}

to be the restriction of

\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}:V_{m+D\cdot d,d}\to\operatorname{ev}^{*}V^{\boxtimes m+D\cdot d}
to
V_{m+D!,d}
.

Since
V
is a vector bundle, we get that
V_{m,d}
is a cone over
X_{m,d}
where the
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-action is given by post composing each curve by the scaling action on
V
.

Definition 4.4
.

A
system of cones for
V
is a collection

(4.11)

C_{\bullet}=(C_{m,d})_{m\in\mathbb{N},\ d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)}

where
C_{m,d}
is a (not necessarily closed) subcone of
V_{m,d}
so that the pullback of
C_{m,d}
to
X_{m+1,d}
via the forgetful map
\pi_{j}
is
C_{m+1,d}

under the identification
V_{m+1,d}\cong\pi_{j}^{*}V_{m,d}
for each
m,j\in\mathbb{N}
with
1\leqslant j\leqslant m
and
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
.

We define
V_{\bullet}
to be the following system of cones for
V
:

(4.12)

V_{\bullet}=(V_{m,d})_{m\in\mathbb{N},\ d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)}.

Now let
D
be a smooth hypersurface.
For each
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
,
we define
C_{m+D!,d}
to be the pullback of the cone
C_{m,d}
to
V_{m+D!,d}

under the map
V_{m+D!,d}\to V_{m,d}
forgetting the last
D\cdot d
marked points.

Definition 4.5
.

Let
C_{\bullet}
be a system of cones for
V
as in Definition
4.4
.
We say that a divisor
D
is
generically transverse to
C_{\bullet}
if for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
and each
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
there exists a dense open subspace
U\subset C_{m,d}
so that
D
is transverse to
\pi\circ u
for each curve
u:\Sigma\to V
corresponding to a
\mathbb{C}
-point in
U
.

4.2.
D
-Volumes of Systems of Cones

In order to estimate Gromov-Witten counts, we need to define the “volume” of a collection of cones with respect to an ample divisor. Such estimates will be stated and proven in Section
5
later on.
Let
X
be a smooth projective variety and let
\pi:V\to X
be a vector bundle.

Lemma 4.6
.

The dual of
V
is globally generated if and only if

L:=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}(V)}(1)
is globally generated.

Proof.

Let
\pi_{\mathbb{P}}:\mathbb{P}(V)\to X
be the natural projection map. Then
(\pi_{\mathbb{P}})_{*}L=V^{*}
.
Hence,
H^{0}(V^{*})\to H^{0}(V^{*}|_{x})
is surjective if and only if
H^{0}(L)\to H^{0}(L|_{\mathbb{P}(V)|_{x}})
is surjective for each
x\in X
.
Since
H^{0}(L|_{\mathbb{P}(V)|_{x}})
is globally generated and any proper linear subsystem of
H^{0}(L|_{\mathbb{P}(V)|_{x}})
is not globally generated, we then get that
V^{*}
is globally generated if and only if
L
is globally generated.
∎

Definition 4.7
.

Define
P_{V}:=\mathbb{P}(V\oplus\mathbb{C})
,
L_{V}:=\mathcal{O}_{P_{V}}(1)
and
\infty_{V}:=\mathbb{P}(V)\subset P_{V}
.
We define
\pi_{P_{V}}:P_{V}\to X
to be the natural projection map.

Let
(V_{i})_{i\in I}
be a finite collection of vector bundles over
X
.
Define the fiber product

(4.13)

P_{(V_{i})_{i\in I}}:=\sideset{}{{}_{X}}{\prod}_{i\in I}P_{V_{i}}.

Define

(4.14)

\pi_{P_{(V_{i})_{i\in I}}}:P_{(V_{i})_{i\in I}}\to X

to be the corresponding projection map.
We define

(4.15)

\infty_{(V_{i})_{i\in I}}:=\sum_{i\in I}P_{i}^{*}\infty_{V_{i}}

where
P_{i}:P_{(V_{i})_{i\in I}}\to P_{V_{i}}
is the natural projection map for each
i\in I
.

If
I=\sqcup_{j=0}^{k}I_{j}
then we sometimes write:

(4.16)

P_{(V_{j})_{j\in I_{0}},\cdots,(V_{j})_{j\in I_{k}}}=P_{(V_{i})_{i\in I}},

(4.17)

\pi_{P_{(V_{j})_{j\in I_{0}},\cdots,(V_{j})_{j\in I_{k}}}}=\pi_{P_{(V_{i})_{i\in I}}},

and

(4.18)

\infty_{(V_{j})_{j\in I_{0}},\cdots,(V_{j})_{j\in I_{k}}}=\infty_{(V_{i})_{i\in I}}.

Corollary 4.8
.

Suppose
L_{0}
is a globally generated line bundle over
X
.
Then
V^{*}\otimes L_{0}
is globally generated if and only if
L_{V}\otimes\pi_{P_{V}}^{*}L_{0}
is globally generated.

Proof.

Let
W:=V\otimes L_{0}^{*}\oplus L_{0}^{*}
.
Then
\mathbb{P}(W)=P_{V}

and
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}(W)}(1)=L_{V}\otimes\pi_{P_{V}}^{*}L_{0}
.
Our result follows from Lemma
4.6
.
∎

Definition 4.9
.

Let
D
be a divisor on
X
.
Let
p_{i}:X^{m}\to X
be the
i
th projection map for each
i=1,\cdots,m
.
We define

(4.19)

D_{\sum m}:=\sum_{i=1}^{m}p_{i}^{*}D.

For each finite collection
(V_{i})_{i\in I}
of vector
bundles over
X
, we define

(4.20)

D_{\sum m,(V_{i})_{i\in I}}:=\pi_{P_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m})_{i\in I}}}^{*}D_{\sum m}+\infty_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m})_{i\in I}}.

If
I=\sqcup_{j=0}^{k}I_{j}
then we sometimes write

(4.21)

D_{\sum m,(V_{j})_{j\in I_{0}},\cdots,(V_{j})_{j\in I_{k}}}:=D_{\sum m,(V_{i})_{i\in I}}.

Corollary 4.10
.

Let
D
be a divisor in
X
so that
\mathcal{O}(D)
and
V^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(D)
are globally generated.
Then for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
we have that
D_{\sum m,V}

is globally generated and hence nef.

Proof.

The line bundle on
P_{V^{\boxtimes m}}
corresponding to
D_{\sum m,V}
is equal to
L_{V^{\boxtimes m}}\otimes\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m}}}^{*}\mathcal{O}(D_{\sum m})
.
This is globally generated by Corollary
4.8
since

(4.22)

\mathcal{O}(D_{\sum m})=\sum_{i=1}^{m}p_{i}^{*}\mathcal{O}(D),\quad(V^{\boxtimes m})^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(D_{\sum m})=\sum_{i=1}^{m}p_{i}^{*}(V^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(D))

are globally generated where
p_{i}:X^{m}\to X
is the
i
th projection map for each
i=1,\cdots,m
.
∎

Corollary 4.11
.

Now suppose that
(V_{i})_{i\in I}
is a finite collection of vector bundles over
X
and suppose that
D_{i}
is a divisor in
X
so that
\mathcal{O}(D_{i})
and
V_{i}^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(D_{i})
are globally generated for each
i\in I
. Let
D
be a divisor in
X
so that
D-\sum_{i\in I}D_{i}
is nef. Then
D_{\sum m,(V_{i})_{i\in I}}
is nef.

Proof.

Let
P_{i}:P_{(V_{i})_{i\in I}}\to P_{V_{i}}
be the natural projection map for each
i\in I
.
Then

(4.23)

D_{\sum m,(V_{i})_{i\in I}}=\sum_{i\in I}P_{i}^{*}((D_{i})_{\sum m,V_{i}})+\pi_{P_{(V_{i})_{i\in I}}}^{*}(D-\sum_{i\in I}D_{i})_{\sum m}

is a sum of nef divisors by Corollary
4.10
.
∎

Lemma 4.12
.

Let
D
,
D^{\prime}
be divisors in
X
so that
V^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(D^{\prime})
and
\mathcal{O}(D^{\prime})
are globally generated and
D-D^{\prime}
is ample.
Then for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
we have
D_{\sum m,V}

is ample.

Proof.

We will use Kleiman’s criterion to prove this.
We have
D^{\prime}_{\sum m,V}

is nef by Corollary
4.10
.
Also,

(4.24)

D_{\sum m,V}=D^{\prime}_{\sum m,V}+\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m}}}^{*}((D-D^{\prime})_{\sum m}).

Now let
C
be in the closure of the cone of curves in
N_{1}(P_{V^{\boxtimes m}})
.
Then if
C
is contained in a fiber of
\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m}}}
then it pairs positively with
D_{\sum m,V}
since
D_{\sum m,V}
restricted to this fiber is a linear hypersurface which is ample.
If
C
is not contained in a fiber then

(4.25)

C\cdot(\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m}}}^{*}(D-D^{\prime})_{\sum m})=\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m}}}(C)\cdot(D-D^{\prime})_{\sum m}>0

since
(D-D^{\prime})_{\sum m}
is ample.
So

(4.26)

C\cdot D_{\sum m,V}\overset{\textnormal{\eqref{eqnsplitdivisor}}}{=}C\cdot D^{\prime}_{\sum m,V}+C\cdot\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m}}}^{*}((D-D^{\prime})_{\sum m})\geqslant

(4.27)

C\cdot\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m}}}^{*}((D-D^{\prime})_{\sum m})\overset{\textnormal{\eqref{eqndivisordifferencepositive}}}{>}0.

∎

Definition 4.13
.

Let
(V_{i})_{i\in I}
be a finite collection of vector bundles over
X
.
Let

(4.28)

C^{i}_{\bullet}=(C^{i}_{m,d})_{m\in\mathbb{N},\ d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)}

be a system of cones for
V_{i}
as in Definition
4.4
for each
i\in I
.
Suppose
D
is a reduced divisor.
Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
and let
m^{\prime}:=m+D\cdot d
.
Define

(4.29)

\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C^{i}_{m+D!,d})_{i\in I}:=\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}\left(\sideset{}{{}_{X_{m+D!,d}}}{\prod}_{i\in I}C^{i}_{m+D!,d}\right)\subset\operatorname{ev}^{*}\oplus_{i\in I}V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}.

We define

(4.30)

\operatorname{ev}^{*}P_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}}

to be the pullback of
P_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}}\to X^{m^{\prime}}

via the map
\operatorname{ev}:X_{m^{\prime},d}\to X^{m^{\prime}}
.
Define

(4.31)

P\operatorname{ev}:\operatorname{ev}^{*}P_{(V_{i})_{i\in I}}\to P_{(V_{i})_{i\in I}}

to be the natural projection map.
We define

(4.32)

\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}((C^{i}_{m+D!,d})_{i\in I})}

to be the closure of
\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C^{i}_{m+D!,d})_{i\in I}
inside
\operatorname{ev}^{*}P_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}}

(Definition
4.3
).
We let

(4.33)

P\operatorname{ev}_{*}\left[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}((C^{i}_{m+D!,d})_{i\in I})}\right]

be the image of its Chow class under the map (
4.31
).

Define the
D
-volume
of
(C^{i}_{m,d})_{i\in I}
to be

(4.34)

D\textnormal{-vol}(C^{i}_{m,d})_{i\in I}:=\frac{1}{m!(D\cdot d)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}\left[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}((C^{i}_{m+D!,d})_{i\in I})}\right]}\exp(D_{\sum m^{\prime},(V_{i})_{i\in I}}).

If
I=\sqcup_{j=0}^{k}I_{j}
then we sometimes write

(4.35)

\operatorname{ev}^{*}P_{(V_{j}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{j\in I_{0}},\cdots,(V_{j}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{j\in I_{k}}}=\operatorname{ev}^{*}P_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}},

(4.36)

\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}((C^{i}_{m+D!,d})_{i\in I_{0}},\cdots,(C^{i}_{m+D!,d})_{i\in I_{k}})=\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}((C^{i}_{m+D!,d})_{i\in I}),

(4.37)

\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}((C^{i}_{m+D!,d})_{i\in I_{0}},\cdots,(C^{i}_{m+D!,d})_{i\in I_{k}})}=\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}((C^{i}_{m+D!,d})_{i\in I})}

and

(4.38)

D\textnormal{-vol}((C^{i}_{m+D!,d})_{i\in I_{0}},\cdots,(C^{i}_{m+D!,d})_{i\in I_{k}})=D\textnormal{-vol}(C^{i}_{m,d})_{i\in I}.

4.3.
V-Large Divisors

In order to compare
D
-volumes with Gromov-Witten counts, we need the divisor
D
to satisfy special properties. These properties ensure that each irreducible component of each curve intersects the divisor in many points so that we can evaluate along enough of these points (Lemma
4.19
is the crucial property that is needed here).
We fix a smooth projective variety
X
and a vector bundle
\pi:V\to X
over it.

Definition 4.14
.

A divisor
D\subset X
is
simple
V
-large
if it is reduced and
for each non-constant
u:\mathbb{P}^{1}\to X
,

(1)

the image of
u
is not contained in
D

(2)

and the number of points in
u^{-1}(D)
is greater than or equal to
1+\int_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}u^{*}(c_{1}(V))
.

We say that
D\subset X
is
V
-large
if there exists vector bundles
(V_{i})_{i\in I}
over
X
so that

(1)

D
is simple
V_{i}
-large for each
i\in I
and

(2)

V\cong\bigoplus_{i\in I}V_{i}
.

Lemma 4.15
.

Let
\iota_{X}:X\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{N}
be a closed immersion and let
(V_{i})_{i\in I}
be vector bundles over
X
satisfying
V\cong\oplus_{i\in I}V_{i}
.
Let
\widehat{D}
be a very general smooth divisor in
\mathbb{P}^{N}

of degree
\delta+2N
so that

(\delta-1)\deg(C)\geqslant c_{1}(V_{i})(C)
for each
i\in I
and each rational curve
C
in
X
.
Then
D=\widehat{D}\cap X
is
V
-large.

Proof.

Let
u:\mathbb{P}^{1}\to X
be a non-constant morphism.
By

[
Voi96
]
, the image of
u
is not contained in
D
.
Now write
u
as a composition:

(4.39)

\mathbb{P}^{1}\overset{e}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{P}^{1}\overset{\phi}{\longrightarrow}X

where
\phi
is a somewhere injective morphism.
By

[
Che04
, Theorem 1.7]
or
[
PR07
, Corollary 4]
,
we get that the number of points in
\phi^{-1}(D)
is greater than
1+\delta\deg(\phi)
, which in turn is
\geqslant 1+\int_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}\phi^{*}(c_{1}(V_{i}))+\deg(\phi)
for each
i\in I
.
Since the sum of the multiplicities of the branch points of
e
is at most
\deg(u)
, our result follows.
∎

Definition 4.16
.

A vector bundle
W\to X
is
convex
if for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
and each curve
u
corresponding to a point
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{C})\to X_{m,d}
we have
H^{1}(u^{*}W)=0
.
We say that
W
is
strongly convex
if it is convex and if
u^{*}W
is globally generated for each such
u
.

If
W
is convex then
W_{m,d}
is a vector bundle over
X_{m,d}
by Grothendieck’s cohomology and base change theorem.
The following lemma will be useful later on in Section
9
.

Lemma 4.17
.

Let
r:V\twoheadrightarrow W
be a surjection of vector bundles.
If
V
is (strongly) convex then
W
is (strongly) convex.

Proof.

Let
u
be a curve corresponding to a point
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{C})\to X_{m,d}
.
Then we have an exact sequence:

(4.40)

H^{1}(u^{*}V)\to H^{1}(u^{*}W)\to 0.

Since
H^{1}(u^{*}V)=0
, we get

H^{1}(u^{*}W)=0
which implies that
W
is convex.
Also,
u^{*}W
is globally generated if
u^{*}V
is.
∎

Corollary 4.18
.

Let
(V_{i})_{i\in I}
be a finite collection of vector bundles over
X
.
If
\bigoplus_{i\in I}V_{i}
is strongly convex, then
V_{i}
is strongly convex for each
i\in I
.

Lemma 4.19
.

Let
D
be a
V
-large divisor.
Suppose also that
V|_{U}
is strongly convex for some open subset
U\subset X
.
For each
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)-0
and for each curve
u
corresponding to a point
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{C})\to X_{m+D!,d}
with image in
U
, the map

(4.41)

\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}|_{u}:V_{m+D!,d}|_{u}\to\operatorname{ev}^{*}(V^{\boxplus m+D\cdot d})|_{u}

from (
4.10
) is injective.

Proof.

By Corollary
4.18
, it is sufficient for us to prove this when
D
is simple
V
-large. Therefore, let us assume that
D
is simple
V
-large.
Let
u:\Sigma\to U
be a curve in
X_{m+D!,d}
as above and let
v\in V_{m+D!,d}|_{u}=H^{0}(u^{*}V)
be an element of the corresponding fiber.
Suppose that the image
\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(v)
of
v
in
\operatorname{ev}^{*}(V^{\boxtimes m+D\cdot d})|_{u}
is zero.
Let
\Sigma^{\prime}\subset\Sigma
be an irreducible component so that
u|_{\Sigma^{\prime}}
is non-constant.
Let
P\subset\Sigma^{\prime}
be the subset of points
p
satisfying
u(p)\in D
.
For each
p\in P
, let
\Sigma_{p}\subset\Sigma
be the connected component of
u^{-1}(u(p))
containing
p
.
Then
\Sigma_{p}
contains a marked point for each
p\in P
since
u
doesn’t have image in
D
and
u
is a limit of curves
v
transverse to
D
with
v^{-1}(D)
a union of marked points.
Since
u|_{\Sigma_{p}}
is constant with image in
D
, we get that
v(p)=0
.
Hence,
v|_{\Sigma^{\prime}}
vanishes on each point
p\in\Sigma^{\prime}
.

Also, since
D
is simple
V
-large, we have

(4.42)

|P|\geqslant 1+\int_{\Sigma^{\prime}}u^{*}(c_{1}(V)).

Since
V
is strongly convex, we get that
u^{*}V
is a sum of line bundles of non-negative degree and hence
(
4.42
) implies
v|_{\Sigma^{\prime}}=0
for degree reasons.
Hence,
v
vanishes on each non-constant irreducible component of
\Sigma
.
Since
\Sigma
is connected and has at least one non-constant irreducible component due to the fact that
d\neq 0
, we get
v=0
.
∎

Definition 4.20
.

Let
U\subset X
be subvariety, let
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
.
We define
X_{m,d}|_{U}\subset X_{m,d}
to be the subspace parameterizing families of curves with image in
U
.
Now let
V\to X
be a vector bundle.
If
C\subset V_{m,d}
is a subcone, then we define
C|_{U}\subset V_{m,d}
to be the subspace parameterizing families of curves mapping to
V
that project to
X_{m,d}|_{U}
.
We think of this as a cone over
X_{m,d}
(rather than
X_{m,d}|_{U}
).
If
C_{\bullet}=(C_{m,d})_{m\in\mathbb{N},\ d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)}
is a system of cones for
V
(Definition
4.4
) then we define the following system of cones for
V
:

(4.43)

C_{\bullet}|_{U}:=(C_{m,d}|_{U})_{m\in\mathbb{N},\ d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)}.

Now let
D\subset X
be a reduced divisor. We define
X_{m+D!,d}|_{U}:=X_{m+D!,d}\cap X_{m+D\cdot d,d}|_{U}
.
If
C^{\prime}\subset V_{m+D!,d}
is a subcone then we define
C^{\prime}|_{U}:=C^{\prime}\cap(V_{m+D\cdot d,d}|_{U})
, thought of as a cone over
X_{m+D!,d}
.

Definition 4.21
.

Let
D
be a
V
-large divisor and suppose
V
is strongly convex.
We define

(4.44)

P\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}:P_{V_{m+D!,d}}\to\operatorname{ev}^{*}P_{(V)^{\boxtimes m+D\cdot d}}

to be the extension
of the map
\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}
to the projective compactification of these vector bundles (which exists by Lemma
4.19
).

5.
Some Bounds Involving
D
-Volumes

5.1.
Segre Classes Coming from Systems of Cones

In Section
7
we compute the virtual fundamental class of the moduli space of stable maps from Segre classes of cones. Therefore, we need to bound Segre class counts in terms of
D
-volumes both from above and below. We will do this in this section.
We let
X
be a smooth projective variety and
\pi:V\to X
a vector bundle.

Definition 5.1
.

Let
C_{\bullet}=(C_{m,d})_{m\in\mathbb{N},\ d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)}

be a system of cones for
V
as in Definition
4.4
.
For each
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
, we define
s(C_{m,d})
to be the Segre class of the closure of
C_{m,d}
inside
V_{m,d}
.
We define the system of classes
(Definition
3.1
)

(5.1)

s(C_{\bullet}):=(s(C_{m,d}))_{m\in\mathbb{N},\ d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)}.

We define
\overline{C_{m,d}}
to be the closure of
C_{m,d}
inside
P_{V_{m,d}}

and
\overline{C_{m+D!,d}}
the closure of
C_{m+D!,d}
inside
P_{V_{m+D!,d}}

for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
and any smooth divisor
D
in
X
.

Now, we wish to bound Gromov-Witten counts coming from Segre classes as in Definition
3.3

in terms of
D
-volume under certain conditions.

Lemma 5.2
.

Suppose that
X
admits a closed immersion into
\mathbb{P}^{N}
for some
N\in\mathbb{N}
.
Then

(5.2)

\dim(X_{m,d})\leqslant(N+1)\deg(d)+N-3+m.

Proof.

Let
\iota_{X}:X\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{N}
be our closed immersion.
Then
X_{m,d}\subset\mathbb{P}^{N}_{m,(\iota_{X})_{*}d}
and the dimension of
\mathbb{P}^{N}_{m,(\iota_{X})_{*}d}
is the right-hand side of (
5.2
) since
T\mathbb{P}^{N}
is convex and
c_{1}(T\mathbb{P}^{N}|_{X})(d)=(N+1)\deg(d)
.
Hence, (
5.2
) is our upper bound.
∎

To compare
D
-volumes with Gromov-Witten counts, we need the following computation of the pushforward of the Segre class of a cone. Here the
V
-large property is essential.

Lemma 5.3
.

Suppose that
V
is strongly convex and that
X
admits a closed immersion into
\mathbb{P}^{N}
for some
N\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
D
be a
V
-large divisor which is generically transverse to some system of cones
C_{\bullet}=(C_{m,d})_{m\in\mathbb{N},\ d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)}
.
Then

(5.3)

\operatorname{ev}_{*}(s(C_{m,d}))=\frac{1}{(D\cdot d)!}\sum_{r=0}^{f_{d}}F_{*}(\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m+D\cdot d}}})_{*}\left(P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+D!,d})}]\cap(\infty_{V^{\boxtimes m+D\cdot d}})^{r}\right)

(Definition
4.13
)
where

(5.4)

F:X^{m+D\cdot d}\to X^{m}

is the projection map to the first
m
factors and
f_{d}\in\mathbb{N}
satisfies

(5.5)

f_{d}\geqslant(N+1)\deg(d)+N-3+c_{1}(V)(d)+\dim(V)

for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)-0
.

Proof.

Let
\underline{\rho}:X_{m+D!,d}\to X_{0,d}
be the map forgetting all the marked points.
Since
V_{m+D!,d}\cong\underline{\rho}^{*}V_{0,d}
, we get an induced map

\rho:P_{V_{m+D!,d}}\to P_{V_{0,d}}
.
We have that

(5.6)

\infty_{V_{m+D!,d}}^{r}=\rho^{*}(\infty_{V_{0,d}})^{r}=0

if
r>f_{d}
since
\dim(P_{V_{0,d}})\leqslant f_{d}
by Lemma
5.2
and the fact that
V
is convex.
Similarly,
\infty_{V_{m,d}}^{r}=0
for
r>f_{d}
.
Hence,

(5.7)

\displaystyle s(C_{m,d})

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=0}^{f_{d}}(\pi_{P_{V_{m,d}}})_{*}([\overline{C_{m,d}}\cap\infty_{V_{m,d}}^{r}]),

\displaystyle s(C_{m+D!,d})

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=0}^{f_{d}}(\pi_{P_{V_{m+D!,d}}})_{*}([\overline{C_{m+D!,d}}\cap\infty_{V_{m+D!,d}}^{r}]).

Let
m^{\prime}:=m+D\cdot d
.
By Lemma
4.19
, the map
\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}
is fiberwise injective.
In particular,

(5.8)

P\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}^{*}P\operatorname{ev}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}}(1)=\mathcal{O}_{P_{V_{m+D!,d}}}(1)

and

(5.9)

P{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}}_{*}[\overline{C_{m+D!,d}}]=[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+D!,d})}]

(Definition
4.21
).
Hence,

(5.10)

\displaystyle\operatorname{ev}_{*}(s(C_{m+D!,d}))

\displaystyle\overset{\textnormal{\eqref{eqnsegreformula}}}{=}\sum_{r=0}^{f_{d}}\operatorname{ev}_{*}((\pi_{P_{V_{m+D!,d}}})_{*}([\overline{C_{m+D!,d}}]\cap(\infty_{V_{m+D!,d}})^{r}))

\displaystyle\overset{\textnormal{\eqref{eqnpulbackofo1r}}}{=}\sum_{r=0}^{f_{d}}\operatorname{ev}_{*}((\pi_{P_{V_{m+D!,d}}})_{*}([\overline{C_{m+D!,d}}]\cap P\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}^{*}P\operatorname{ev}^{*}(\infty_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}^{r})))

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=0}^{f_{d}}(\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}})_{*}P\operatorname{ev}_{*}P{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}}_{*}([\overline{C_{m+D!,d}}]\cap P\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}^{*}P\operatorname{ev}^{*}(\infty_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}^{r}))

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=0}^{f_{d}}(\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}})_{*}(P\operatorname{ev}\circ P\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}})_{*}([\overline{C_{m+D!,d}}])\cap(P\operatorname{ev}\circ P\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}})^{*}(\infty_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}^{r})

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=0}^{f_{d}}(\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}})_{*}(P\operatorname{ev}\circ P\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}})_{*}([\overline{C_{m+D!,d}}])\cap\infty_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}^{r}

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=0}^{f_{d}}(\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}})_{*}P\operatorname{ev}_{*}P\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}_{*}([\overline{C_{m+D!,d}}])\cap\infty_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}^{r}

\displaystyle\overset{\textnormal{\eqref{eqnpushforwardclosurerelation}}}{=}\sum_{r=0}^{f_{d}}(\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}})_{*}\left(P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+D!,d})}]\cap\infty_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}^{r}\right).

Let
\pi:X_{m+D!,d}\to X_{m,d}
be the map forgetting the last
D\cdot d
marked points.
Let
P\pi:P_{V_{m+D!,d}}\to P_{V_{m,d}}

be the map induced by this forgetful map.
This map satisfies:

(5.11)

\infty_{V_{m+D!,d}}=P\pi^{*}\infty_{V_{m,d}}.

Since
D
is generically transverse to
C_{\bullet}
, we have that

P\pi|_{C_{0,m+D!,d}}:C_{0,m+D!,d}\to C_{0,m,d}
is a generically étale morphism of order
(D\cdot d)!
.
Hence,

(5.12)

P\pi_{*}([\overline{C_{m+D!,d}}])=(D\cdot d)![\overline{C_{m,d}}].

We have

(5.13)

\displaystyle\pi_{*}(s(C_{m+D!,d}))

\displaystyle\overset{\textnormal{\eqref{eqnsegreformula}}}{=}\sum_{r=0}^{f_{d}}\pi_{*}((\pi_{P_{V_{m+D!,d}}})_{*}([\overline{C_{m+D!,d}}]\cap(\infty_{V_{m+D!,d}})^{r}))

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=0}^{f_{d}}(\pi_{P_{V_{m,d}}})_{*}P\pi_{*}(([\overline{C_{m+D!,d}}]\cap(\infty_{V_{m+D!,d}})^{r}))

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eqnpullbackinfinitybypi}}{=}\sum_{r=0}^{f_{d}}(\pi_{P_{V_{m,d}}})_{*}P\pi_{*}(([\overline{C_{m+D!,d}}]\cap P\pi^{*}(\infty_{V_{m,d}})^{r}))

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=0}^{f_{d}}(\pi_{P_{V_{m,d}}})_{*}(P\pi_{*}([\overline{C_{m+D!,d}}])\cap(\infty_{V_{m,d}})^{r})

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eqnpushfowardidentity}}{=}\sum_{r=0}^{f_{d}}(\pi_{P_{V_{m,d}}})_{*}((D\cdot d)![\overline{C_{m,d}}]\cap(\infty_{V_{m,d}})^{r})

\displaystyle\overset{\textnormal{\eqref{eqnsegreformula}}}{=}(D\cdot d)!s(C_{m,d}).

Combining this with Equation (
5.10
) together with the fact that
\operatorname{ev}\circ\pi=F\circ\operatorname{ev}
gives Equation (
5.3
).
∎

Definition 5.4
.

Let
V
be a finite dimensional complex vector space with a Hermitian metric.
Let
\omega_{V}\in\wedge^{2}V^{*}
be the corresponding imaginary part of the Hermitian form.
Then we have an induced metric on
\wedge^{k}V^{*}
where an orthonormal basis
\eta_{1},\cdots,\eta_{h}
of
V^{*}

gives an orthonormal basis
\eta_{i_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\eta_{i_{k}}
,
1\leqslant i_{1}<\cdots<i_{k}\leqslant h

for
\wedge^{k}V^{*}
.
We write
|\cdot|_{\omega_{V}}
for the corresponding norm on
\wedge^{k}V^{*}
.

Let
\omega
be a Kähler form on a smooth variety
Y
.
If
\alpha
is a
k
-form on
Y
then the
C^{0}
-norm of
\alpha
with respect to
\omega
, denoted by
|\alpha|_{C^{0},\omega}
, is the supremum of the
|\cdot|_{\omega|_{x}}
norm of
\alpha|_{x}\in\wedge^{k}T^{*}_{x}M
over all
x\in M
.

Remark 5.5
.

If
M
is a smooth complex manifold,
\eta\in\Omega^{k}(M)
and
\omega
is a Kähler form on
M
then

(5.14)

\left|\int_{M}\eta\right|\leqslant|\eta|_{C^{0},\omega}\int_{M}\exp(\omega).

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
\pi:Y\to X
be a morphism of smooth varieties and let
\omega
be a Kähler form on
Y
.
Let
n=\dim(X)
.
Also, let
\alpha
be a
C^{\infty}
differential form on
Y
and
\beta
a
C^{\infty}
differential form on
X
.
Then

(5.15)

|\alpha\wedge\pi^{*}\beta|_{C^{0},\omega}\leqslant 2^{2n}|\alpha|_{C^{0},\omega}|\pi^{*}\beta|_{C^{0},\omega}.

If
\beta
is homogenous of degree
j
, then

(5.16)

|\alpha\wedge\pi^{*}\beta|_{C^{0},\omega}\leqslant{2n\choose j}|\alpha|_{C^{0},\omega}|\pi^{*}\beta|_{C^{0},\omega}.

Proof.

Let us first assume that
\beta
is homogenous of degree
j
.
Suppose
y\in Y
and let
V\subset T_{y}Y
be the kernel of
d\pi|_{T_{y}Y}:T_{y}Y\to T_{\pi(y)}X
.
Let
V^{\perp}\subset T_{y}Y
be the
\omega
-orthogonal subspace of
V

and suppose
\Omega:=\omega|_{T_{y}Y}
.
Choose an orthonormal basis
v_{1},\cdots,v_{h}
of
T_{y}Y
so that
v_{1},\cdots,v_{k}
is an orthonormal basis for
V^{\perp}
and
v_{k+1},\cdots,v_{n}
is an orthonormal basis for
V
. Since
\dim X=n
, we have
k\leqslant 2n
.
Let
v^{*}_{1},\cdots,v_{n}^{*}
be the corresponding dual basis.
For each
I\subset\{1,\cdots,h\}
, write
v^{*}_{I}:=v^{*}_{i_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge v^{*}_{i_{k}}
where
I=\{i_{1},\cdots,i_{k}\}
and
i_{1}<\cdots<i_{k}
.
Then
\pi^{*}\beta|_{T_{y}Y}=\sum_{\underset{I\subset\{1,\cdots,k\}}{|I|=j}}b_{I}v^{*}_{I}
for some
b_{I}\in\mathbb{R}
,
I\subset\{1,\cdots,k\}
,
|I|=j
.
So,

(5.17)

\alpha\wedge\pi^{*}\beta|_{T_{y}Y}=\sum_{\underset{I\subset\{1,\cdots,k\}}{|I|=j}}b_{I}\alpha|_{T_{y}Y}\wedge v^{*}_{I}.

Hence, by the triangle inequality, we have

(5.18)

\big|\alpha\wedge\pi^{*}\beta|_{T_{y}Y}\big|_{C^{0},\Omega}\leqslant\sum_{\underset{I\subset\{1,\cdots,k\}}{|I|=j}}|b_{I}|\big|\alpha|_{T_{y}Y}\big|_{C^{0},\Omega}

(5.19)

\leqslant\sum_{\underset{I\subset\{1,\cdots,k\}}{|I|=j}}|\pi^{*}\beta|_{T_{y}Y}|_{C^{0},\Omega}\big|\alpha|_{T_{y}Y}\big|_{C^{0},\Omega}={2k\choose j}|\pi^{*}\beta|_{T_{y}Y}|_{C^{0},\Omega}\big|\alpha|_{T_{y}Y}\big|_{C^{0},\Omega}

(5.20)

\leqslant{2n\choose j}|\pi^{*}\beta|_{T_{y}Y}|_{C^{0},\Omega}\big|\alpha|_{T_{y}Y}\big|_{C^{0},\Omega}.

Hence,

(5.21)

\big|\alpha\wedge\pi^{*}\beta\big|_{C^{0},\omega}=\sup_{y\in Y}\big|\alpha\wedge\pi^{*}\beta|_{T_{y}Y}\big|_{C^{0},\omega|_{T_{y}Y}}

(5.22)

\leqslant\sup_{y\in Y}{2n\choose j}|\pi^{*}\beta|_{C^{0},\omega|_{T_{y}Y}}\big|\alpha|_{T_{y}Y}\big|_{C^{0},\omega|_{T_{y}Y}}

(5.23)

\leqslant\sup_{y\in Y}{2n\choose j}|\pi^{*}\beta|_{T_{y}Y}|_{C^{0},\omega|_{T_{y}Y}}\sup_{y^{\prime}\in Y}\big|\alpha|_{T_{y^{\prime}}Y}\big|_{C^{0},\omega|_{T_{y^{\prime}}Y}}={2n\choose j}|\alpha|_{C^{0},\omega}|\pi^{*}\beta|_{C^{0},\omega}.

Now suppose that
\beta
is not necessarily homogenous. Then we can write
\beta=\sum_{j=0}^{2n}\beta_{j}
where
\beta_{j}
is homogenous of degree
j
for each
j=0,\cdots,2n
.
Then

(5.24)

|\alpha\wedge\pi^{*}\beta|_{C^{0},\omega}\leqslant\sum_{j=0}^{2n}|\alpha\wedge\pi^{*}\beta_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}\leqslant\sum_{j=0}^{2n}{2n\choose j}|\alpha|_{C^{0},\omega}|\pi^{*}\beta_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(5.25)

\leqslant\sum_{j=0}^{2n}{2n\choose j}|\alpha|_{C^{0},\omega}|\pi^{*}\beta|_{C^{0},\omega}=2^{2n}|\alpha|_{C^{0},\omega}|\pi^{*}\beta|_{C^{0},\omega}.

∎

Lemma 5.7
.

Let
\Omega_{1},\Omega_{2}\in\wedge^{2}V^{*}
be two imaginary parts of Hermitian forms on a finite dimensional vector space
V
where the first one is positive definite and the second is positive semidefinite.
Let
\Gamma\in\wedge^{k}V^{*}
.
Then

(5.26)

|\Gamma|_{\Omega_{1}+\Omega_{2}}\leqslant|\Gamma|_{\Omega_{1}},

(5.27)

|\wedge^{j}\Omega_{1}|_{\Omega_{1}+\Omega_{2}}\leqslant j!{\dim(V)\choose j},

(5.28)

|\wedge^{j}\Omega_{2}|_{\Omega_{1}+\Omega_{2}}\leqslant j!{\dim(V)\choose j},\quad\forall\ j=0,\cdots,\dim(V).

Proof.

We can choose a basis so that the metrics associated to
\Omega_{1}
and
\Omega_{1}+\Omega_{2}
are both diagonal with the first one being the identity matrix.
The diagonal entries of the second matrix are
\geqslant 1
since
\Omega_{2}
has positive semidefinite associated Hermitian form. So, if we write
\Gamma
as a linear combination of wedge products of elements of the corresponding dual basis, then its
|\cdot|_{\Omega_{1}}
-norm squared is the sum of the squares of the corresponding coefficients because the norms of these wedge products are
1
, and they are orthogonal to each other. However, the
|\cdot|_{\Omega_{1}+\Omega_{2}}
-norm of each such wedge product of basis elements is
\leqslant 1
because the diagonal entries of the second matrix are
\geqslant 1

and so Equation (
5.26
) holds.

Since
\Omega_{2}
has positive semidefinite associated Hermitian form, we can approximate it by a sequence
\Omega_{2,i}
,
i\in\mathbb{N}
whose associated Hermitian forms are positive definite.
Let
\Omega_{1,i}=\Omega_{1}+\Omega_{2}-\Omega_{2,i}
for each
i\in\mathbb{N}
. Then
\Omega_{1,i}
has positive definite associated Hermitian form for each sufficiently large
i\in\mathbb{N}
. Therefore, after passing to a subsequence, we can assume
\Omega_{1,i}
has positive definite associated Hermitian form for each
i\in\mathbb{N}
too.
Since
\Omega_{k,i}
has a positive definite associated Hermitian form, it is equal to
\frac{i}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{\dim V}z_{j}^{*}\wedge\overline{z}_{j}^{*}
with respect to some orthonormal basis
z_{1}^{*},\cdots,z_{\dim(V)}^{*}
of
V^{*}
with respect to the Hermitian metric coming from
\Omega_{k,i}
for each
k=1,2
,
i\in\mathbb{N}
.
Hence, by the first part of this lemma with
\Omega_{1}
and
\Omega_{2}
replaced with
\Omega_{1,i}
and
\Omega_{2,i}
respectively, each of the forms
z_{j}^{*}\wedge\overline{z}_{j}^{*}
has
|\cdot|_{\Omega_{1}+\Omega_{2}}=|\cdot|_{\Omega_{1,i}+\Omega_{2,i}}
norm at most
1
.
Also, the wedge product of
z_{j}^{*}\wedge\overline{z}_{j}^{*}
with itself is zero.
Hence:

(5.29)

|\wedge^{j}\Omega_{k,i}|_{\Omega_{1}+\Omega_{2}}\leqslant j!{\dim(V)\choose j},\quad\forall\ j=0,\cdots,\dim(V),\ k=1,2

for each
i\in\mathbb{N}
.
Taking the limit as
i
goes to infinity gives Equations (
5.27
) and (
5.28
).
∎

Lemma 5.8
.

Let
\pi:Y\to Z
be a morphism between smooth projective varieties.
Let
\omega_{Y}
be a Kähler form on
Y
and
\omega_{Z}
a Kähler form on
Z
.
Define
\omega=\omega_{Y}+\pi^{*}\omega_{Z}
.
Let
T
be a Chow cycle on
Y
of dimension at most
h\in\mathbb{N}
and which is equal to a positive sum of closed integral subschemes and suppose the scheme theoretic image of each such subscheme in
Z
has dimension at most
b\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
p_{i}:Y\to Z_{i}
,
i=1,\cdots,m
be morphisms to smooth projective varieties of dimension at most
n
.
Also, let
\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{m}
be differential forms on
Z_{1},\cdots,Z_{m}
respectively and let
k\in\mathbb{N}
.

Then

(5.30)

\left|\int_{T}\left(\cup_{j=1}^{m}p_{j}^{*}\alpha_{j}\right)\cup(\omega_{Y}-\pi^{*}\omega_{Z})^{k}\right|\leqslant(4b+2mn+4)!h^{2b}h!\prod_{j=1}^{m}|p_{j}^{*}\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}\int_{T}\exp(\omega).

Proof.

By linearity combined with the triangle inequality and the fact that
T
is a positive sum of integral subschemes it is sufficient for us to prove this when
T\subset X
is an integral subscheme.
So, let us assume that this is the case.

There exists a dense open subspace
T^{0}\subset T
with the property that both
T^{0}
and
\pi(T_{0})
are smooth.
Define
\pi_{0}:=\pi|_{T^{0}}:T^{0}\to\pi(T^{0})
.
Let
\iota:T^{0}\hookrightarrow Y
and
\underline{\iota}:\pi(T^{0})\to Z
be the natural inclusion maps.

Since
\underline{\iota}^{*}\omega_{Z}^{j}=0
for
j>b
, we have

(5.31)

\left|\int_{T}\left(\cup_{j=1}^{m}p_{j}^{*}\alpha_{j}\right)\cup(\omega_{Y}-\pi^{*}\omega_{Z})^{k}\right|=\left|\int_{T^{0}}\left(\cup_{j=1}^{m}p_{j}^{*}\alpha_{j}\right)\cup(\omega_{Y}-\pi^{*}\omega_{Z})^{k}\right|

(5.32)

\overset{\textnormal{Remark \ref{remarkcomparisonofnorms}}}{\leqslant}\left|\left(\cup_{j=1}^{m}\iota^{*}p_{j}^{*}\alpha_{j}\right)\cup(\iota^{*}\omega_{Y}-\iota^{*}\pi^{*}\omega_{Z})^{k}\right|_{C^{0},\iota^{*}\omega}\int_{T}\exp(\iota^{*}\omega)

(5.33)

=\left|\left(\cup_{j=1}^{m}\iota^{*}p_{j}^{*}\alpha_{j}\right)\cup(\iota^{*}\omega_{Y}-\pi_{0}^{*}\underline{\iota}^{*}\omega_{Z})^{k}\right|_{C^{0},\iota^{*}\omega}\int_{T}\exp(\iota^{*}\omega)

(5.34)

=\left|\sum_{j=0}^{b}(-1)^{j}{k\choose j}\left(\cup_{j=1}^{m}\iota^{*}p_{j}^{*}\alpha_{j}\right)\cup\iota^{*}\omega_{Y}^{k-j}\cup\pi_{0}^{*}\underline{\iota}^{*}\omega_{Z}^{j}\right|_{C^{0},\iota^{*}\omega}\int_{T}\exp(\iota^{*}\omega)

(5.35)

\leqslant\sum_{j=0}^{b}{k\choose j}\left|\left(\cup_{j=1}^{m}\iota^{*}p_{j}^{*}\alpha_{j}\right)\cup(\iota^{*}\omega_{Y})^{k-j}\cup(\pi_{0}^{*}\underline{\iota}^{*}\omega_{Z})^{j}\right|_{C^{0},\iota^{*}\omega}\int_{T}\exp(\omega)

(5.36)

\overset{\textnormal{Lemma \ref{lemmaproductnorms}}}{\leqslant}\sum_{j=0}^{b}{k\choose j}2^{2mn}{2b\choose 2}^{j}\left|(\iota^{*}\omega_{Y})^{k-j}\right|_{C^{0},\iota^{*}\omega}\left|(\pi_{0}^{*}\underline{\iota}^{*}\omega_{Z})\right|^{j}_{C^{0},\iota^{*}\omega}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\left|\iota^{*}p_{j}^{*}\alpha_{j}\right|_{C^{0},\iota^{*}\omega}\int_{T}\exp(\omega)

(5.37)

\overset{\textnormal{Lemma \ref{lemmaaboutpositivedefiniteforms}}}{\leqslant}\sum_{j=0}^{b}{k\choose j}2^{2mn}{2b\choose 2}^{j}(k-j)!{h\choose k-j}h^{j}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\left|\iota^{*}p_{j}^{*}\alpha_{j}\right|_{C^{0},\iota^{*}\omega}\int_{T}\exp(\omega)

(5.38)

\leqslant\sum_{j=0}^{b}k^{j}2^{2mn}{2b\choose 2}^{j}h!h^{b}\prod_{j=1}^{m}\left|\iota^{*}p_{j}^{*}\alpha_{j}\right|_{C^{0},\iota^{*}\omega}\int_{T}\exp(\omega)

(5.39)

\leqslant(b+1)(kh)^{b}2^{2mn}((2b)^{2})^{b} h!\prod_{j=1}^{m}\left|\iota^{*}p_{j}^{*}\alpha_{j}\right|_{C^{0},\iota^{*}\omega}\int_{T}\exp(\omega)

(5.40)

\leqslant(b+1)h^{2b}2^{2mn}(2b)^{2b}h!\prod_{j=1}^{m}\left|\iota^{*}p_{j}^{*}\alpha_{j}\right|_{C^{0},\iota^{*}\omega}\int_{T}\exp(\omega)

(5.41)

\leqslant h^{2b}2^{2mn}(2b+1)^{2b+1}h!\prod_{j=1}^{m}\left|\iota^{*}p_{j}^{*}\alpha_{j}\right|_{C^{0},\iota^{*}\omega}\int_{T}\exp(\omega)

since
k\leqslant h
.
Combining this with the following inequality:

(5.42)

\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}g_{i}!\prod_{j=1}^{k^{\prime}}e_{j}^{f_{j}}\leqslant(\sum_{i}g_{i}+\sum_{j}e_{j}+f_{j})!,\quad\forall\ e_{1},\cdots,e_{k^{\prime}},f_{1},\cdots,f_{k^{\prime}},g_{1},\cdots,g_{\ell}\in\mathbb{N},

we get (
5.41
)

(5.43)

\leqslant(2+2mn+2b+1+2b+1)!h^{2b}h!\prod_{j=1}^{m}\left|\iota^{*}p_{j}^{*}\alpha_{j}\right|_{C^{0},\iota^{*}\omega}\int_{T}\exp(\omega)

(5.44)

=(4b+2mn+4)!h^{2b}h!\prod_{j=1}^{m}\left|\iota^{*}p_{j}^{*}\alpha_{j}\right|_{C^{0},\iota^{*}\omega}\int_{T}\exp(\omega).

∎

Proposition 5.9
.

Suppose
X
is a subvariety of
\mathbb{P}^{N}
.
Suppose that
V
is a strongly convex vector bundle over
X
.
Let
D,D^{\prime}
be divisors on
X
so that:

(1)

V^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(D^{\prime})
and
\mathcal{O}(D^{\prime})
are globally generated,

(2)

D
is linearly equivalent to
4D^{\prime}
,

(3)

D^{\prime}
is ample,

(4)

D
is smooth,
V
-large and generically transverse to some system of cones

(5.45)

C_{\bullet}=(C_{m,d})_{m\in\mathbb{N},\ d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)}

for
V
.

Let
\omega
be a Kähler form Poincaré dual to
D^{\prime}
.
Also, let
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
and let

\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{m}
be closed de Rham forms on
X
.
Then

(5.46)

\left|\langle[\alpha_{1}],\cdots,[\alpha_{m}]\rangle_{m,d}^{s(C_{\bullet})}\right|\leqslant(9e_{d}+2mN+6m+9)!((\rho_{d}+1))^{4(e_{d}+m+1)}\rho_{d}!D\textnormal{-vol}(C_{m,d})\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

where

(5.47)

e_{d}:=(N+1)\deg(d)+N,

(5.48)

\rho_{d}:=c_{1}(V)(d)+\dim(V).

Proof.

Let
m^{\prime}=m+D\cdot d
.
Let
p_{j}:X^{m^{\prime}}\to X
be the
j
th projection map for each
j=1,\cdots,m^{\prime}
.
Also, let
D^{\prime\prime}:=2D^{\prime}
.
By Lemma
4.12
,
D^{\prime\prime}_{\sum m^{\prime},V}
is ample and so there is a Kähler form
\widetilde{\omega}
Poincaré dual to it.
Let
f_{d}:=e_{d}+\rho_{d}
.
Define
\omega_{\sum m^{\prime}}:=\sum_{j=1}^{m^{\prime}}p_{j}^{*}\omega
.

We have

(5.49)

\left|\langle[\alpha_{1}],\cdots,[\alpha_{m}]\rangle_{m,d}^{s(C_{\bullet})}\right|=\left|\int_{\operatorname{ev}_{*}(s(C_{m,d}))}\prod_{i=1}^{m}p_{i}^{*}\alpha_{i}\right|

(5.50)

\overset{\textnormal{Lemma \ref{lemmasegre}}}{=}\frac{1}{(D\cdot d)!}\left|\sum_{r=0}^{f_{d}}\int_{(\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}})_{*}(P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+D!,d})}]\cap\infty_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}^{r})}\prod_{i=1}^{m}p_{i}^{*}\alpha_{i}\right|

(5.51)

=\frac{1}{(D\cdot d)!}\left|\sum_{r=0}^{f_{d}}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+D!,d})}]\cap\infty_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}^{r}}\prod_{i=1}^{m}\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}}^{*}p_{i}^{*}\alpha_{i}\right|

(5.52)

=\frac{1}{(D\cdot d)!}\left|\sum_{r=0}^{f_{d}}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+D!,d})}]}\prod_{i=1}^{m}\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}}^{*}p_{i}^{*}\alpha_{i}\cup(D^{\prime\prime}_{\sum m^{\prime},V}-\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}}^{*}D^{\prime\prime}_{\sum m^{\prime}})^{r}\right|

(5.53)

=\frac{1}{(D\cdot d)!}\left|\sum_{r=0}^{f_{d}}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+D!,d})}]}\prod_{i=1}^{m}\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}}^{*}p_{i}^{*}\alpha_{i}\cup(\widetilde{\omega}-2\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}}^{*}\omega_{\sum m^{\prime}})^{r}\right|

(5.54)

\leqslant\frac{1}{(D\cdot d)!}\sum_{r=0}^{f_{d}}\left|\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+D!,d})}]}\prod_{i=1}^{m}\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}}^{*}p_{i}^{*}\alpha_{i}\cup(\widetilde{\omega}-2\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}}^{*}\omega_{\sum m^{\prime}})^{r}\right|.

Let
\omega^{\prime}:=2\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}}^{*}\omega_{\sum m^{\prime}}+\widetilde{\omega}

and

(5.55)

\omega^{i}_{\epsilon}=\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}}^{*}p_{i}^{*}\omega+\epsilon\omega^{\prime}

for each
i=1,\cdots,m^{\prime}
and
\epsilon>0
.
Then
\omega^{i}_{\epsilon}
and
\omega^{\prime}-\omega^{i}_{\epsilon}
are both ample for
\epsilon>0
small.
Hence, by Lemma
5.7
,

(5.56)

|\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}}^{*}p_{i}^{*}\alpha_{i}|_{C^{0},\omega^{\prime}}\leqslant\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}|\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}}^{*}p_{i}^{*}\alpha_{i}|_{C^{0},\omega^{i}_{\epsilon}}=|\alpha_{i}|_{C^{0},\omega}.

We have that the dimension
X_{m+D!,d}
is
\leqslant e_{d}+m
by Lemma
5.2

and the dimension of
\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+D!,d})}
is
\leqslant f_{d}+m
.
Therefore, by Lemma
5.8
we have (
5.54
) is less than or equal to:

(5.57)

\frac{1}{(D\cdot d)!}\sum_{r=0}^{f_{d}}(4e_{d}+2mN+4)!(f_{d}+m)^{2(e_{d}+m)}(f_{d}+m)!\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}}^{*}p_{j}^{*}\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega^{\prime}}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+D!,d})}]}\exp(\omega^{\prime})

(5.58)

\overset{\textnormal{\eqref{eqninequalityforpullback}}}{\leqslant}\frac{1}{(D\cdot d)!}\sum_{r=0}^{f_{d}}(4e_{d}+2mN+4)!(f_{d}+m)^{2(e_{d}+m)}(f_{d}+m)!\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+D!,d})}]}\exp(\omega^{\prime})

(5.59)

\overset{\textnormal{Definition \ref{defnvolumeformultiplecones}}}{=}\frac{1}{(D\cdot d)!}\sum_{r=0}^{f_{d}}(4e_{d}+2mN+4)!(f_{d}+m)^{2(e_{d}+m)}(f_{d}+m)!\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}m!(D\cdot d)!D\textnormal{-vol}(C_{m,d})

(5.60)

\leqslant(f_{d}+1)(4e_{d}+2mN+4)!(f_{d}+m)^{2(e_{d}+m)}(f_{d}+m)!m!D\textnormal{-vol}(C_{m,d})\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(5.61)

\leqslant(4e_{d}+2mN+4)!(f_{d}+m)^{2(e_{d}+m)}(e_{d}+\rho_{d}+m+1)!m!D\textnormal{-vol}(C_{m,d})\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(5.62)

\leqslant(4e_{d}+2mN+4)!(f_{d}+m)^{2(e_{d}+m)}{f_{d}+m+1\choose e_{d}+m+1}(e_{d}+m+1)!\rho_{d}!m!D\textnormal{-vol}(C_{m,d})\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(5.63)

\leqslant(4e_{d}+2mN+4)!(f_{d}+m)^{2(e_{d}+m)}(f_{d}+m+1)^{e_{d}+m+1}(e_{d}+m+1)!\rho_{d}!m!D\textnormal{-vol}(C_{m,d})\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(5.64)

\leqslant(4e_{d}+2mN+4)!(f_{d}+m)^{2(e_{d}+m)}(f_{d}+m+1)^{2(e_{d}+m+1)}\rho_{d}!m!D\textnormal{-vol}(C_{m,d})\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(5.65)

\leqslant(4e_{d}+2mN+4)!(f_{d}+m+1)^{4(e_{d}+m+1)}\rho_{d}!m!D\textnormal{-vol}(C_{m,d})\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(5.66)

\overset{\textnormal{\eqref{eqnreallreallyterribleinequality}}}{\leqslant}(4e_{d}+2mN+m+4)!(f_{d}+m+1)^{4(e_{d}+m+1)}\rho_{d}!D\textnormal{-vol}(C_{m,d})\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(5.67)

=(4e_{d}+2mN+m+4)!(\rho_{d}+e_{d}+m+1)^{4(e_{d}+m+1)}\rho_{d}!D\textnormal{-vol}(C_{m,d})\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(5.68)

\leqslant(4e_{d}+2mN+m+4)!((\rho_{d}+1)(e_{d}+m+1))^{4(e_{d}+m+1)}\rho_{d}!D\textnormal{-vol}(C_{m,d})\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(5.69)

\overset{\textnormal{\eqref{eqnreallreallyterribleinequality}}}{\leqslant}(4e_{d}+2mN+m+4+e_{d}+m+1+4(e_{d}+m+1))!(\rho_{d}+1)^{4(e_{d}+m+1)}\rho_{d}!D\textnormal{-vol}(C_{m,d})\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(5.70)

=(9e_{d}+2mN+6m+9)!((\rho_{d}+1))^{4(e_{d}+m+1)}\rho_{d}!D\textnormal{-vol}(C_{m,d})\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}.

∎

We now wish to bound
D
-volume in terms of Segre counts.
Before we do this, we need a preliminary definition and some lemmas.

Definition 5.10
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
.
Suppose
D,E
are reduced divisors in
X
. Let
m^{\prime}=m+D\cdot d
and
m^{\prime\prime}:=m^{\prime}+E\cdot d
.
We define
X_{m+D!+E!,d}^{\circ}\subset X_{m^{\prime\prime},d}
be the subspace parameterizing families of curves
u
where

(1)

the image of
u
under the forgetful map forgetting the last
m^{\prime\prime}-m^{\prime}
marked points lies in
X_{m+D!,d}^{\circ}

(2)

and the image of
u
under the forgetful map forgetting the marked points labelled by
m+1,\cdots,m^{\prime}
lies in
X_{m+E!,d}^{\circ}
.

Define

(5.71)

X_{m+D!+E!,d}\subset X_{m^{\prime\prime},d}

to be its closure.
Let
V\to X
be a vector bundle and let
C_{\bullet}=(C_{\check{m},\check{d}})_{\check{m}\in\mathbb{N},\ \check{d}\in H_{2}^{+}(X)}
be a system of cones for
V
.
Define
C^{o}_{m+D!+E!,d}\subset V_{m^{\prime\prime},d}
to be the
restriction of
C_{m^{\prime\prime},d}
to
X^{o}_{m+D!+E!,d}\subset X_{m^{\prime\prime},d}
.
Similarly, define
C_{m+D!+E!,d}\subset V_{m^{\prime\prime},d}
to be the restriction of
C_{m^{\prime\prime},d}\subset V_{m^{\prime\prime},d}
to
X_{m+D!+E!,d}
.

Let
(V_{i})_{i\in I}
be a finite collection of vector bundles over
X
.
Let
C^{i}_{\bullet}=(C^{i}_{\check{m},\check{d}})_{\check{m}\in\mathbb{N},\ \check{d}\in H_{2}^{+}(X)}
be a system of cones for
V_{i}
for each
i\in I
.
Define

(5.72)

\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+D!+E!,d}^{i})_{i\in I}:=\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}\left(\sideset{}{{}_{X_{m+D!+E!,d}}}{\prod}_{i\in I}C_{m+D!+E!,d}^{i}\right).

We let
\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+D!+E!,d}^{i})_{i\in I}}
be the closure of

(5.73)

\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+D!+E!,d}^{i})_{i\in I}

in
\operatorname{ev}^{*}P_{(V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}})_{i\in I}}
.
If
I=\{\star\}
then we sometimes just write:

(5.74)

\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+D!+E!,d}^{\star})}=\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+D!+E!,d}^{i})_{i\in I}}.

The same definitions above hold when
X
is replaced with a smooth quasi-projective variety.

Lemma 5.11
.

Let
(V_{i})_{i\in I}
be a finite collection of vector bundles over
X
.
Let
C^{i}_{\bullet}
be a system of cones for
V_{i}
for each
i\in I
.
Suppose
D,D^{\prime},E,E^{\prime}

are reduced divisors generically transverse to
C^{i}_{\bullet}
for each
i\in I

and so that
D\sim D^{\prime}
and
E\sim E^{\prime}
.
Let
d\in H^{2}_{+}(X)
,
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
m^{\prime\prime}:=m+D\cdot d+E\cdot d

and let
\alpha
be a closed differential form in
X^{m^{\prime\prime}}
.
Then

(5.75)

\displaystyle\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+D!+E!,d})}]}\alpha=\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+D^{\prime}!+E^{\prime}!,d})}]}\alpha.

Proof.

Let
p:\mathbb{C}\times X\to X
be the natural projection map.
Let
(D_{t})_{t\in\mathbb{C}}
be the one dimensional linear family of divisors satisfying
D_{0}=D
and
D_{1}=D^{\prime}
.
Similarly, let
(E_{t})_{t\in\mathbb{C}}
be the one dimensional linear family of divisors satisfying
E_{0}=E
and
E_{1}=E^{\prime}
.
Define

(5.76)

\widetilde{D}:=\{(t,x)\in\mathbb{C}\times X:x\in D_{t}\},\quad\widetilde{E}:=\{(t,x)\in\mathbb{C}\times X:x\in E_{t}\}.

Define
\widetilde{D}_{t}:=\widetilde{D}\cap(\{t\}\times X)
and
\widetilde{E}_{t}:=\widetilde{E}\cap(\{t\}\times X)
for each
t\in\mathbb{C}
.
Let
\widetilde{V}_{i}:=p^{*}V_{i}
and let

\widetilde{C}^{i}_{\check{m},(0,\check{d})}
be the pullback of
C^{i}_{\check{m},\check{d}}
to
(\widetilde{V}_{i})_{\check{m},(0,\check{d})}

for each
\check{m}\in\mathbb{N}
,
\check{d}\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
and
i\in I
.
Also, let
(C^{i}_{t})_{\check{m},(0,\check{d})}
be the restriction of
\widetilde{C}^{i}_{\check{m},(0,\check{d})}
to
(\{t\}\times X)_{\check{m},\check{d}}
for each
t\in\mathbb{C}
,
\check{m}\in\mathbb{N}
,
\check{d}\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
and
i\in I
.
Define

(5.77)

C:=\sideset{}{{}_{(\mathbb{C}\times X)_{m+\widetilde{D}!+\widetilde{E}!}}}{\prod}_{i\in I}\widetilde{C}^{i}_{m+\widetilde{D}!+\widetilde{E}!,(0,d)},\quad C^{o}:=\sideset{}{{}_{(\mathbb{C}\times X)^{o}_{m+\widetilde{D}!+\widetilde{E}!}}}{\prod}_{i\in I}{C^{i}}^{o}_{m+\widetilde{D}!+\widetilde{E}!,(0,d)}

and

(5.78)

C_{t}:=\sideset{}{{}_{(\{t\}\times X)_{m+\widetilde{D}_{t}!+\widetilde{E}_{t}!}}}{\prod}_{i\in I}(C^{i}_{t})_{m+\widetilde{D}_{t}!+\widetilde{E}_{t}!,(0,d)},\quad C^{o}_{t}:=\sideset{}{{}_{(\{t\}\times X)_{m+\widetilde{D}_{t}!+\widetilde{E}_{t}!}}}{\prod}_{i\in I}(C^{i}_{t})^{o}_{m+\widetilde{D}_{t}!+\widetilde{E}_{t}!,(0,d)}

for each
t\in\mathbb{C}
.
Let
\Delta_{t}
be the divisor equal to the preimage of the divisor
\{t\}\subset\mathbb{C}
under the natural map:

(5.79)

\operatorname{ev}^{*}P_{(\widetilde{V_{i}}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}})_{i\in I}}\to(\mathbb{C}\times X)_{m+\widetilde{D}!+\widetilde{E}!,(0,d)}\to\mathbb{C}.

We let
\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C)}
,
\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{t})}
be the closures of
\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C)
,
\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{t})
in
\operatorname{ev}^{*}P_{(\widetilde{V_{i}}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}})_{i\in I}}
for each
t\in\mathbb{C}
.
Let
\widetilde{\alpha}:=p^{*}\alpha
.

Since
D
,
D^{\prime}
,
E
,
E^{\prime}
are generically transverse to
C_{\bullet}
, we have that

\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C^{o}_{t})=\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C^{o})\cap\Delta_{t}
is dense in
\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C)}\cap\Delta_{t}
for
t=0,1
.
Hence,
\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C)}\cap\Delta_{t}=\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{t})}
for all
t=0,1
.
Hence:

(5.80)

\displaystyle\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+D!+E!,d})}]}\alpha=\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{0})}]}\widetilde{\alpha}=\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}([\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C)}]\cap\Delta_{0})}\widetilde{\alpha}=

\displaystyle\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}([\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C)}]\cap\Delta_{1})}\widetilde{\alpha}=\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}([\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{1})}])}\widetilde{\alpha}=\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+D!+E!,d})}]}\alpha.

∎

Lemma 5.12
.

Let
(V_{i})_{i\in I}
be a finite collection of vector bundles over
X
.
Suppose
C^{i}_{\bullet}
is a system of cones for
V_{i}
for each
i\in I
.
Let
D
and
E

be reduced divisors generically transverse to
C^{i}_{\bullet}
with
E\sim D
.
Let
d\in H^{2}_{+}(X)
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
m^{\prime}:=m+D\cdot d
and
m^{\prime\prime}:=m^{\prime}+E\cdot d
.
Define
F:X^{m^{\prime\prime}}\to X^{m^{\prime}}
to be the projection map to the first
m^{\prime}
factors.
Let
H
be a divisor on
X^{m^{\prime}}
.
Then

(5.81)

\displaystyle\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+D!,d})}]}\exp(\infty_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}}+\pi_{P_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}}}^{*}H)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(E\cdot d)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+D!+E!,d})}]}\exp(\infty_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}})_{i\in I}}+\pi_{P_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}})_{i\in I}}}^{*}F^{*}H).

Also, if
E=D
and
\infty_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}}+\pi_{P_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}}}^{*}H
is nef then the restriction of

(5.82)

\infty_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}})_{i\in I}}+\pi_{P_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}})_{i\in I}}}^{*}F^{*}H

to
\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+D!+E!,d})}
is also nef.

Proof.

Let

(5.83)

\pi:{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C^{i}_{m+D!+D!,d})_{i\in I}}\to{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C^{i}_{m+D!,d})_{i\in I}}

be the projection map projecting out the last
m^{\prime\prime}-m^{\prime}
factors of the product bundle
\operatorname{ev}^{*}(\oplus_{i\in I}V_{i})^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}
and then forgetting the last
m^{\prime\prime}-m^{\prime}
marked points.
The map (
5.83
) extends to a rational map:

(5.84)

\overline{\pi}:\overline{{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C^{i}_{m+D!+D!,d})_{i\in I}}}\dashrightarrow\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C^{i}_{m+D!,d})_{i\in I}}.

This rational map is ill-defined in the region:

(5.85)

\overline{{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C^{i}_{m+D!+D!,d})_{i\in I}}}\cap(\operatorname{ev}^{*}P_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}})_{i\in I}}-\operatorname{ev}^{*}\bigoplus_{i\in I}V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}})\cap\operatorname{ev}^{*}(0\times P_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}-m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}})

which is empty.
Hence, the map (
5.83
) extends to a generically étale map:

(5.86)

\overline{\pi}:\overline{{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C^{i}_{m+D!+D!,d})_{i\in I}}}\to\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C^{i}_{m+D!,d})_{i\in I}}

of degree
(D\cdot d)!

with the additional property that the restriction of
P\operatorname{ev}^{*}\infty_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}}
to the codomain of
\overline{\pi}
pulls back to the restriction of
P\operatorname{ev}^{*}\infty_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}})_{i\in I}}
to the domain of
\overline{\pi}
.
Hence:

(5.87)

\displaystyle\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+D!,d})}]}\exp(\infty_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}}+\pi_{P_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}}}^{*}H)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(D\cdot d)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+D!+D!,d})}]}\exp(\overline{\pi}^{*}\infty_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}}+\overline{\pi}^{*}\pi_{P_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}}}^{*}H)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(D\cdot d)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+D!+D!,d})}]}\exp(\infty_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}})_{i\in I}}+\pi_{P_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}})_{i\in I}}}^{*}F^{*}H)

\displaystyle\overset{\textnormal{Lemma \ref{lemmaperturbingdivisors}}}{=}\frac{1}{(E\cdot d)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+D!+E!,d})}]}\exp(\infty_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}})_{i\in I}}+\pi_{P_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}})_{i\in I}}}^{*}F^{*}H).

Now suppose that
\infty_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}}+\pi_{P_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}}}^{*}H
is nef.
Then
\overline{\pi}^{*}\infty_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}}+\overline{\pi}^{*}\pi_{P_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}}}^{*}H
is nef
and hence the restriction of
\infty_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}})_{i\in I}}+\pi_{P_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}})_{i\in I}}}^{*}F^{*}H
to
\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+D!+D!,d})}
is also nef.
∎

Lemma 5.13
.

Let
C_{\bullet}=(C_{m,d})_{m\in\mathbb{N},\ d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)}
be a system of cones for
V

and suppose that
V
is strongly convex.
Let
D
,
E
be smooth and generically transverse to
C_{\bullet}
and so that
\mathcal{O}(D)
and
V^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(D)
are globally generated and
E\sim D
.
Suppose
D
is also
V
-large.
Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
and define
m^{\prime}=m+D\cdot d
and
m^{\prime\prime}=m^{\prime}+E\cdot d
.
Define
p_{j}:X^{m^{\prime\prime}}\to X
to be the
j
th projection map for each
j=1,\cdots,m^{\prime\prime}
.
Let
\widehat{D}=\sum_{j=m+1}^{m^{\prime}}p_{j}^{*}D
.
Then

(5.88)

D\textnormal{-vol}(C_{m,d})\leqslant\frac{1}{m!(D\cdot d)!(E\cdot d)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m^{\prime}+E!,d})}]\cap\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}}}^{*}\widehat{D}}\exp(\infty_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}}+\sum_{j=1}^{m^{\prime}}\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}}}^{*}p_{j}^{*}D).

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{O}(D)
is globally generated and
D\sim E
, we can choose
E
so that

a)

E
is still smooth and generically transverse to
C_{\bullet}
,

b)

E
is a small perturbation of
D
,

c)

the right-hand side of (
5.88
) is unchanged by Lemma
5.11
and

d)

there is an open dense subset
\nu
of
C_{m,d}
so that each curve
u
in the image of
\nu
in
X_{m,d}
has the property that
u^{-1}(D\cap E)
is empty.

Let
D^{\prime},E^{\prime}
be the pullbacks of
D
and
E
respectively to
\mathbb{C}\times X
via the natural projection map to
X
.
Suppose
D^{\prime}|_{t}:=D^{\prime}|_{t\times X}
and
E^{\prime}|_{t}:=E^{\prime}|_{t\times X}
for each
t\in\mathbb{C}
.
Let
(D_{j})_{j=m+1,\cdots,m^{\prime}}
be generic among tuples of smooth divisors in
\mathbb{C}\times X
linearly equivalent to
D
, each of which are transverse to
0\times X
, and whose restriction to
0\times X
is
D
.
Suppose
\widetilde{p}_{j}:(\mathbb{C}\times X)^{m^{\prime\prime}}\to\mathbb{C}\times X
is the
j
th projection map for each
j=1,\cdots,m^{\prime\prime}
.
Let
\widetilde{D}:=\sum_{j=m+1}^{m^{\prime}}\widetilde{p}_{j}^{*}D_{j}
.

We let
\widetilde{U}

be the subspace of curves
u
in
(\mathbb{C}\times X)_{m^{\prime}+E^{\prime}!,(0,d)}

so that

(1)

the
j
th marked point maps to
D_{j}
for each
j=m+1,\cdots,m^{\prime}
and

(2)

if we remove the last
m^{\prime\prime}-m
marked points of
u
then the resulting map is still stable (I.e. no irreducible components of the domain curve are contracted under the forgetful map).

By
d)
combined with the fact that
D
and
E
are generically transverse to
C_{\bullet}
, we have that there is a neighborhood
N\subset\widetilde{U}
of the image of an open dense subset of
C_{m+D!+E!,d}
in
X_{m+D!+E!,d}=X_{m+D^{\prime}|_{0}!+E^{\prime}|_{0}!,(0,d)}
.

Let
(\mathbb{C}\times X)_{m+\widetilde{D}^{\widetilde{D}}+E!,(0,d)}
be the closure of
\widetilde{U}

inside
(\mathbb{C}\times X)_{m^{\prime}+E^{\prime}!,(0,d)}
.
Let
\widetilde{V}
be the pullback of
V
to
\mathbb{C}\times X
via the natural projection map
and
C_{m,(0,d)}\subset\widetilde{V}_{m,(0,d)}
the pullback of
C_{m,d}
via the map taking a curve
in
(\mathbb{C}\times X)_{m,(0,d)}

and post composing it with the projection map to
X
.
Also, let
C_{m+\widetilde{D}^{\widetilde{D}}+E^{\prime}!,(0,d)}\subset\widetilde{V}_{m^{\prime\prime},(0,d)}
