be the pullback
of
C_{m,(0,d)}
to
(\mathbb{C}\times X)_{m+\widetilde{D}^{\widetilde{D}}+E^{\prime}!,(0,d)}
via the natural projection map
forgetting the last
m^{\prime\prime}-m
marked points.
Define
\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+\widetilde{D}^{\widetilde{D}}+E^{\prime}!,(0,d)})}

to be the closure of

\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+\widetilde{D}^{\widetilde{D}}+E^{\prime}!,(0,d)})

in
\operatorname{ev}^{*}P_{\widetilde{V}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}}
.
Let

(5.89)

(C_{m+\widetilde{D}^{\widetilde{D}}+E^{\prime}!,(0,d)})_{t}\subset C_{m+\widetilde{D}^{\widetilde{D}}+E^{\prime}!,(0,d)}

be the restriction of
C_{m+\widetilde{D}^{\widetilde{D}}+E^{\prime}!,(0,d)}
to

(5.90)

(\{t\}\times X)_{m^{\prime}+E^{\prime}|_{t}!,(0,d)}\cap(\mathbb{C}\times X)_{m+\widetilde{D}^{\widetilde{D}}+E^{\prime}!,(0,d)}.

Let
\Delta_{t}
be the Cartier divisor in
\operatorname{ev}^{*}P_{\widetilde{V}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}}
given by the preimage
of the Cartier divisor
(\{t\}\times X)_{m^{\prime\prime},(0,d)}
inside
(\mathbb{C}\times X)_{m^{\prime\prime}!,(0,d)}
for each
t\in\mathbb{C}
.
Also, let
\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+\widetilde{D}^{\widetilde{D}}+E^{\prime}!,(0,d)})}_{t}
be the closure of

\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+\widetilde{D}^{\widetilde{D}}+E^{\prime}!,(0,d)})_{t}
in
\operatorname{ev}^{*}P_{\widetilde{V}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}}
for each
t\in\mathbb{C}
.
By Lemma
4.19
this is isomorphic to the restriction of
\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+\widetilde{D}^{\widetilde{D}}+E^{\prime}!,(0,d)})}

to
\operatorname{ev}^{*}P_{\widetilde{V}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}}|_{\textnormal{\eqref{eqnttimesXspace}}}

for each
t\in\mathbb{C}
.
Therefore, we have

(5.91)

[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+\widetilde{D}^{\widetilde{D}}+E^{\prime}!,(0,d)})}]\cap\Delta_{t}=[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+\widetilde{D}^{\widetilde{D}}+E^{\prime}!,(0,d)})_{t}}]

for each
t\in\mathbb{C}
since any defining function for
\{t\}\subset\mathbb{C}
pulls back to a regular one on

(5.92)

\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+\widetilde{D}^{\widetilde{D}}+E^{\prime}!,(0,d)})}.

We will identify
X_{m^{\prime}+E!,d}
with
(\{t\}\times X)_{m^{\prime}+E^{\prime}|_{t}!,(0,d)}
for each
t\in\mathbb{C}

via the natural identification
X=\{t\}\times X
.
Let

(5.93)

C_{m+\widetilde{D}^{\widetilde{D}}+E^{\prime}!,(0,d)}|_{\widetilde{U}}\subset C_{m+\widetilde{D}^{\widetilde{D}}+E^{\prime}!,(0,d)}

be the corresponding pullback to
\widetilde{U}
.
Since
\widetilde{U}
contains
N
, we have that a dense open subset of
C_{m+D!+E!,d}
is also open in
(C_{m+\widetilde{D}^{\widetilde{D}}+E^{\prime}!,(0,d)})_{0}
.
Therefore, by (
5.91
),
we have that

(5.94)

[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+\widetilde{D}^{\widetilde{D}}+E^{\prime}!,(0,d)})}]\cap\Delta_{0}-[C_{m+D!+E!,d}]

is represented by a positive sum of integral substacks
in
CH_{*}(\operatorname{ev}^{*}P_{\widetilde{V}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}})
.

Since
V
is strongly convex and
D
is
V
-large, we have by Lemma
4.19
that

(5.95)

\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}:\widetilde{V}_{m^{\prime}+D^{\prime}!}\to\operatorname{ev}^{*}\widetilde{V}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}

is injective.
Since
E
is a small perturbation of
D
, the map

(5.96)

\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}:\widetilde{V}_{m^{\prime}+E^{\prime}!}\to\operatorname{ev}^{*}\widetilde{V}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}

is also injective.
Hence, the map

(5.97)

\pi:\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+\widetilde{D}^{\widetilde{D}}+E^{\prime}!,d})\to\operatorname{ev}^{*}\widetilde{V}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}

given by projecting out the last
m^{\prime\prime}-m^{\prime}
factors and then forgetting the last
m^{\prime\prime}-m^{\prime}
marked points
extends to a map

(5.98)

\overline{\pi}:\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+\widetilde{D}^{\widetilde{D}}+E^{\prime}!,d})}\to\operatorname{ev}^{*}P_{\widetilde{V}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}

with the property that
\overline{\pi}^{*}\infty_{\widetilde{V}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}
is equal to the restriction of
\infty_{\widetilde{V}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}}
to the domain of
\overline{\pi}
.
Therefore, since
D_{\sum m^{\prime},V}
is nef by Corollary
4.10
,

(5.99)

P\operatorname{ev}^{*}(\infty_{\widetilde{V}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}}+\sum_{j=1}^{m^{\prime}}\pi^{*}_{\widetilde{V}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}}\widetilde{p}_{j}^{*}D_{j})|_{\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+\widetilde{D}^{\widetilde{D}}+E^{\prime}!,d})}}=\overline{\pi^{*}}D^{\prime}_{\sum m^{\prime},\widetilde{V}}

is also nef.
Combining this with the above stated fact that (
5.94
) is represented by a positive sum of integral substacks in
\operatorname{ev}^{*}P_{\widetilde{V}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}}
, we have

(5.100)

\displaystyle\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+D!,d})}]}\exp(D_{\sum m^{\prime},V})

\displaystyle\overset{\textnormal{Lemma \ref{lemmawelldefinedprojectionmap}}}{=}\frac{1}{(E\cdot d)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+D!+E!,d})}]}\exp(\infty_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}}+\sum_{j=1}^{m^{\prime}}\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}}}^{*}p_{j}^{*}D)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{(E\cdot d)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+\widetilde{D}^{\widetilde{D}}+E^{\prime}!,(0,d)})}]\cap\Delta_{0}}\exp(\infty_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}}+\sum_{j=1}^{m^{\prime}}\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}}}^{*}\widetilde{p}_{j}^{*}D_{j}).

Hence,

(5.101)

D\textnormal{-vol}(C_{m,d})\overset{\textnormal{Definition \ref{defnvolumeformultiplecones}}}{=}\frac{1}{m!(D\cdot d)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+D!,d})}]}\exp(D_{\sum m^{\prime},V})

(5.102)

\overset{\textnormal{\eqref{eqndifficultpartDD}}}{\leqslant}\frac{1}{m!(D\cdot d)!(E\cdot d)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+\widetilde{D}^{\widetilde{D}}+E^{\prime}!,(0,d)})}]\cap\Delta_{0}}\exp(\infty_{\widetilde{V}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}}+\sum_{j=1}^{m^{\prime}}\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}}}^{*}\widetilde{p}_{j}^{*}D_{j})

(5.103)

=\frac{1}{m!(D\cdot d)!(E\cdot d)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+\widetilde{D}^{\widetilde{D}}+E^{\prime}!,d})}]\cap\Delta_{1}}\exp(\infty_{\widetilde{V}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}}+\sum_{j=1}^{m^{\prime}}\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}}}^{*}\widetilde{p}_{j}^{*}D_{j}).

Since
D_{j}|_{\{1\}\times X}
,
j=1,\cdots,m
is a generic tuple of smooth divisors in the linear system containing
D
,
we have the following equality of Chow classes:

(5.104)

\displaystyle P\operatorname{ev}_{*}([\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+\widetilde{D}^{\widetilde{D}}+E^{\prime}!,d})}]\cap\Delta_{1})

\displaystyle\overset{\textnormal{\eqref{eqneffectiveintersection}}}{=}P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+\widetilde{D}^{\widetilde{D}}+E^{\prime}!,d})_{1}}]

\displaystyle=P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m^{\prime}+E^{\prime}|_{1}!,d})}]\cap\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}}}^{*}\widetilde{D}|_{(\{1\}\times X)^{m^{\prime\prime}}}

\displaystyle=P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m^{\prime}+E!,d})}]\cap\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}}}^{*}\widehat{D}

inside
P_{\widehat{V}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}}
.
Hence, (
5.103
) is equal to the right-hand side of (
5.88
).

∎

Proposition 5.14
.

Let
C_{\bullet}=(C_{m,d})_{m\in\mathbb{N},\ d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)}
be a system of cones for
V
and suppose that
V
is strongly convex.
Let
D
be
V
-large, generically transverse to
C_{\bullet}
and so that
\mathcal{O}(D)
and
V^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(D)
are globally generated.
Also, let
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
and define
m^{\prime}=m+D\cdot d
.
Suppose that
C_{0,d}\to X_{0,d}
has pure relative dimension
h
and that
X_{0,d}
is of pure dimension too.
Then

(5.105)

D\textnormal{-vol}(C_{m,d})\leqslant\frac{1}{h!m!(D\cdot d)!}\sum_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{m^{\prime}}=0}^{\dim X}\left|\langle D^{i_{1}},\cdots,D^{i_{m^{\prime}}}\rangle_{m^{\prime},d}^{s(C_{\bullet})}\right|.

Proof.

Let
m^{\prime\prime}:=m^{\prime}+D\cdot d
.
Define
p_{j}:X^{m^{\prime\prime}}\to X
to be the
j
th projection map for each
j=1,\cdots,m^{\prime\prime}
.
We also let
E
be a generic linearly equivalent perturbation of
D
.
Let
\widehat{D}=\sum_{j=m+1}^{m^{\prime}}p_{j}^{*}D
.

We have

(5.106)

D\textnormal{-vol}(C_{m,d})

(5.107)

\overset{\textnormal{Lemma \ref{lemmaDDDfactorialinequality}}}{\leqslant}\frac{1}{m!((D\cdot d)!)^{2}}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m^{\prime}+E!,d})}]\cap\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}}}^{*}\widehat{D}}\exp(\infty_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}}+\sum_{j=1}^{m^{\prime}}\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}}}^{*}p_{j}^{*}D)

(5.108)

=\frac{1}{m!((D\cdot d)!)^{2}}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m^{\prime}+E!,d})}]\cap\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}}}^{*}\widehat{D}}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\sum_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{m^{\prime}}=0}^{\dim(X)}\frac{(\infty_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}})^{k}}{k!}\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}}}^{*}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{m^{\prime}}p_{j}^{*}\frac{D^{i_{j}}}{i_{j}!}\right)

(5.109)

=\frac{1}{m!((D\cdot d)!)^{2}}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m^{\prime}+E!,d})}]}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\sum_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{m^{\prime}}=0}^{\dim(X)}\frac{(\infty_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}})^{k}}{k!}\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}}}^{*}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{h}p_{j}^{*}\frac{D^{i_{j}}}{i_{j}!}\prod_{j=m+1}^{m^{\prime}}p_{j}^{*}\frac{D^{i_{j}+1}}{i_{j}!}\right)

(5.110)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{m!((D\cdot d)!)^{2}}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\sum_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{m^{\prime}}=0}^{\dim(X)}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{k!\prod_{j=1}^{m^{\prime}}i_{j}!}\left|\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m^{\prime}+E!,d})}]}(\infty_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}})^{k}\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}}}^{*}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{h}p_{j}^{*}D^{i_{j}}\prod_{j=m+1}^{m^{\prime}}p_{j}^{*}D^{i_{j}+1}\right)\right|

(5.111)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{m!((D\cdot d)!)^{2}}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\sum_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{m^{\prime}}=0}^{\dim(X)}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{k!}\left|\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m^{\prime}+E!,d})}]}(\infty_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}})^{k}\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}}}^{*}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{h}p_{j}^{*}D^{i_{j}}\prod_{j=m+1}^{m^{\prime}}p_{j}^{*}D^{i_{j}+1}\right)\right|

(5.112)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{m!((D\cdot d)!)^{2}}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\sum_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{m^{\prime}}=0}^{\dim(X)}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{k!}\left|\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m^{\prime}+E!,d})}]}(\infty_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}})^{k}\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}}}^{*}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{m^{\prime}}p_{j}^{*}D^{i_{j}}\right)\right|

(5.113)

\displaystyle\overset{\textnormal{\eqref{eqnpushforwardclosurerelation}}}{\leqslant}\frac{1}{m!((D\cdot d)!)^{2}}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\sum_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{m^{\prime}}=0}^{\dim(X)}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{k!}\left|\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}P\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}_{*}[\overline{(C_{m^{\prime}+E!,d})}]}(\infty_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}})^{k}\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}}}^{*}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{m^{\prime}}p_{j}^{*}D^{i_{j}}\right)\right|

(5.114)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m!((D\cdot d)!)^{2}}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\sum_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{m^{\prime}}=0}^{\dim(X)}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{k!}\left|\int_{[\overline{C_{m^{\prime}+E!,d}}]}P\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}^{*}P\operatorname{ev}^{*}(\infty_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}})^{k}P\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}^{*}P\operatorname{ev}^{*}\pi_{P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}}}^{*}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{m^{\prime}}p_{j}^{*}D^{i_{j}}\right)\right|

(5.115)

\displaystyle\overset{\textnormal{\eqref{eqnpulbackofo1r}}}{=}\frac{1}{m!((D\cdot d)!)^{2}}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\sum_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{m^{\prime}}=0}^{\dim(X)}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{k!}\left|\int_{[\overline{C_{m^{\prime}+E!,d}}]}\infty_{V_{m^{\prime}+E!,d}}^{k}\pi_{P_{V_{m+E!,d}}}^{*}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{m^{\prime}}\operatorname{ev}_{j}^{*}D^{i_{j}}\right)\right|

(5.116)

=\frac{1}{m!((D\cdot d)!)^{2}}\sum_{k=h}^{\infty}\sum_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{m^{\prime}}=0}^{\dim(X)}\frac{1}{k!}\left|\int_{[\overline{C_{m^{\prime}+E!,d}}]}\infty_{V_{m^{\prime}+E!,d}}^{k}\pi_{P_{V_{m+E!,d}}}^{*}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{m^{\prime}}\operatorname{ev}_{j}^{*}D^{i_{j}}\right)\right|

(5.117)

\leqslant\frac{1}{h!m!((D\cdot d)!)^{2}}\sum_{k=h}^{\infty}\sum_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{m^{\prime}}=0}^{\dim(X)}\left|\int_{[\overline{C_{m^{\prime}+E!,d}}]}\infty_{V_{m^{\prime}+E!,d}}^{k}\pi_{P_{V_{m+E!,d}}}^{*}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{m^{\prime}}\operatorname{ev}_{j}^{*}D^{i_{j}}\right)\right|

(5.118)

\overset{\textnormal{Definition \ref{defnsegrefromsystemsofcones}}}{=}\frac{1}{h!m!(D\cdot d)!}\sum_{k=h}^{\infty}\sum_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{m^{\prime}}=0}^{\dim(X)}\left|\int_{[\overline{C_{m^{\prime},d}}]}(\infty_{V_{m^{\prime},d}})^{k}\pi_{P_{V_{m^{\prime},d}}}^{*}\prod_{j=1}^{m^{\prime}}\operatorname{ev}_{j}^{*}D^{i_{j}}\right|

(5.119)

\overset{\textnormal{Definitions \ref{defnsefsef} and \ref{defnsegrefromsystemsofcones}}}{=}\frac{1}{h!m!(D\cdot d)!}\sum_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{m^{\prime}}=0}^{\dim(X)}\left|\left<D^{i_{1}},\cdots,D^{i_{m^{\prime}}}\right>^{s(C_{\bullet})}\right|.

The last equality also holds because
C_{m^{\prime},d}
has pure dimension.

∎

5.2.
Bounds for Summands

We need to compute how
D
-volume changes when we remove a cone (See Proposition
5.17
). This is important in the proof of the main theorem in Section
9
.
Let
X
be a smooth projective variety.

Lemma 5.15
.

Let
U\subset X
be open and
I
a finite set.
Suppose
(D^{\prime}_{i})_{i\in I}
,
D
are divisors on
X
so that
D
is smooth and let

(V_{i})_{i\in I}
be a finite collection of vector bundles over
X
.
Also, let
C^{i}_{\bullet}
be a system of cones for
V_{i}
for each
i\in I
.
Suppose that
\mathcal{O}(D^{\prime}_{i})
and
V_{i}^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(D^{\prime}_{i})
are globally generated for each
i\in I

and that
D-\sum_{i\in I}D^{\prime}_{i}
is nef.
Then

(5.120)

D\textnormal{-vol}(C^{i}_{m,d})_{i\in I}\geqslant D\textnormal{-vol}(C^{i}_{m,d}|_{U})_{i\in I}

for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
(Definition
4.20
).

Proof.

Let
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
m^{\prime}=m+D\cdot d
.
By Corollary
4.11
we have that

D_{\sum m^{\prime},(V_{i})_{i\in I}}
is nef.
Hence,

(5.121)

D\textnormal{-vol}(C^{i}_{m,d})_{i\in I}\overset{\textnormal{Definition \ref{defnvolumeformultiplecones}}}{=}\frac{1}{m!(D\cdot d)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}\left[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}((C^{i}_{m+D!,d})_{i\in I})}\right]}\exp(D_{\sum m^{\prime},(V_{i})_{i\in I}})

(5.122)

\geqslant\frac{1}{m!(D\cdot d)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}\left[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}((C^{i}_{m+D!,d}|_{U})_{i\in I})}\right]}\exp(D_{\sum m^{\prime},(V_{i})_{i\in I}})\overset{\textnormal{Definition \ref{defnvolumeformultiplecones}}}{=}D\textnormal{-vol}(C^{i}_{m,d}|_{U})_{i\in I}.

∎

Lemma 5.16
.

Let
(V_{i})_{i\in I}
be a finite collection of vector bundles over
X
.
Let
C_{\bullet}^{i}
be a system of cones for
V_{i}
for each
i\in I
.
Also, let
D^{\prime}_{i}
be a divisor on
X
so that
\mathcal{O}(D^{\prime}_{i})
and
V_{i}^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(D^{\prime}_{i})
are globally generated for each
i\in I
. Let
D,F
be smooth divisors on
X
so that

(1)

they are generically transverse to
C_{\bullet}^{i}
for each
i\in I
,

(2)

\mathcal{O}(D)
is globally generated,

(3)

D-\sum_{i\in I}D^{\prime}_{i}
is nef

(4)

and
F\sim 2D
.

Then

D\textnormal{-vol}(C_{m,d}^{i})_{i\in I}\leqslant\frac{1}{m!(F\cdot d)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C_{m+F!,d})}]}\exp(D_{\sum m^{\prime\prime},(V_{i})_{i\in I}})

for each
d\in H_{2}(X)^{+}
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
where
m^{\prime\prime}=m+F\cdot d
.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{O}(D)
is globally generated, we can choose a smooth linearly equivalent divisor
E

so that
D+E
is reduced and both
E
and
D+E
are generically transverse to
C_{\bullet}^{i}
for each
i\in I
.
Let
d\in H_{2}(X)^{+}
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
.
Define
m^{\prime}=m+D\cdot d
.
Let
F:X^{m^{\prime\prime}}\to X^{m^{\prime\prime}-m^{\prime}}
be the projection map to the last
m^{\prime\prime}-m^{\prime}
factors.
We have

(5.123)

D\textnormal{-vol}(C_{m,d}^{i})_{i\in I}\overset{\textnormal{Definition \ref{defnvolumeformultiplecones}}}{=}\frac{1}{m!(D\cdot d)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C^{i}_{m+D!,d})_{i\in I}}]}\exp(D_{\sum m^{\prime},(V_{i})_{i\in I}})

(5.124)

\overset{\textnormal{Lemma \ref{lemmawelldefinedprojectionmap}}}{=}\frac{1}{m!(D\cdot d)!(D\cdot d)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C^{i}_{m+D!+D!,d})_{i\in I}}]}\exp(D_{\sum m^{\prime\prime},(V_{i})_{i\in I}}-\pi_{P_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}})_{i\in I}}}^{*}F^{*}D_{\sum m^{\prime\prime}-m^{\prime}})

(See Definition
5.10
).
By Lemma
5.12
combined with Corollary
4.11
, we have that the restriction of

D_{\sum m^{\prime\prime},(V_{i})_{i\in I}}-\pi_{P_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}})_{i\in I}}}^{*}F^{*}D_{\sum m^{\prime\prime}-m^{\prime}}
to
\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C^{i}_{m+D!+D!,d})}
is nef. Also,
\pi_{P_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}})_{i\in I}}}^{*}F^{*}D_{\sum m^{\prime\prime}-m^{\prime}}
is nef.
Since the integral over a positive sum of integral substacks of a wedge product of nef classes is non-negative, we get that (
5.124
) is less than or equal to:

(5.125)

\frac{1}{m!(D\cdot d)!(D\cdot d)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C^{i}_{m+D!+D!,d})_{i\in I}}]}\exp(D_{\sum m^{\prime\prime},(V_{i})_{i\in I}})

(5.126)

\overset{\textnormal{Lemma \ref{lemmaperturbingdivisors}}}{=}\frac{1}{m!(D\cdot d)!(E\cdot d)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C^{i}_{m+D!+E!,d})_{i\in I}}]}\exp(D_{\sum m^{\prime\prime},(V_{i})_{i\in I}})

(5.127)

=\frac{1}{m!(D\cdot d+E\cdot d)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C^{i}_{m+(D+E)!,d})_{i\in I}}]}\exp(D_{\sum m^{\prime\prime},(V_{i})_{i\in I}})

(5.128)

\overset{\textnormal{Lemma \ref{lemmaperturbingdivisors}}}{=}\frac{1}{m!(F\cdot d)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(C^{i}_{m+F!,d})_{i\in I}}]}\exp(D_{\sum m^{\prime\prime},(V_{i})_{i\in I}}).

∎

Proposition 5.17
.

Let
(V_{i})_{i\in I}
be a finite collection of vector bundles over
X
and let
\star\in I
.
Suppose that
V_{\star}|_{U}
is strongly convex for some dense open subset
U\subset X
.
Let
C^{i}_{\bullet}
be a system of cones for
V_{i}
for each
i\in I
and suppose

C^{\star}_{\bullet}|_{U}=(V_{\star})_{\bullet}|_{U}
.
Suppose that
D^{\prime}_{i}
is a divisor in
X
so that
\mathcal{O}(D^{\prime}_{i})
and
V_{i}^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(D^{\prime}_{i})
are globally generated for each
i\in I
.
Suppose that
D
and
F
are smooth divisors on
X
satisfying

•

D-\sum_{i\in I}D^{\prime}_{i}
is nef,

•

F
is
V_{\star}
-large,
(Definition
4.14
),

•

F\sim 2D
,

•

and
D
and
F

are generically transverse to
(C^{i}_{\bullet})_{i\in I}
(Definition
4.5
).

Then

(5.129)

D\textnormal{-vol}(C^{i}_{m,d}|_{U})_{i\in I-\star}\leqslant g_{d}!F\textnormal{-vol}(C^{i}_{m,d})_{i\in I}

where

(5.130)

g_{d}=c_{1}(V_{\star})(d)+\dim(V_{\star})

for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)-0
.

Proof.

Let
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)-0
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
m^{\prime\prime}=m+F\cdot d
.
Also, let

(5.131)

P_{\star}:P_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}})_{i\in I}}\to P_{V_{\star}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}}

(5.132)

P_{\perp}:P_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}})_{i\in I}}\to P_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}})_{i\in I-\star}}

be the natural projection maps.
We have

(5.133)

D\textnormal{-vol}(C^{i}_{m,d}|_{U})_{i\in I-\star}

(5.134)

\overset{\textnormal{Lemma \ref{lemmaboundDvolbyanother}}}{\leqslant}\frac{1}{m!(F\cdot d)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}\left[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}((C^{i}_{m+F!,d}|_{U})_{i\in I-\star})}\right]}\exp(D_{\sum m^{\prime\prime},(V_{i})_{i\in I-\star}}).

Since
V_{\star}|_{U}
is convex, we get that

(5.135)

(V_{\star})_{m+F!,d}|_{U}=(C^{\star})_{m+F!,d}|_{U}

is a vector bundle over
X_{m+F!,d}|_{U}
of dimension
g_{d}
. Combining this with Lemma
4.19
and the fact that
F
is
V_{\star}
-large, the map
P_{\perp}|_{\operatorname{ev}(\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}((C^{i}_{m+F!,d}|_{U}))_{i\in I})}

has fibers equal to projective space of dimension
g_{d}
.
Therefore,
(
5.134
) is equal to

(5.136)

\frac{1}{m!(F\cdot d)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}\left[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}((C^{i}_{m+F!,d}|_{U})_{i\in I})}\right]}((P_{\star})^{*}\infty_{V_{\star}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime\prime}}})^{g_{d}}\cdot P_{\perp}^{*}\exp(D_{\sum m^{\prime\prime},(V_{i})_{i\in I-\star}})

(5.137)

=\frac{1}{m!(F\cdot d)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}\left[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}((C^{i}_{m+F!,d}|_{U})_{i\in I})}\right]}((P_{\star})^{*}D_{\sum m^{\prime\prime},V_{\star}})^{g_{d}}\cdot P_{\perp}^{*}\exp(D_{\sum m^{\prime\prime},(V_{i})_{i\in I-\star}})

(5.138)

=\frac{g_{d}!}{m!(F\cdot d)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}\left[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}((C^{i}_{m+F!,d}|_{U})_{i\in I})}\right]}\frac{((P_{\star})^{*}D_{\sum m^{\prime\prime},V_{\star}})^{g_{d}}}{g_{d}!}\cdot P_{\perp}^{*}\exp(D_{\sum m^{\prime\prime},(V_{i})_{i\in I-\star}}).

Since the integral of a wedge product of nef classes is non-negative, we get by Corollary
4.11
that this is less than or equal to

(5.139)

\leqslant\frac{g_{d}!}{m!(F\cdot d)!}\sum_{r\geqslant 0}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}\left[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}((C^{i}_{m+F!,d}|_{U})_{i\in I})}\right]}\frac{((P_{\star})^{*}D_{\sum m^{\prime\prime},V_{\star}})^{r}}{r!}\cdot P_{\perp}^{*}\exp(D_{\sum m^{\prime\prime},(V_{i})_{i\in I-\star}})

(5.140)

\leqslant\frac{g_{d}!}{m!(F\cdot d)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}\left[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}((C^{i}_{m+F!,d}|_{U})_{i\in I})}\right]}\exp((P_{\star})^{*}D_{\sum m^{\prime\prime},V_{\star}})\cdot\exp(P_{\perp}^{*}D_{\sum m^{\prime\prime},(V_{i})_{i\in I-\star}})

(5.141)

=\frac{g_{d}!}{m!(F\cdot d)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}\left[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}((C^{i}_{m+F!,d}|_{U})_{i\in I})}\right]}\exp((P_{\star})^{*}D_{\sum m^{\prime\prime},V_{\star}}+P_{\perp}^{*}D_{\sum m^{\prime\prime},(V_{i})_{i\in I-\star}})

(5.142)

=\frac{g_{d}!}{m!(F\cdot d)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}\left[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}((C^{i}_{m+F!,d}|_{U})_{i\in I})}\right]}\exp(F_{\sum m^{\prime\prime},(V_{i})_{i\in I}})

(5.143)

\overset{\textnormal{Definition \ref{defnvolumeformultiplecones}}}{=}g_{d}!F\textnormal{-vol}(C^{i}_{m,d}|_{U})_{i\in I}\overset{\textnormal{Lemma \ref{lemmaineqaulityrestriction}}}{\leqslant}g_{d}!F\textnormal{-vol}(C^{i}_{m,d})_{i\in I}.

∎

5.3.
Curves in Compactifications of Vector Bundles

We need to compare the
E
-volume of the projective compactification of a vector bundle with the
D
-volume of its base for appropriate
D
and
E
.
We will use the following well known lemma.

Lemma 5.18
.

Let
Z
be a proper variety with a
(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{k}
-action and let
L\to Z
be a line bundle.
Then
L
is nef if and only if

c_{1}(L)\cdot C\geqslant 0
for each
(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{k}
-equivariant proper curve
C
in
Z
.

Proof.

If
L
is nef then
c_{1}(L)\cdot C\geqslant 0
for each
(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{k}
-equivariant proper curve
C
in
Z
by definition.

Now suppose that
c_{1}(L)\cdot C\geqslant 0
for each
(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{k}
-equivariant proper curve
C
in
Z
.
It is sufficient for us to show that

c_{1}(L)\cdot C\geqslant 0
for each proper irreducible curve
C
in
Z
.
Let us fix such a proper irreducible curve
C
in
Z
.

Let
d\in H_{2}^{+}(Z)
be its homology class and
g
the genus of its normalization.
Suppose
f:\widetilde{C}\to Z
is the corresponding map from its normalization to
Z
.
Also, let
Z_{g,0,d}
be the moduli space of stable genus
g
nodal curves mapping to
Z
representing
d
.
Recall that
\underline{Z_{g,0,d}}
is its coarse moduli space.
Then there is a one parameter subgroup
\mathbb{C}^{*}\subset(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{k}

so that its fixed point locus on
\underline{Z_{g,0,d}}
is the same as the corresponding
(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{k}
fixed point locus.
Let us fix this
\mathbb{C}^{*}
action on
\underline{Z_{g,0,d}}
.

Then
f
corresponds to an element
[f]
of
\underline{Z_{g,0,d}}
. Let
\widehat{f}:\mathbb{C}^{*}\to\underline{Z_{g,0,d}}
be the
\mathbb{C}^{*}
orbit satisfying
\widetilde{f}(0)=[f]
.
Since
\underline{Z_{g,0,d}}
is proper, this extends to a
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-equivariant map
F:\mathbb{P}^{1}\to\underline{Z_{g,0,d}}
.
Any curve
u:C^{\prime}\to Z
in
Z_{g,0,d}
mapping to
F(\infty)
is invariant under the
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-action.
Therefore,
c_{1}(L)\cdot u_{*}(C^{\prime})\geqslant 0
. Since
u
is homologous to
C
, we get
c_{1}(L)\cdot C=c_{1}(L)\cdot u_{*}(C^{\prime})\geqslant 0
.

∎

Lemma 5.19
.

Let
X
be a smooth projective variety.
Let
(V_{i})_{i\in I}
be a finite collection of vector bundles over
X
and let
\check{I}\subset I
.
Define
V_{\check{I}}=\bigoplus_{i\in\check{I}}V_{i}
.
Let
D^{\prime}_{i}\subset X
be a reduced divisor so that
V_{i}^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(D^{\prime}_{i})
and
\mathcal{O}(D^{\prime}_{i})
are globally generated for each
i\in I
and let
D
be a reduced divisor so that
D-\sum_{i\in I}D^{\prime}_{i}
is nef.
Let

(5.144)

P_{0}:=P_{(V_{i})_{i\in(I-I^{\prime})\sqcup\{\check{I}\}}},\quad P_{1}:=P_{(V_{i})_{i\in I}}

and
\pi_{j}:P_{j}\to X
,
j=0,1
the natural projection maps.
Suppose
f:\chi\to X
is a proper morphism from a stack
\chi
.
Let
C\subset f^{*}\oplus_{i\in I}V_{i}
be a substack that is invariant under the
\mathbb{C}^{*}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}
action given by

(5.145)

\displaystyle(\mathbb{C}^{*}\times\mathbb{C}^{*})\times f^{*}\oplus_{i\in I}V_{i}\to f^{*}\oplus_{i\in I}V_{i}

\displaystyle(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\cdot(v_{i})_{i\in I}\to((\xi_{1}v_{i})_{i\in I-\check{I}},(\xi_{2}v_{i})_{i\in\check{I}}).

We let
C_{0}
be its closure inside
f^{*}P_{0}

and
C_{1}
its closure inside
f^{*}P_{1}
.
Let
Pf_{*}:f^{*}P_{j}\to P_{j}
be the natural map covering
f
for
j=0,1
.
Then

(5.146)

\int_{Pf_{*}[C_{0}]}\exp(\pi_{0}^{*}D+\infty_{(V_{i})_{i\in(I-\check{I})\sqcup\{\check{I}\}}})\leqslant\int_{Pf_{*}[C_{1}]}\exp(\pi_{1}^{*}D+\infty_{(V_{i})_{i\in I}}).

Remark 5.20
.

Note that this inequality is very poor in some cases. For instance, if
X
is a point,
V_{i}=\mathbb{C}
for each
i\in I
,
f
is the identity map and
C=\bigoplus_{i\in I}V_{i}
, then the left-hand side of (
5.146
) is the volume of
\mathbb{P}^{|I|}
and the right-hand side of (
5.146
) is the volume of
(\mathbb{P}^{1})^{|I|}
which is
|I|!
times bigger.

Proof of Lemma
5.19
.

We have that
Pf_{*}[C_{0}]
and
Pf_{*}[C_{1}]
are sums
\sum_{j=0}^{k}\alpha_{j}[\mathcal{C}^{0}_{j}]
and
\sum_{j=0}^{k}\alpha_{j}[\mathcal{C}^{1}_{j}]

respectively where
\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{k}\geqslant 0
and
\mathcal{C}^{0}_{j}
and
\mathcal{C}^{1}_{j}
are closures of the same
\mathbb{C}^{*}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}
-invariant integral subscheme
\mathcal{C}_{j}\subset\oplus_{i\in I}V_{i}
in
P_{0}
and
P_{1}
respectively for each
j=1,\cdots,k
where
\mathbb{C}^{*}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}
acts on

(5.147)

\oplus_{i\in I}V_{i}=\left(\oplus_{i\in I-\check{I}}V_{i}\right)\bigoplus\left(\oplus_{i\in\check{I}}V_{i}\right)

by scaling the first summand by the first
\mathbb{C}^{*}
factor and scaling the second summand by the second
\mathbb{C}^{*}
factor.
Therefore, by linearity, it is sufficient to prove that Equation (
5.146
) holds where
\chi=X
and
f
is the identity map and hence
C\subset\oplus_{i\in I}V_{i}
,
C_{0}\subset P_{0}
and
C_{1}\subset P_{1}
.

Let

(5.148)

P^{\prime}:=P_{(V_{i})_{i\in I-\check{I}}}\subset P_{1}.

Let
B
be the blowup of
0\times P^{\prime}
inside
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times P_{1}
and let
E\cong P_{0}
be the exceptional divisor.
Define
p:B\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
to be the composition

(5.149)

B\overset{\mathrm{Bl}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{P}^{1}\times P_{1}\overset{}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{P}^{1}

where the first map is the blowdown map and the second map is the natural projection map.
Define

(5.150)

\widehat{D}:=\mathrm{Bl}^{*}(\mathbb{P}^{1}\times(\pi_{1}^{*}D+\infty_{(V_{i})_{i\in I}}))+p^{-1}\{\infty\}-E.

We will now prove that
\widehat{D}
is nef.
We have a
\mathbb{C}^{*}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}
action on
B
extending the one on
(\mathbb{C}-0)\times\oplus_{i\in I}V_{i}
where
(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\in\mathbb{C}^{*}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}
sends
(z,(v_{i})_{i\in I})

to
(\xi_{1}z,(\xi_{2}v)_{i\in I})
.
By Lemma
5.18
,
it is sufficient to check that
\widehat{D}
pairs non-negatively with each
\mathbb{C}^{*}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}
-equivariant proper irreducible curve.

Let us fix such a curve
C
.
If
C
is contained in
E
then it pairs non-negatively with
\widehat{D}
since the restriction of
\widetilde{D}
to
E\cong P_{0}
is
\pi_{0}^{*}D+\infty_{(V_{i})_{i\in(I-\check{I})\sqcup\{\check{I}\}}}
which is nef by Corollary
4.11
.
If
C
is disjoint from
E
then it
is contained in
(\mathbb{P}^{1}-0)\times P_{1}

and since
\widehat{D}=(\mathbb{P}^{1}-0)\times(\pi_{1}^{*}D+D_{(V_{i})_{i\in I}})+p^{-1}(\{\infty\})
in this region we get it pairs non-negatively with
\widehat{D}

by Corollary
4.11
too.
If
C
is contained in the proper transform of
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times x
for some
x\in X
then
\widehat{D}\cdot C=p^{-1}(\infty)\cdot C-E\cdot C\geqslant 0
since
p^{-1}(\infty)\cdot C=1
and
E\cdot C\leqslant 1
.
Also, if
C
is any other
(\mathbb{C}^{*}\times\mathbb{C}^{*})
-equivariant curve then it is the closure of the proper transform of
0\times L
in
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\oplus_{i\in I}V_{i}
where
L
is a line in a fiber of
\oplus_{i\in I}V_{i}
.
Such a line intersects
E
transversely once,
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathrm{Bl}^{*}\infty_{(V_{i})_{i\in I}}
at least once and the remaining summands of (
5.150
) zero times. Hence,
\widehat{C}\cdot C\geqslant 0
too.
Hence,
\widehat{D}
is nef.

Let
\widehat{C}
be the closure of the proper transform of
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times C_{1}
in
B
.
Since
C
is invariant under the action (
5.145
) and since
\widehat{D}
is nef, we have that
we have

(5.151)

\int_{[C_{0}]}\exp(\pi_{0}^{*}D+\infty_{(V_{i})_{i\in(I-\check{I})\sqcup\{\check{I}\}}})=\int_{[\widehat{C}]\cap[E]}\exp(\widehat{D})

(5.152)

\leqslant\int_{[\widehat{C}]\cap[p^{-1}(0)]}\exp(\widehat{D})

(5.153)

=\int_{[\widehat{C}]\cap[p^{-1}(1)]}\exp(\widehat{D})=\int_{[C_{1}]}\exp(\pi_{1}^{*}D+\infty_{(V_{i})_{i\in I}}).

∎

Proposition 5.21
.

Let
V
be a strongly convex vector bundle over a smooth projective variety
X
.
Suppose
(V_{i})_{i\in I}
is a finite collection of vector bundles over
P_{V}
so that
V_{i}=\pi_{P_{V}}^{*}(V_{i}|_{X})
for each
i\in I
.
Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
and let
\delta\in H_{2}^{+}(P_{V})
be the image of
d
induced by the natural inclusion map.
Let
C^{i}_{\bullet}
be a system of cones for
V_{i}
and
\underline{C}^{i}_{\bullet}
a system of cones for
V_{i}|_{X}
for each
i\in I
. Suppose that
C^{i}_{m,\delta}|_{V_{m,d}}=\Pi_{i}^{*}\underline{C}^{i}_{m,d}
via the natual identification
\Pi_{i}^{*}(V_{i}|_{X})_{m,d}=(V_{i})_{m,d}
where
\Pi_{i}:V_{m,d}\to X_{m,d}
is the natural projection map for each
i\in I
.
Define
\underline{C}^{V}_{\bullet}:=V_{\bullet}
.

Let
D
be a smooth divisor in
X
so that
D-\sum_{i\in I}D^{\prime}_{i}-D^{\prime}
is nef where
\mathcal{O}(D^{\prime}_{i})
and
V_{i}^{*}|_{X}\otimes\mathcal{O}(D^{\prime}_{i})
are globally generated for some divisor
D^{\prime}_{i}
in
X
for each
i\in I
and
V^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(D^{\prime})
is globally generated for some divisor
D^{\prime}
on
X
.
Let
E
be a smooth divisor on
P_{V}
which is generically transverse to
(C^{i}_{\bullet})_{i\in I}
so that
E-\pi_{P_{V}}^{*}D-\infty_{V}
is nef
and
E-\sum_{i\in I}E^{\prime}_{i}
is nef with
\mathcal{O}(E^{\prime}_{i})
and
V_{i}^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(E^{\prime}_{i})
globally generated for some divisor
E^{\prime}_{i}
for each
i\in I
.
Then

(5.154)

E\textnormal{-vol}(C^{i}_{m,\delta})_{i\in I}\geqslant D\textnormal{-vol}(\underline{C}^{i}_{m,d})_{i\in I\sqcup\{V\}}.

Proof.

Let
m^{\prime}:=m+D\cdot d
.
Let

p_{i}:X^{m^{\prime}}\to X

be the
i
th projection map for each

i=1,\cdots,m^{\prime}
.
Also, let

(5.155)

\displaystyle P_{0}

\displaystyle\equiv P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}\times_{X^{m^{\prime}}}P_{((V_{i}|_{X})^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}}=P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}},((V_{i}|_{X})^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}},

\displaystyle\quad P_{1}

\displaystyle\equiv(P_{V}^{m^{\prime}})\times_{X^{m^{\prime}}}P_{((V_{i}|_{X})^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}}=P_{((p_{i}^{*}V)_{i\in\{1,\cdots,m^{\prime}\}},(V_{i}|_{X})^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}}=P_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}}

be two different compactifications of
V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in I}(V_{i}|_{X})^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}
.
Suppose

(5.156)

\displaystyle\pi_{j}:P_{j}\to X^{m^{\prime}},\quad j=1,2,

\displaystyle p:P_{1}\to(P_{V})^{m^{\prime}},

\displaystyle P:P_{1}\to P_{((V_{i}|_{X})^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}}

are the natural projection maps.

Let
C_{0}
and
C_{1}
be the closures of

(5.157)

\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}(V_{m+D!,d},(\underline{C}^{i}_{m+D!,d})_{i\in I})

(Definition
4.13
) inside
\operatorname{ev}^{*}P_{0}
and
\operatorname{ev}^{*}P_{1}

respectively.
Since
E-\pi_{P_{V}}^{*}D-\infty_{V}
is nef,
we get that

(5.158)

p^{*}E_{\sum m^{\prime}}+P^{*}\infty_{(V_{i}|_{X})_{i\in I}}-(\pi_{1}^{*}D_{\sum m^{\prime}}+\infty_{(p_{i}^{*}V)_{i\in\{1,\cdots,m^{\prime}\}},((V_{i}|_{X})^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}})

(5.159)

=p^{*}\left((E-\pi_{P_{V}}^{*}D-\infty_{V})_{\sum m^{\prime}}\right)

is nef (see Definition
4.7
).
Combining this with the fact that
E-\sum_{i\in I}E^{\prime}_{i}
is nef we get

(5.160)

E\textnormal{-vol}(C^{i}_{m,\delta})_{i\in I}\overset{\textnormal{Lemma \ref{lemmaineqaulityrestriction}}}{\geqslant}E\textnormal{-vol}(C^{i}_{m,\delta}|_{V})_{i\in I}

(5.161)

\overset{\textnormal{Definition }\ref{defnvolumeformultiplecones}}{=}\frac{1}{m!(E\cdot\delta)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}((C^{i}_{m+D!,d}|_{V})_{i\in I})}]}\exp(E_{\sum m^{\prime},(V_{i})_{i\in I}})

(5.162)

=\frac{1}{m!(E\cdot\delta)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[C_{1}]}\exp(p^{*}E_{\sum m^{\prime}}+\infty_{(\pi_{V}^{*}(V_{i}|_{X}))_{i\in I}})

(5.163)

\geqslant\frac{1}{m!(E\cdot\delta)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[C_{1}]}\exp(\pi_{1}^{*}D_{\sum m^{\prime}}+\infty_{(p_{i}^{*}V)_{i\in\{1,\cdots,m^{\prime}\}},((V_{i}|_{X})^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}})

(5.164)

\geqslant\frac{1}{m!(D\cdot d)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[C_{1}]}\exp(\pi_{1}^{*}D_{\sum m^{\prime}}+\infty_{(p_{i}^{*}V)_{i\in\{1,\cdots,m^{\prime}\}},((V_{i}|_{X})^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}}).

By Lemma
5.19
, we have (
5.164
) is greater than or equal to

(5.165)

\frac{1}{m!(D\cdot d)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[C_{0}]}\exp(\pi_{0}^{*}D_{\sum m^{\prime}}+\infty_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}},((V_{i}|_{X})^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}})=D\textnormal{-vol}(\underline{C}^{i}_{m,d})_{i\in I\sqcup\{V\}}.

∎

5.4.
From Many Cones to One Cone

The aim of this section is to bound the
D
-volume of a fiber product of cones. This is because in Section
7.3
we have many cones that need to be consolidated into one cone in order to give an upper bound for a Gromov-Witten count.
We fix a smooth projective variety
X
.

Proposition 5.22
.

Let
(V_{i})_{i\in I}
be a finite collection of vector bundles over
X
.
Let
C^{i}_{\bullet}
be a system of cones for
V_{i}
(Definition
4.4
) for each
i\in I
.
Suppose that there is a divisor
D^{\prime}_{i}
in
X
so that
\mathcal{O}(D^{\prime}_{i})
and
V_{i}^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(D^{\prime}_{i})
are globally generated for each
i\in I
.
Let
D
be a divisor in
X

so that
D-\sum_{i\in I}D^{\prime}_{i}
is nef.
Then for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
,

(5.166)

D\textnormal{-vol}((C^{i}_{m,d})_{i\in I})\geqslant D\textnormal{-vol}\left(\sideset{}{{}_{X_{m,d}}}{\prod}_{i\in I}C^{i}_{m,d}\right).

Proof.

Let
C_{0}
be the closure of
\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}((C^{i}_{m,d})_{i\in I})
inside
\operatorname{ev}^{*}P_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}}

and
C_{1}
its closure in

(5.167)

\operatorname{ev}^{*}P_{\bigoplus_{i\in I}V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}=\operatorname{ev}^{*}P_{(\oplus_{i\in I}V_{i})^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}.

We have

(5.168)

D\textnormal{-vol}((V_{i})_{i\in I})=\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[C_{0}]}\exp(D_{\sum m^{\prime},(V_{i})_{i\in I}})

(5.169)

\overset{\textnormal{Lemma \ref{lemmmasmoothingsums}}}{\geqslant}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[C_{1}]}\exp(D_{\sum m^{\prime},\bigoplus_{i\in I}V_{i}})=D\textnormal{-vol}\left(\sideset{}{{}_{X_{m,d}}}{\prod}_{i\in I}C^{i}_{m,d}\right).

∎

6.
Bounds for Normal Cones.

In this section we wish to bound the
D
-volume of the normal cone of a moduli space of curves inside a larger one. We will need this in the proof of the main theorem in Section
9
since normal cones are needed when computing the Virtual fundamental class (See Section
7.1
).

6.1.
Divisors Compatible with Blowups

Normal cones are connected with blowups, so we need a blowup compatibility condition for divisors.
Let
X
be a smooth projective subvariety of a smooth projective variety
Y
and let
\iota_{X}:X\hookrightarrow Y
be the corresponding inclusion map.
We let
\pi:V\to X
be the normal bundle of
X
inside
Y
.

Definition 6.1
.

A divisor
D
on
Y
is
\mathrm{Bl}_{X}Y
-compatible

if
\mathrm{Bl}^{*}D-E
is nef on the blowup
\mathrm{Bl}_{X}Y
of
Y
along
X
where
\mathrm{Bl}:\mathrm{Bl}_{X}Y\to Y
is the blowdown map.

We let
z
be the coordinate on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
after fixing an identification
\mathbb{P}^{1}=\mathbb{C}\cup\{\infty\}
.

Lemma 6.2
.

If
D
is a
\mathrm{Bl}_{X}Y
compatible nef divisor
then
p_{Y}^{*}(D)+p_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{*}\{\infty\}
is
\mathrm{Bl}_{0\times X}(\mathbb{P}^{1}\times Y)
-compatible
where
p_{Y}:\mathbb{P}^{1}\times Y\to Y
and
p_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}:\mathbb{P}^{1}\times Y\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
are the natural projection maps.

Proof.

We have a
\mathbb{C}^{*}
action on
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times Y
given by sending
(z,y)\in\mathbb{P}^{1}\times Y
to
(\xi z,y)
for each
\xi\in\mathbb{C}^{*}
.
This lifts uniquely to a
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-action on
\overline{\chi}:=\mathrm{Bl}_{0\times X}(\mathbb{P}^{1}\times Y)
.
Let
E^{\prime}
be the exceptional divisor of

\overline{\chi}
and
E
the exceptional divisor of
\mathrm{Bl}_{X}Y
.
Let
\mathrm{Bl}:\overline{\chi}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}\times X
be the blowdown map.

We need to show that
D^{\prime}:=\mathrm{Bl}^{*}(p_{Y}^{*}D+p_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{*}\{\infty\})-E^{\prime}
is nef.
By Lemma
5.18
it is sufficient to check that this divisor is non-negative on
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-equivariant proper irreducible curves.
If such a curve
C
is contained
\mathrm{Bl}_{X}Y=\mathrm{Bl}_{0\times X}(0\times Y)\subset\mathrm{Bl}_{0\times X}(\mathbb{P}^{1}\times Y)
, then
D^{\prime}\cdot C=(\mathrm{Bl}_{X}^{*}D-E)\cdot C\geqslant 0
.
If it is a different curve then it is either

(1)

contained in
\{\infty\}\times Y
or

(2)

is in a fiber of
\mathrm{Bl}
or

(3)

is the proper transform of
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\{y\}
for some
y\in Y
.

In case (
1
)
D^{\prime}\cdot C\geqslant 0
since
D
is nef. In case (
2
)
D^{\prime}\cdot C\geqslant 0
holds due to the fact that the dual of the normal bundle of
E^{\prime}
is
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}(V\oplus\mathbb{C})}(1)
.
Also, in case (
3
)
D^{\prime}\cdot C\geqslant 0
since the curve intersects
E^{\prime}
at most once, but it intersects
\mathrm{Bl}^{*}(p_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{*}\{\infty\})
exactly once and has intersection number zero with
\mathrm{Bl}^{*}(p_{Y}^{*}D)
.
Hence,
D^{\prime}
is nef.
∎

The following lemma gives a sufficient condition for
D
to be
\mathrm{Bl}_{X}Y
-compatible. We will use this in Section
9
.

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
D
be a divisor in
Y
so that
\mathcal{O}(D)
and
\mathcal{O}(D)\otimes\mathfrak{I}_{X}
are globally generated where
\mathfrak{I}_{X}
is the ideal sheaf of
X
in
Y
.
Then
D
is
\mathrm{Bl}_{X}Y
-compatible.
Also,
\iota_{X}^{*}\mathcal{O}(D)\otimes V^{*}
is globally generated.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{O}(D)
is globally generated, we can perturb
D
so that its support does not contain any irreducible component of
X
, allowing us to define
D|_{X}:=\iota_{X}^{*}D
.
We have an exact sequence:

(6.1)

\mathcal{O}_{Y}^{a}(-D)\to\mathfrak{I}_{X}\to 0

for some
a\in\mathbb{N}

since
\mathcal{O}(D)\otimes\mathfrak{I}_{X}
is globally generated.
Since taking pullbacks is right exact and since
\iota_{X}^{*}\mathfrak{I}_{X}=V^{*}
, we get an exact sequence

(6.2)

\mathcal{O}_{X}(-D|_{X})\to V^{*}\to 0.

Hence,
V^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(D|_{X})=\iota_{X}^{*}\mathcal{O}(D)\otimes V^{*}
is globally generated.

Let
E
be the exceptional divisor of the blowdown map
\mathrm{Bl}:\mathrm{Bl}_{X}Y\to Y
.
Let
C
be a proper irreducible curve in
\mathrm{Bl}_{X}Y
.
Suppose that
C
is not contained in
E
and let
p\in C-E
.
Then since
\mathcal{O}(D)\otimes\mathfrak{I}_{X}
is globally generated, there exists an element
s
of
H^{0}(\mathcal{O}(D))
which vanishes along
X
and which is non-zero at
p
.
Since
\mathrm{Bl}^{*}s
vanishes along
E
, we get that
\mathrm{Bl}^{*}s
corresponds to a section
\widetilde{s}
of
\mathcal{O}(\mathrm{Bl}^{*}D-E)
which is non-zero at
p
.
Then
(\mathrm{Bl}^{*}D-E)\cdot C=\widetilde{s}^{-1}(0)\cdot C\geqslant 0
.

Now suppose that
C
is contained in
E\cong\mathbb{P}(V)
.
The restriction of
\mathcal{O}(\mathrm{Bl}^{*}D-E)
to
E\cong\mathbb{P}(V)

is
F:=q^{*}(\mathcal{O}(D|_{X}))\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}(V)}(1)
where
q:\mathbb{P}(V)\to X
is the natural projection map.
By Lemma
4.6
combined with the fact that
V^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(D|_{X})
is globally generated we get that
F
is nef and hence its first Chern class pairs non-negatively with
C
.
∎

6.2.
Bounds for Normal Cones

In this section we give bounds for the
D
-volume of the normal cone of a moduli space in a larger one.
Let
X\subset Y
be a smooth projective subvariety of a smooth projective variety and
\iota_{X}:X\hookrightarrow Y
the natural inclusion map.
Let
\mathfrak{I}_{X}
be the ideal sheaf defining
X
in
Y
.
Let
\pi:V\to X
be the normal bundle of
X
.
We let
C_{m,d}
be the normal cone of
X_{m,d}
inside
Y_{m,(\iota_{X})_{*}d}
for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
.
We have the following commutative diagram:

(6.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Y_{m,(\iota_{X})_{*}d}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Y_{m+1,(\iota_{X})_{*}d}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{m,d}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{m+1,d}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{m+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{ev}_{m+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{m+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{ev}_{m+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{X}}

which induces the following diagram of normal cones:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi_{m+1}^{*}C_{m,d}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C_{m+1,d}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C_{m,d}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{ev}_{m+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{m+1}}

for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
.
This represents a flat family of stable genus zero curves parameterized by
C_{m,d}
mapping to
V

since the corresponding curves projected to
X
are stable.
So, by the universal property of moduli spaces, we get an induced map

(6.4)

\iota_{C}:C_{m,d}\to V_{m,d}

for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
.

Definition 6.4
.

The
system of normal cones

(6.5)

C^{X/Y}_{\bullet}=(C^{X/Y}_{m,d})_{m\in\mathbb{N},\ d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)}

for
X/Y
is defined as follows:
We define
C^{X/Y}_{m,d}\subset V_{m,d}

to be the image of
\iota_{C}
inside
V_{m,d}
for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
.

Proposition 6.5
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
.
Let
U\subset X
be an open subset.
Suppose
(V_{i})_{i\in I}
is a finite collection of vector bundles over
Y
. Let
C^{i}_{\bullet}
be a system of cones for
V_{i}
for each
i\in I
.
Let
D\subset Y
be a smooth divisor so that

(1)

D-\sum_{i\in I}D^{\prime}_{i}

and
(D-\sum_{i\in I}D^{\prime}_{i})\otimes\mathfrak{I}_{X}

are globally generated
where
D_{i}
is a divisor in
Y
so that
\mathcal{O}(D^{\prime}_{i})
and
V_{i}^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(D^{\prime}_{i})
are globally generated
for each
i\in I
,

(2)

D
is generically transverse to
C^{i}_{\bullet}
for each
i\in I
,

(3)

D
is transverse to
X
,

(4)

D|_{X}:=D\cap X
is generically transverse to
C^{X/Y}_{\bullet}
and
C^{i}_{\bullet}|_{X}
for each
i\in I
.

Then for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
,

(6.6)

D\textnormal{-vol}(C^{i}_{m,(\iota_{X})_{*}d})_{i\in I}\geqslant(D|_{X})\textnormal{-vol}(\check{C}^{i}_{m,d})_{i\in I\sqcup\{X/Y\}}

where

\check{C}^{i}_{\bullet}=C^{i}_{\bullet}|_{U}
for each
i\in I
and
\check{C}^{X/Y}_{\bullet}=C^{X/Y}_{\bullet}|_{U}
.

Proof.

We will prove this proposition by a deformation to the normal cone argument, essentially comparing integrals associated with the central fiber and a generic one.
By Lemma
5.15
, we can assume that
C^{i}_{\bullet}=C^{i}_{\bullet}|_{\widetilde{U}}
for each
i\in I
where
\widetilde{U}\subset Y
is an open subset satisfying
\widetilde{U}\cap X=U
.
Define
\overline{\chi}:=\mathrm{Bl}_{0\times X}(\mathbb{P}^{1}\times Y)
and let
\pi_{\overline{\chi}}:\overline{\chi}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
be the composition of the blowdown map with the natural projection map
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times Y\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
.
Let
\chi\subset\overline{\chi}
be the complement of the proper transform of
0\times Y

and
\pi_{\chi}:=\pi_{\overline{\chi}}|_{\chi}
.
Then
\pi_{\chi}
is the deformation to the normal cone of
X
. Its central fiber is the normal bundle
V
of
X
in
Y
.
Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
,
m^{\prime}:=m+(D|_{X})\cdot d
. and
\delta=(\iota_{X})_{*}d
.
We let

(6.7)

\Pi_{\Delta}:\Delta_{m+D!,d}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}

be the deformation to the normal cone of
X_{m+(D|_{X})!,d}
inside
Y_{m+D!,\delta}
.
Similarly, let

(6.8)

\Pi^{\prime}_{\Delta}:\Delta_{m+1+D!,d}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}

be the deformation to the normal cone of
X_{m+1+(D|_{X})!,d}
inside
Y_{m+1+D!,\delta}
.
The diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Y_{m+D!,\delta}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Y_{m+1+D!,\delta}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{m+(D|_{X})!,d}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{m+1+(D|_{X})!,d}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{m^{\prime}+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{ev}_{m^{\prime}+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{m^{\prime}+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{ev}_{m^{\prime}+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{X}}

induces a diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Delta_{0,h+1+D!,d}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chi}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Delta_{0,h+D!,d}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{ev}_{h+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{h+1}}

which is a flat family of curves mapping to
\chi
.
Hence, we have an induced map:

(6.9)

\iota_{\Delta}:\Delta_{m+D!,d}\to\chi_{m^{\prime},\delta^{\prime}}

where
\delta^{\prime}
is the image of
d
in
\chi
under pushforward by the natural inclusion map

(6.10)

X=0\times X\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}\times X\hookrightarrow\chi

where
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times X\subset\mathbb{P}^{1}\times Y
is the proper transform of
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times X
in
\chi
.

Let
\delta^{\prime\prime}
be the image of
\delta^{\prime}
under the natural inclusion map
\chi\hookrightarrow\overline{\chi}
. Let
i:\chi_{m^{\prime},\delta^{\prime}}\hookrightarrow\overline{\chi}_{m^{\prime},\delta^{\prime\prime}}
be the natural inclusion map
and define

(6.11)

\overline{\iota}_{\Delta}:=i\circ\iota_{\Delta}.

This fits into a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Delta_{m+D!,d}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\overline{\chi}_{m^{\prime},\delta^{\prime\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\overline{\chi}^{m^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Y_{m+D!,\delta}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Y^{m^{\prime}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\iota}_{\Delta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{Y_{m+D!,\delta}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{ev}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(p_{Y}\circ\mathrm{Bl})^{m^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{ev}}

where
\mathrm{Bl}:\overline{\chi}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}\times Y
is the blowdown map and
p_{Y}:\mathbb{P}^{1}\times Y\to Y
is the natural projection map and

F_{Y_{m+D!,\delta}}

is the natural projection map coming from the deformation to the normal cone construction.

Now let
\widetilde{V}_{i}
be the pullback of
V_{i}
to
\overline{\chi}

via the map
\overline{\chi}\overset{\mathrm{Bl}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{P}^{1}\times Y\overset{p_{Y}}{\longrightarrow}Y
for each
i\in I
.
We have the following evaluation maps

(6.12)

\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}_{\widetilde{V}_{i}}:F_{Y_{m+D!,\delta}}^{*}((V_{i})_{m,\delta})\to\operatorname{ev}^{*}\widetilde{V}_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}},\quad i\in I

covering
\overline{\iota}_{\Delta}

and hence an evaluation map:

(6.13)

\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}:=\bigoplus_{i\in I}\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}_{\widetilde{V}_{i}}:\oplus_{i\in I}F_{Y_{m+D!,\delta}}^{*}((V_{i})_{m,\delta})\to\oplus_{i\in I}\operatorname{ev}^{*}\widetilde{V}_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}.

Let
\widetilde{C}^{i}_{m+D!,\delta}\subset F_{Y_{m+D!,d}}^{*}((V_{i})_{m,\delta})

be the pullback of the subcone
C^{i}_{m+D!,\delta}\subset(V_{i})_{m+D!,\delta}
for each
i\in I
.
Define
\overline{C}
to be the closure inside
\operatorname{ev}^{*}P_{(\widetilde{V}_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}}
of

(6.14)

\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}\left(\sideset{}{{}_{\Delta_{m+D!,\delta}}}{\prod}_{i\in I}\widetilde{C}^{i}_{m+D!,\delta}\right).

Let
\overline{C}_{0}
be the closure of

(6.15)

\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}((\check{C}^{i}_{m+(D|_{X})!,d})_{i\in I\sqcup\{X/Y\}})

inside
\operatorname{ev}^{*}P_{((V_{i}|_{X})^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I\sqcup\{X/Y\}}}
where we define
V_{X/Y}|_{X}:=V
.
Let

(6.16)

\nu:P_{(\widetilde{V}_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}}\to\overline{\chi}^{m^{\prime}},\quad\mu:P_{((V_{i}|_{X})^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I\sqcup\{X/Y\}}}\to P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}

be the natural projection maps.
The composition

(6.17)

\overline{C}\to\operatorname{ev}^{*}P_{(\widetilde{V}_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}}\overset{P\operatorname{ev}}{\longrightarrow}P_{(\widetilde{V}_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}}\overset{\nu}{\longrightarrow}\overline{\chi}^{m^{\prime}}\overset{(\pi_{\overline{\chi}})^{m^{\prime}}}{\longrightarrow}(\mathbb{P}^{1})^{m^{\prime}},

where the first map is the natural inclusion map,
factors through the small diagonal
\mathbb{P}^{1}\subset(\mathbb{P}^{1})^{m^{\prime}}
because curves in
\overline{\chi}_{m^{\prime},\delta^{\prime\prime}}
become constant curves in
\mathbb{P}^{1}
after composition with
\pi_{\overline{\chi}}

and so let

(6.18)

\widetilde{\Pi}:\overline{C}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}

be the corresponding map.
Let
\widetilde{D}
be the proper transform of
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times D
inside
\overline{\chi}
.
Then

(6.19)

\mathrm{Bl}^{*}D\cong\widetilde{D},

where
\mathrm{Bl}:\overline{\chi}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}\times Y
is the blowdown map
since
D
is transverse to
X
.
Also,

(6.20)

(\mathrm{Bl}^{*}D-E^{\prime})|_{E^{\prime}}\cong\pi_{P_{V}}^{*}(D|_{X})+\infty_{V}

via the identification
E^{\prime}\cong P_{V}
where
E^{\prime}
is the exceptional divisor of
\mathrm{Bl}
.
Combining this with (
6.19
), we get

(6.21)

(\widetilde{D}-E^{\prime})|_{E^{\prime}}\cong\pi_{P_{V}}^{*}(D|_{X})+\infty_{V}.

Since
V\cong\pi_{\chi}^{-1}(0)\subset\overline{\chi}
we have a natural subspace
V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}\subset\overline{\chi}^{m^{\prime}}
.
We also have a natural subspace
V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}\subset P_{V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}
.
We also have natural identifications:

(6.22)

\nu^{-1}(V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})\cong\mu^{-1}(V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})\cong\sideset{}{{}_{X}}{\prod}_{i\in I}P_{(V_{i}|_{X})^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}}\times_{X^{m^{\prime}}}V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}.

Under these identifications, we have

(6.23)

P\operatorname{ev}^{-1}\nu^{-1}(V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})\cap\overline{C}=P\operatorname{ev}^{-1}\mu^{-1}(V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})\cap\overline{C}_{0}.

Also,
P\operatorname{ev}^{-1}\nu^{-1}((E^{\prime})^{m^{\prime}})\cap\overline{C}
is a codimension
0
substack of
\widetilde{\Pi}^{-1}(0)

because all curves in
\overline{\chi}_{m^{\prime},\delta^{\prime\prime}}

with image intersecting
V\subset E^{\prime}
are contained in
E^{\prime}

and so

(6.24)

[\widetilde{\Pi}^{-1}(0)]=[P\operatorname{ev}^{-1}\nu^{-1}((E^{\prime})^{m^{\prime}})\cap\overline{C}]+C^{\prime}

inside
CH_{*}(\operatorname{ev}^{*}P_{(\widetilde{V}_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}})
where
C^{\prime}
is a positive sum of integral closed substacks of
\operatorname{ev}^{*}P_{(\widetilde{V}_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}}
.
We have a natural identification:

(6.25)

P_{(p_{i}^{*}V)_{i\in\{1,\cdots,m^{\prime}\}},(V_{i}|_{X})_{i\in I}}\cong\nu^{-1}((E^{\prime})^{m^{\prime}})

where
p_{i}:X^{m^{\prime}}\to X
is the
i
th projection map for each
i=1,\cdots,m^{\prime}
.
By Equation (
6.21
),
we get

(6.26)

(D|_{X})_{\sum m^{\prime},(p_{i}^{*}V)_{i=1}^{m^{\prime}},(V_{i}|_{X})_{i\in I}}\cong(\widetilde{D}_{\sum m^{\prime},(\widetilde{V}_{i})_{i\in I}}-\pi_{P_{(\widetilde{V}_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}}}^{*}(E^{\prime})_{\sum m^{\prime}})|_{\nu^{-1}((E^{\prime})^{m^{\prime}})}

under the identification (
6.25
).
We have

(6.27)

(D|_{X})\textnormal{-vol}(\check{C}^{i}_{m,\delta})_{i\in I\sqcup\{X/Y\}}=\frac{1}{m!(D|_{X}\cdot d)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\overline{C}_{0}]}\exp((D|_{X})_{\sum m^{\prime},(V_{i}|_{X})_{i\in I\sqcup\{X/Y\}}})

(6.28)

=\frac{1}{m!(D\cdot\delta)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}\overline{P\operatorname{ev}^{-1}\mu^{-1}(V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})\cap\overline{C}_{0}}}\exp((D|_{X})_{\sum m^{\prime},(V_{i}|_{X})_{i\in I\sqcup\{X/Y\}}})

(6.29)

\overset{\textnormal{\eqref{eqnintersectionofC0withC1}}}{=}\frac{1}{m!(D\cdot\delta)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}\overline{P\operatorname{ev}^{-1}\nu^{-1}(V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})\cap\overline{C}}}\exp((D|_{X})_{\sum m^{\prime},(V_{i}|_{X})_{i\in I\sqcup\{X/Y\}}})

where
\overline{P\operatorname{ev}^{-1}\mu^{-1}(V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})\cap\overline{C}_{0}}
and
\overline{P\operatorname{ev}^{-1}\nu^{-1}(V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})\cap\overline{C}}
is the closure of
P\operatorname{ev}^{-1}\mu^{-1}(V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})\cap\overline{C}_{0}
and
P\operatorname{ev}^{-1}\nu^{-1}(V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})\cap\overline{C}
respectively inside
\operatorname{ev}^{*}P_{((V_{i}|_{X})^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I\sqcup\{X/Y\}}}
.

By
Lemma
5.19

applied to the intersection of

\overline{P\operatorname{ev}^{-1}\nu^{-1}(V^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})\cap\overline{C}}

with

(6.30)

\operatorname{ev}^{*}\oplus_{i=1}^{m^{\prime}}p_{i}^{*}V\bigoplus\oplus_{i\in I}(V_{i}|_{X})^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}}

compactifying to

\operatorname{ev}^{*}P_{((V_{i}|_{X})^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I\sqcup\{X/Y\}}}

and the pullback via
\operatorname{ev}
of
(
6.25
) respectively
we get
(
6.29
) is less than or equal to

(6.31)

\frac{1}{m!(D\cdot\delta)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[P\operatorname{ev}^{-1}\nu^{-1}({E^{\prime}}^{m^{\prime}})\cap\overline{C}]}\exp((D|_{X})_{\sum m^{\prime},(p_{i}^{*}V)_{i\in\{1,\cdots,m^{\prime}\}},(V_{i}|_{X})_{i\in I}})

(6.32)

\overset{\textnormal{\eqref{eqnproductrestrictioneqn}}}{=}\frac{1}{m!(D\cdot\delta)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[P\operatorname{ev}^{-1}\nu^{-1}({E^{\prime}}^{m^{\prime}})\cap\overline{C}]}\exp(\widetilde{D}_{\sum m^{\prime},(\widetilde{V}_{i})_{i\in I}}-\pi_{P_{(\widetilde{V}_{i})_{i\in I}}}^{*}(E^{\prime})_{\sum m^{\prime}}).

Let
\widetilde{D^{\prime}_{i}}
be
the pullback of
D^{\prime}_{i}
via the projection map
\overline{\chi}\to Y
for each
i\in I
.
Let

(6.33)

P:P_{(\widetilde{V}_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}}\to P_{(V_{i}^{\boxtimes m^{\prime}})_{i\in I}}

be the natural projection map.
We have by Corollary
4.11
that
(\sum_{i\in I}D^{\prime}_{i})_{\sum m^{\prime},(V_{i})_{i\in I}}
is nef.
Also, by Lemmas
6.2
and
6.3
and Equation (
6.19
), we have that
\widetilde{D}-\sum_{i\in I}\widetilde{D}^{\prime}_{i}-E^{\prime}
is nef for each
i\in I
.
Therefore,

(6.34)

\widetilde{D}_{\sum m^{\prime},(\widetilde{V}_{i})_{i\in I}}-\pi_{P_{(\widetilde{V}_{i})_{i\in I}}}^{*}(E^{\prime})_{\sum m^{\prime}}=P^{*}((\sum_{i\in I}D_{i})_{\sum m^{\prime},(V_{i})_{i\in I}})+\pi_{P_{(\widetilde{V}_{i})_{i\in I}}}^{*}((\widetilde{D}-\sum_{i\in I}\widetilde{D}^{\prime}_{i}-E^{\prime})_{\sum m^{\prime}})

is nef.
Combining this fact with Equation (
6.24
) gives us that (
6.32
)
is less than or equal to:

(6.35)

\frac{1}{m!(D\cdot\delta)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\widetilde{\Pi}^{-1}(0)]}\exp(\widetilde{D}_{\sum m^{\prime},(\widetilde{V}_{i})_{i\in I}}-\pi_{P_{(\widetilde{V}_{i})_{i\in I}}}^{*}(E^{\prime})_{\sum m^{\prime}})

(6.36)

=\frac{1}{m!(D\cdot\delta)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\widetilde{\Pi}^{-1}(1)]}\exp(\widetilde{D}_{\sum m^{\prime},(\widetilde{V}_{i})_{i\in I}}-\pi_{P_{(\widetilde{V}_{i})_{i\in I}}}^{*}(E^{\prime})_{\sum m^{\prime}})

(6.37)

=\frac{1}{m!(D\cdot\delta)!}\int_{P\operatorname{ev}_{*}[\widetilde{\Pi}^{-1}(1)]}\exp(\widetilde{D}_{\sum m^{\prime},(\widetilde{V}_{i})_{i\in I}})=D\textnormal{-vol}(C^{i}_{m,d})_{i\in I}.

∎

7.
Connection with Virtual Fundamental Class

7.1.
Siebert’s description of the Virtual Fundamental Class

In this section we give Siebert’s description of the virtual fundamental class of the moduli space of stable genus zero curves in terms of the normal cone from
[
Sie04
]
.
Let
\iota_{X}:X\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{N}
be a closed immersion from a smooth projective variety
X
and
V\to X
the normal bundle of this closed immersion.
We define
\mathfrak{M}_{m}
to be the moduli stack of genus zero pre-stable curves with
m
marked points with the fppf topology.

We have a perfect obstruction theory

(7.1)

((R\pi_{m+1})_{*}\operatorname{ev}_{m+1}^{*}(T_{X}))^{\vee}\to L^{\bullet}_{X_{m,d}/\mathfrak{M}_{m}}

defining the virtual fundamental class on
X_{m,d}

where
L^{\bullet}_{X_{m,d}/\mathfrak{M}_{m}}
is the cotangent complex
(See
[
BF97
, Proposition 6.2]
and
[
Beh97
]
).
We can truncate these complexes giving a map:

(7.2)

((R\pi_{m+1})_{*}\operatorname{ev}_{m+1}^{*}(T_{X}))^{\vee}\to\tau_{\geqslant-1}L^{\bullet}_{X_{m,d}/\mathfrak{M}_{m}}.

By
[
Ill06
, Chapitre III, Corollaire 3.1.3]
,
this corresponds to a commutative diagram:

(7.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{((\pi_{m+1})_{*}\operatorname{ev}_{m+1}^{*}{\mathcal{N}^{*}}^{\vee}_{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}})^{\vee}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathcal{N}^{*}}_{X_{m,d}/\mathbb{P}^{N}_{m,\delta}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{((\pi_{m+1})_{*}\operatorname{ev}_{m+1}^{*}(\Omega^{\vee}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}|_{X}))^{\vee}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Omega_{\mathbb{P}^{N}_{m,\delta}/\mathfrak{M}_{m}}|_{X_{m,d}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

where
{\mathcal{N}}^{*}_{A/B}
is the conormal sheaf of
A
in
B
for any closed substack
A\subset B
.
The bottom map in this diagram is an isomorphism since
T\mathbb{P}^{N}
is convex.
The top map in (
7.3
) is surjective since it comes from a perfect obstruction theory.
The following proposition allows us to compute the virtual fundamental classes from the data above.
The top map in Equation (
7.3
) gives us a morphism of cones

(7.4)

C_{m,d}\to V_{m,d}

where
C_{m,d}
is the normal cone of
X_{m,d}
inside
\mathbb{P}^{N}_{m,\delta}
.
(See
[
Sie04
, Section 3]
).

Proposition 7.1
.

(see
[
Sie04
]
).
Let

(7.5)

[]^{s(C_{\bullet})\cap c(V_{\bullet})}:=([X_{m,d}]^{s(C_{\bullet})\cap c(V_{\bullet})})_{m\in\mathbb{N},\ d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)}

where

(7.6)

[X_{m,d}]^{s(C_{\bullet})\cap c(V_{\bullet})}:=s(C_{m,d})\cap c(V_{m,d}),\quad\forall\ m\in\mathbb{N},\ d\in H_{2}^{+}(X).

Then

(7.7)

[X_{m,d}]^{\operatorname{vir}}=\{[X_{m,d}]^{s(C_{\bullet})\cap c(V_{\bullet})}\}_{\dim=c_{1}(TX)(d)+n-3+m},\ \forall\ m\in\mathbb{N},\ d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)

where
c
is the total Chern class and
\{-\}_{\dim=h}
means that we only consider the dimension
h
component of such a class for any
h\in\mathbb{N}
.

7.2.
Embeddings of Normal Cones

In Section
6.2
, we gave bounds for the
D
-volume of the image of the normal cone of a moduli space of curves in another one under the evaluation map. However, we really wish to use the normal cone since Siebert’s formula describing the virtual fundamental class from Section
7.1
uses the normal cone rather than its image.
Therefore, we need to show such cones are isomorphic under certain conditions.
This is the purpose of this section.

Let
Y
be a smooth (not necessarily projective) variety and
X\subset Y
a smooth closed subvariety where
\iota_{X}:X\hookrightarrow Y
is the natural inclusion map.
Suppose that
TY
is convex.
Let
\pi:V\to X
be the normal bundle of
X
in
Y
.

Now let us fix
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
.
We wish to show
\iota_{C}
from Equation (
6.4
) is an immersion.
Before we do this we need a preliminary lemma and some notation.
Let
\delta=(\iota_{X})_{*}d
.
We let
\mathcal{I}_{X}
be the ideal sheaf defining
X
in
Y
.
We let
\iota_{m,d}:X_{m,d}\hookrightarrow Y_{m,\delta}

and
\iota_{m+1,d}:X_{m+1,d}\hookrightarrow Y_{m+1,\delta}

be the natural inclusion maps.
Let
A:=\iota_{m+1,d}^{*}\operatorname{ev}_{m+1}^{*}\mathcal{I}_{X}\in\operatorname{Coh}(X_{m+1,d})
.

Lemma 7.2
.

Consider the two functors:

(7.8)

F_{1}:\operatorname{Coh}(X_{m,d})\to\mathbb{C}\textnormal{-Vect},\quad F_{1}(B)=\mathrm{Hom}(A,\pi_{m+1}^{*}B)

(7.9)

\displaystyle F_{2}:\operatorname{Coh}(X_{m,d})\to\mathbb{C}\textnormal{-Vect},\quad F_{2}(B)=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}(((\pi_{m+1})_{*}A^{\vee})^{\vee},B)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}((\pi_{m+1}^{*}(\pi_{m+1})_{*}A^{\vee})^{\vee},\pi_{m+1}^{*}B).

where
\mathbb{C}\textnormal{-Vect}
is the category of
\mathbb{C}
vector spaces.
Then composing with the natural map

(7.10)

A\to A^{\vee\vee}\to(\pi_{m+1}^{*}(\pi_{m+1})_{*}(A^{\vee}))^{\vee}

induces a natural isomorphism
F_{2}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}F_{1}
.

Note that the second equality in (
7.9
) holds since
(\pi_{m+1})_{*}A^{\vee}
is locally free by Lemma
4.17

combined with the fact that the map
B\to(\pi_{m+1})_{*}\pi_{m+1}^{*}B
is an isomorphism by
[
Sta18
, 0E0L, 07US]
.

Proof.

Instead of working with the category of coherent sheaves, we will work with the category of Abelian cones (See
[
BF97
, Section 1]
,
[
EGA61
, Section 1.7]
).
In other words, for any stack
Z
, the category
\operatorname{AbC}(Z)
whose objects are stacks over
Z
of the form
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{O}_{Z}}\mathcal{F})

where
\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{O}_{Z}}\mathcal{F}
is the free symmetric algebra generated by a coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}
and where morphisms between such stacks are the ones induced by morphism between sheaves (or, equivalently, morphisms respecting the linear structure on these Abelian cones).

Consider the functors:

(7.11)

F^{\prime}_{1}:\operatorname{AbC}(X_{m,d})\to\mathbb{C}\textnormal{-Vect},\quad F^{\prime}_{1}(B^{\prime})=\mathrm{Hom}_{\operatorname{AbC}(X_{m+1,d})}(\pi_{m+1}^{*}B^{\prime},\operatorname{ev}_{m+1}^{*}V),

(7.12)

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{2}:\operatorname{AbC}(X_{m,d})\to\mathbb{C}\textnormal{-Vect},\quad F^{\prime}_{2}(B^{\prime})=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\operatorname{AbC}(X_{m,d})}(B^{\prime},V_{m,d})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\operatorname{AbC}(X_{m+1,d})}(\pi_{m+1}^{*}B^{\prime},V_{m+1,d}).

Then composing each element of
F^{\prime}_{2}(B^{\prime})
with the evaluation map

(7.13)

\operatorname{ev}_{m+1}:V_{m+1,d}\to\operatorname{ev}_{m+1}^{*}V

for each
B^{\prime}\in\operatorname{AbC}(X_{m,d})

induces a natural transformation
\check{\eta}:F^{\prime}_{2}\overset{}{\longrightarrow}F^{\prime}_{1}
.

Since the map
\operatorname{Coh}(Y)\to\operatorname{AbC}(Y)
sending
\mathcal{F}
to
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathrm{Sym}_{\mathcal{O}_{Y}}\mathcal{F})
is an anti-equivalence for any stack
Y
(
[
EGA61
, Section 1.7]
), it is sufficient to prove that
\check{\eta}
is a natural isomorphism.
By definition, the following universal property is satisfied:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi_{m+1}^{*}B^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{ev}_{m+1}^{*}V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V_{m+1,d}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V_{m,d}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\forall}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exists!}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{m+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{ev}_{m+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{m+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exists!}

By examining this diagram we see that
\check{\eta}
is a natural isomorphism.
The uniqueness property ensures that
\check{\eta}(B^{\prime})
is injective, and the existence property ensures that it is surjective.
∎

Lemma 7.3
.

The map
\iota_{C}
from Equation (
6.4
) is a closed immersion.

Proof.

Let
F_{1}
,
F_{2}
be as in Lemma
7.2
above
and let
\nu:F_{2}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}F_{1}
be the natural isomorphism from the same Lemma.
Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
and
\delta=(\iota_{X})_{*}d
.
Also, let
\mathcal{I}_{X_{m,d}}
be the ideal sheaf in
\mathcal{O}_{Y_{m,\delta}}

defining
X_{m,d}
.

We let
N_{\check{m},d}
be the normal bundle of
X_{\check{m},d}
inside
Y_{\check{m},\delta}
for each
\check{m}\in\mathbb{N}
.
The commutative diagram (
6.3
) gives us the following diagram of normal bundles:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\pi_{m+1}^{*}N_{m,d}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{N_{m+1,d}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{N_{m,d}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{ev}_{m+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{m+1}}

Since this represents a flat family of curves mapping to
V

we get an induced map

(7.14)

\iota_{N}:N_{m,d}\to V_{m,d}.

The map of cones
\iota_{C}
factors as:

(7.15)

C_{m,d}\hookrightarrow N_{m,d}\overset{\iota_{N}}{\longrightarrow}V_{m,d}.

where
C_{m,d}\hookrightarrow N_{m,d}
is the natural closed immersion from the normal cone to the normal bundle.
Therefore, it is sufficient to prove that
\iota_{N}
is a closed immersion.
Equivalently, it is sufficient to show that the corresponding map of coherent sheaves:

(7.16)

((\pi_{m+1})_{*}A^{\vee})^{\vee}\to\iota_{m,d}^{*}\mathcal{I}_{X_{m,d}}

is surjective.
Now the map (
7.16
) is equal to

\nu^{-1}(\operatorname{ev}_{m+1}^{*})
where

(7.17)

\operatorname{ev}_{m+1}^{*}:A\to\iota_{m+1,d}^{*}\mathcal{I}_{X_{m+1,d}}=\pi_{m+1}^{*}\iota_{m,d}^{*}\mathcal{I}_{X_{m,d}}

is induced by the evaluation map.
Here
\operatorname{ev}_{m+1}^{*}\in F_{2}(B)
where
B=\iota_{m,d}^{*}\mathcal{I}_{X_{m,d}}
.

We let
\omega=\omega_{X_{m+1,d}/X_{m,d}}
be the relative dualizing sheaf.
Consider the sequence of natural isomorphisms in the derived category
D^{b}(\mathcal{O}_{X_{m,d}}\text{-mod})
:

(7.18)

\displaystyle H^{0}(\mathrm{Hom}(A,\pi_{m+1}^{*}(-)))\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle H^{0}(\mathrm{R}{\mathcal{H}}{\mathit{o}m}(A,\pi_{m+1}^{*}(-)))

\displaystyle\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle H^{0}(\mathrm{R}{\mathcal{H}}{\mathit{o}m}(A\otimes\omega,\pi_{m+1}^{*}(-)\otimes\omega))

\displaystyle\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle H^{0}(\mathrm{R}{\mathcal{H}}{\mathit{o}m}(A\otimes\omega,\pi_{m+1}^{!}(-)[-1]))

\displaystyle\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle H^{0}(\mathrm{R}{\mathcal{H}}{\mathit{o}m}((R\pi_{m+1})_{*}(A\otimes\omega[1]),(-)))

\displaystyle\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle H^{0}(\mathrm{R}{\mathcal{H}}{\mathit{o}m}((R\pi_{m+1})_{*}((A^{\vee})^{\vee}\otimes\omega[1]),(-)))

\displaystyle\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle H^{0}(\mathrm{R}{\mathcal{H}}{\mathit{o}m}((R\pi_{m+1})_{*}((A^{\vee})^{\vee}\otimes\pi^{!}_{m+1}\mathcal{O}_{X_{m,d}}),(-)))

\displaystyle\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle H^{0}(\mathrm{R}{\mathcal{H}}{\mathit{o}m}((R\pi_{m+1})_{*}(\mathrm{R}{\mathcal{H}}{\mathit{o}m}(A^{\vee},\pi^{!}_{m+1}\mathcal{O}_{X_{m,d}})),(-)))

\displaystyle\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle H^{0}(\mathrm{R}{\mathcal{H}}{\mathit{o}m}((R\pi_{m+1})_{*}(\mathrm{R}{\mathcal{H}}{\mathit{o}m}((R\pi_{m+1})_{*}A^{\vee},\mathcal{O}_{X_{m,d}}))),(-))

\displaystyle\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle H^{0}(\mathrm{R}{\mathcal{H}}{\mathit{o}m}((R\pi_{m+1})_{*}(A^{\vee})^{\vee},(-)))

\displaystyle\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle H^{0}(\mathrm{Hom}((\pi_{m+1})_{*}(A^{\vee})^{\vee},(-))).

We have

(7.19)

H^{0}(\mathrm{Hom}(A,\pi_{m+1}^{*}(B)))=F_{1}(B)

for each coherent sheaf
B
. Similarly,
we get

(7.20)

H^{0}(\mathrm{Hom}((\pi_{m+1})_{*}((A^{\vee}))^{\vee},B))=F_{2}(B)

for each coherent sheaf
B
.
Therefore, (
7.18
) induces a natural isomorphism
\nu^{\prime}
between
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
.
Hence,
\nu^{\prime}\circ\nu
induces an automorphism
\Phi
of
(\pi_{m+1})_{*}((A^{\vee}))^{\vee}
in
\operatorname{Coh}(X_{m,d})
by the Yoneda lemma.

The map
\nu^{\prime}(\operatorname{ev}_{m+1}^{*})
is equal to the top map in (
7.3
)
by the discussion before Proposition 6.2 in
[
BF97
]
.
Also, the top map in (
7.3
) is surjective since the diagram (
7.3
) comes from a perfect obstruction theory.
Hence,
\nu^{\prime}(\operatorname{ev}_{m+1}^{*})
is surjective.
Since
\Phi^{-1}\circ\nu^{\prime}(\operatorname{ev}_{m+1}^{*})=\nu^{-1}(\operatorname{ev}_{m+1}^{*})
and since
\Phi
comes from an automorphism of
(\pi_{m+1})_{*}((A^{\vee}))^{\vee}
,
we get that
\nu^{-1}((\operatorname{ev}_{m+1})^{*})
is also surjective.
Therefore, since (
7.16
) is equal to
\nu^{-1}((\operatorname{ev}_{m+1})^{*})
we get that
\iota_{N}
is a closed immersion.
Hence,
\iota_{C}
is a closed immersion.
∎

Corollary 7.4
.

Let
\iota_{X}:X\hookrightarrow Y
be a closed immersion from a projective variety
X
into a smooth variety
Y
with
TY
convex
(Definition
4.16
).
Let

(7.21)

C^{X/Y}_{\bullet}=(C^{X/Y}_{m,d})_{m\in\mathbb{N},\ d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)}

be a system of normal cones for
X/Y
as in Definition
6.4
.
Suppose
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
.
Also, let
C_{m,d}
be the normal cone of
X_{m,d}
inside
Y_{m,(\iota_{X})_{*}d}
.
Then
C_{m,d}\cong C^{X/Y}_{m,d}
.

7.3.
Virtual Fundamental Class in Terms of Segre Classes

In this Section we wish to compute the virtual fundamental class as well as give estimates of Gromov-Witten counts using Segre classes only and hence use the
D
-volume to bound such Gromov-Witten counts.
The other purpose of this section is to use an auxillary bundle
T
, which in the main theorem in Section
9
is easier to bound than the normal bundle
V
of a smooth projective variety in projective space.

Lemma 7.5
.

Let
\pi:B^{\prime}\to B^{\prime\prime}
be a morphism of stacks and let
W^{\prime\prime}
be a vector bundle over
B^{\prime\prime}
. Define
W^{\prime}:=\pi^{*}W^{\prime\prime}
.
Suppose that the of dimension
B^{\prime\prime}
is at most
\delta
.
Let
C
be any cone over
B^{\prime}
.
Then:

(7.22)

s(C)\cap c(W^{\prime})=\sum_{\ell=0}^{\delta}\sum_{k=0}^{\delta-\ell}(-1)^{\ell}{k+\ell\choose\ell}s(C\oplus(W^{\prime})^{\ell}).

Proof.

Let
c^{-1}(W^{\prime})
be the inverse of the total Chern class
c(W^{\prime})
of
W^{\prime}
.
We have

(7.23)

c(W^{\prime})=1/(1+(c^{-1}(W^{\prime})-1))=\sum_{k=0}^{\delta}(-1)^{k}(c^{-1}(W^{\prime})-1)^{k}

(7.24)

=\sum_{k=0}^{\delta}\sum_{\ell=0}^{k}(-1)^{k+(k-\ell)}{k\choose\ell}c^{-1}(W^{\prime})^{\ell}=\sum_{\ell=0}^{\delta}\sum_{k=\ell}^{\delta}(-1)^{\ell}{k\choose\ell}c^{-1}(W^{\prime})^{\ell}

(7.25)

=\sum_{\ell=0}^{\delta}\sum_{k=0}^{\delta-\ell}(-1)^{\ell}{k+\ell\choose\ell}c^{-1}(W^{\prime})^{\ell}=\sum_{\ell=0}^{\delta}\sum_{k=0}^{\delta-\ell}(-1)^{\ell}{k+\ell\choose\ell}c^{-1}((W^{\prime})^{\ell}).

Hence,

(7.26)

s(C)\cap c(W^{\prime})=\sum_{\ell=0}^{\delta}\sum_{k=0}^{\delta-\ell}(-1)^{\ell}{k+\ell\choose\ell}s(C)\cap c^{-1}((W^{\prime})^{\ell})

(7.27)

=\sum_{\ell=0}^{\delta}\sum_{k=0}^{\delta-\ell}(-1)^{\ell}{k+\ell\choose\ell}s(C\oplus(W^{\prime})^{\ell}).

∎

Let
\iota_{X}:X\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{N}
be the inclusion map of a smooth projective variety
X
into projective space
and let
V
be its normal bundle.
Also, let

(7.28)

C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}}_{\bullet}=(C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}}_{m,d})_{m\in\mathbb{N},\ d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)}

be a system of normal cones for
X/\mathbb{P}^{N}
as in Definition

6.4
.
Now let us suppose we have a short exact sequence of strongly convex vector bundles

(7.29)

0\to V\to T\to W\to 0

over
X
.

Lemma 7.6
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
and let
a_{1},\cdots,a_{m}\in H^{*}(X)
be cohomology classes of pure degree.
Let

(7.30)

e_{d}\geqslant(N+1)\deg(d)+N-3

and suppose that
\sum_{j=1}^{m}\deg(a_{j})=2\dim([X_{m,d}]^{\operatorname{vir}})
.
Then

(7.31)

\displaystyle\langle a_{1},\cdots,a_{m}\rangle^{X}_{m,d}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=0}^{e_{d}}\sum_{k=0}^{e_{d}-\ell}(-1)^{\ell}{k+\ell\choose\ell}\langle a_{1},\cdots,a_{m}\rangle_{m,d}^{s(C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}}_{\bullet}\oplus T^{\ell}_{\bullet}\oplus W_{\bullet})}

Proof.

Since
V,T,W
are convex, we have a short exact sequence:

(7.32)

0\to V_{m,d}\to T_{m,d}\to W_{m,d}\to 0

and so

(7.33)

s(C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}})\cap c(V_{m,d})=s(C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}}_{m,d}\oplus W_{m,d})\cap c(T_{m,d}).

Therefore, by Lemma
7.5
with

•

B^{\prime}=X_{m,d}
,
B^{\prime\prime}=X_{0,d}
,

•

\pi
the map forgetting all marked points,

•

C=C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}}_{m,d}\oplus W_{m,d}
,

•

W^{\prime}=T_{m,d}
and

•

\delta=e_{d}
(Lemma
5.2
)

combined with Proposition
7.1
and Corollary
7.4

we get that (
7.31
) holds.

∎

Lemma 7.7
.

Let
D,D^{\prime}
be divisors on
X
so that

(1)

D
is linearly equivalent to
4D^{\prime}
where
D^{\prime}
ample,

(2)

\mathcal{O}(D^{\prime})
,
V^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(D^{\prime})
,
W^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(D^{\prime})
,
T^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(D^{\prime})
are all globally generated and

(3)

D
is smooth and generically transverse to the system of normal cones
C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}}_{\bullet}
(Definitions
4.5
and
6.4
)
and is
V
,
T
and
W
-large (Definition
4.14
).

Let
\omega
be a Kähler form Poincaré dual to
D^{\prime}
.
Also, let
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
and let
\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{m}
be closed homogenous de Rham forms on
X
.
Then

(7.34)

\displaystyle\left|\langle[\alpha_{1}],\cdots,[\alpha_{m}]\rangle^{X}_{m,d}\right|\leqslant

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=0}^{r_{d}}\sum_{k=0}^{r_{d}-\ell}{k+\ell\choose\ell}(23r_{d}+2mN+18m+\rho_{d,T}+23)!(\ell\rho_{d,T})!D\textnormal{-vol}(C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}}_{m,d},T_{m,d}^{\ell},W_{m,d})\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

if
X_{m,d}
is non-empty
where

(7.35)

r_{d}:=(N+1)\deg(d)+N+c_{1}(V)(d)+c_{1}(W)(d)+\dim(V)+\dim(W)

(7.36)

\rho_{d,T}:=c_{1}(T)(d)+\dim(T).

Note that
r_{d}
and
\rho_{d,T}
are non-negative numbers since

(7.37)

c_{1}(V)(d)\geqslant 0,\quad c_{1}(W)(d)\geqslant 0,\quad c_{1}(T)(d)\geqslant 0

due to the fact that
V,W,T
are strongly convex.

Proof of Lemma
7.7
.

We can assume that
\langle[\alpha_{1}],\cdots,[\alpha_{m}]\rangle^{X}_{m,d}
is non-zero. Hence,

\sum_{j=1}^{m}\deg(\alpha_{j})=\dim{[X_{m,d}]^{\operatorname{vir}}}
.
Let
e_{d}
be as in Equation (
5.47
).
By Lemma
7.6
, we have

(7.38)

\left|\langle[\alpha_{1}],\cdots,[\alpha_{m}]\rangle^{X}_{m,d}\right|

(7.39)

\leqslant\sum_{\ell=0}^{e_{d}}\sum_{k=0}^{e_{d}-\ell}{k+\ell\choose\ell}\left|\langle[\alpha_{1}],\cdots,[\alpha_{m}]\rangle_{m,d}^{s(C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}}_{\bullet}\oplus T^{\ell}_{\bullet}\oplus W_{\bullet})}\right|.

For each vector bundle
W^{\prime}
define

(7.40)

\rho_{d,W^{\prime}}:=c_{1}(W^{\prime})(d)+\dim(W^{\prime}).

Since
D
is
V
,
T
and
W
-large, it is also
V\oplus T^{\ell}\oplus W
-large for each
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
Hence, by Proposition
5.9
, we have that (
7.39
) is bounded above by:

(7.41)

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=0}^{e_{d}}\sum_{k=0}^{e_{d}-\ell}{k+\ell\choose\ell}(9e_{d}+2mN+6m+9)!(\rho_{d,V\oplus T^{\ell}\oplus W}+1)^{4(e_{d}+m+1)}(\rho_{d,V\oplus T^{\ell}\oplus W})!

\displaystyle\times D\textnormal{-vol}(C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}}_{m,d}\oplus T_{m,d}^{\ell}\oplus W_{m,d})\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}.

By Proposition
5.22
, this is bounded above by:

(7.42)

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=0}^{e_{d}}\sum_{k=0}^{e_{d}-\ell}{k+\ell\choose\ell}(9e_{d}+2mN+6m+9)!(\rho_{d,V\oplus T^{\ell}\oplus W}+1)^{4(e_{d}+m+1)}(\rho_{d,V\oplus T^{\ell}\oplus W})!

\displaystyle\times D\textnormal{-vol}(C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}}_{m,d},T_{m,d}^{\ell},W_{m,d})\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(7.43)

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{\ell=0}^{e_{d}}\sum_{k=0}^{e_{d}-\ell}{k+\ell\choose\ell}(9e_{d}+2mN+6m+9)!(\ell\rho_{d,T}+\rho_{d,V\oplus W}+1)^{4(e_{d}+m+1)}(\ell\rho_{d,T}+\rho_{d,V\oplus W})!

\displaystyle\times D\textnormal{-vol}(C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}}_{m,d},T_{m,d}^{\ell},W_{m,d})\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(7.44)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=0}^{e_{d}}\sum_{k=0}^{e_{d}-\ell}{k+\ell\choose\ell}(9e_{d}+2mN+6m+9)!(\ell\rho_{d,T}+\rho_{d,V\oplus W}+1)^{4(e_{d}+m+1)}

\displaystyle\times{\ell\rho_{d,T}+\rho_{d,V\oplus W}\choose\rho_{d,V\oplus W}}(\rho_{d,V\oplus W})!(\ell\rho_{d,T})!D\textnormal{-vol}(C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}}_{m,d},T_{m,d}^{\ell},W_{m,d})\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(7.45)

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{\ell=0}^{e_{d}}\sum_{k=0}^{e_{d}-\ell}{k+\ell\choose\ell}(9e_{d}+2mN+6m+9)!(\ell\rho_{d,T}+\rho_{d,V\oplus W}+1)^{4(e_{d}+m+1)}

\displaystyle\times(\ell\rho_{d,T}+\rho_{d,V\oplus W})^{\rho_{d,V\oplus W}}(\rho_{d,V\oplus W})!(\ell\rho_{d,T})!D\textnormal{-vol}(C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}}_{m,d},T_{m,d}^{\ell},W_{m,d})\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(7.46)

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{\ell=0}^{e_{d}}\sum_{k=0}^{e_{d}-\ell}{k+\ell\choose\ell}(9e_{d}+2mN+6m+9)!(\ell\rho_{d,T}+\rho_{d,V\oplus W}+1)^{4(e_{d}+m+1)+\rho_{d,V\oplus W}}

\displaystyle\times\rho_{d,V\oplus W}!(\ell\rho_{d,T})!D\textnormal{-vol}(C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}}_{m,d},T_{m,d}^{\ell},W_{m,d})\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(7.47)

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{\ell=0}^{e_{d}}\sum_{k=0}^{e_{d}-\ell}{k+\ell\choose\ell}(9e_{d}+2mN+6m+9)!(\ell\rho_{d,T}+\rho_{d,V\oplus W}+1)^{4(e_{d}+m+1)+\rho_{d,V\oplus W}}

\displaystyle\times\rho_{d,V\oplus W}^{\rho_{d,V\oplus W}}(\ell\rho_{d,T})!D\textnormal{-vol}(C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}}_{m,d},T_{m,d}^{\ell},W_{m,d})\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(7.48)

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{\ell=0}^{e_{d}}\sum_{k=0}^{e_{d}-\ell}{k+\ell\choose\ell}(9e_{d}+2mN+6m+9)!(\ell\rho_{d,T}+\rho_{d,V\oplus W}+1)^{4(e_{d}+m+1)+2\rho_{d,V\oplus W}}

\displaystyle\times(\ell\rho_{d,T})!D\textnormal{-vol}(C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}}_{m,d},T_{m,d}^{\ell},W_{m,d})\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(7.49)

\displaystyle\overset{\textnormal{\eqref{eqnedeqn},\eqref{eqndefonrorho},\eqref{eqnaefaefforrd}},\eqref{eqnnonnegativity}}{\leqslant}\sum_{\ell=0}^{e_{d}}\sum_{k=0}^{e_{d}-\ell}{k+\ell\choose\ell}(9r_{d}+2mN+6m+9)!(\ell\rho_{d,T}+r_{d}+1)^{4(r_{d}+m+1)+2r_{d}}

\displaystyle\times(\ell\rho_{d,T})!D\textnormal{-vol}(C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}}_{m,d},T_{m,d}^{\ell},W_{m,d})\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(7.50)

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{\ell=0}^{e_{d}}\sum_{k=0}^{e_{d}-\ell}{k+\ell\choose\ell}(9r_{d}+2mN+6m+9)!(\ell\rho_{d,T}+r_{d}+1)^{6(r_{d}+m+1)}

\displaystyle\times(\ell\rho_{d,T})!D\textnormal{-vol}(C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}}_{m,d},T_{m,d}^{\ell},W_{m,d})\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(7.51)

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{\ell=0}^{e_{d}}\sum_{k=0}^{e_{d}-\ell}{k+\ell\choose\ell}(9r_{d}+2mN+6m+9)!((\ell+1)(\rho_{d,T}+r_{d}+1))^{6(r_{d}+m+1)}

\displaystyle\times(\ell\rho_{d,T})!D\textnormal{-vol}(C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}}_{m,d},T_{m,d}^{\ell},W_{m,d})\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(7.52)
