\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=0}^{e_{d}}\sum_{k=0}^{e_{d}-\ell}{k+\ell\choose\ell}(9r_{d}+2mN+6m+9)!(\ell+1)^{6(r_{d}+m+1)}(\rho_{d,T}+r_{d}+1)^{6(r_{d}+m+1)}

\displaystyle\times(\ell\rho_{d,T})!D\textnormal{-vol}(C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}}_{m,d},T_{m,d}^{\ell},W_{m,d})\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(7.53)

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{\ell=0}^{e_{d}}\sum_{k=0}^{e_{d}-\ell}{k+\ell\choose\ell}(9r_{d}+2mN+6m+9)!(r_{d}+1)^{6(r_{d}+m+1)}(\rho_{d,T}+r_{d}+1)^{6(r_{d}+m+1)}

\displaystyle\times(\ell\rho_{d,T})!D\textnormal{-vol}(C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}}_{m,d},T_{m,d}^{\ell},W_{m,d})\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(7.54)

\displaystyle\overset{\textnormal{\eqref{eqnreallreallyterribleinequality}}}{\leqslant}\sum_{\ell=0}^{e_{d}}\sum_{k=0}^{e_{d}-\ell}{k+\ell\choose\ell}

\displaystyle\times(9r_{d}+2mN+6m+9+r_{d}+1+6(r_{d}+m+1)+\rho_{d,T}+r_{d}+1+6(r_{d}+m+1))!

\displaystyle\times(\ell\rho_{d,T})!D\textnormal{-vol}(C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}}_{m,d},T_{m,d}^{\ell},W_{m,d})\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(7.55)

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{\ell=0}^{e_{d}}\sum_{k=0}^{e_{d}-\ell}{k+\ell\choose\ell}(23r_{d}+2mN+18m+\rho_{d,T}+23)!(\ell\rho_{d,T})!D\textnormal{-vol}(C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}}_{m,d},T_{m,d}^{\ell},W_{m,d})\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}.

∎

8.
Coarse Bounds for Gromov-Witten invariants in Products of Projective space

In this section we wish to give crude, but explicit, bounds for twisted Gromov-Witten invariants of products of projective space. We will use the localization technique of
[
Kon95
]
to do this.

8.1.
Computing Gromov-Witten Invariants for Products of Projective Space

Let

(8.1)

N,M,m,a,a_{1},\cdots,a_{m},a^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,a^{\prime}_{m},b_{1},\cdots,b_{m},\ell,i_{1},\cdots,i_{\ell}

be natural numbers.
Define
P:=\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}^{M}
and let
\mathcal{O}(c,d):=\pi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}(c)\otimes\pi_{2}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{M}}(d)
for each
c,d\in\mathbb{Z}

where
\pi_{1}:P\to\mathbb{P}^{N}
and
\pi_{2}:P\to\mathbb{P}^{M}
are the natural projection maps.
Let
d\in H_{2}^{+}(P)-0
.
We are interested in an explicit bound for the norm of the following twisted Gromov-Witten invariant:

(8.2)

\left<c_{1}(\mathcal{O}(a_{1},a^{\prime}_{1}))^{b_{1}},\cdots,c_{1}(\mathcal{O}(a_{m},a^{\prime}_{m}))^{b_{m}};\prod_{j=1}^{l}c_{i_{j}}(\mathcal{O}(a,0))\right>^{P}_{m,d}

(8.3)

:=\int_{[P_{m,d}]^{\operatorname{vir}}}\prod_{j=1}^{\ell}c_{i_{j}}((\pi_{m+1})_{*}(\operatorname{ev}_{m+1}^{*}\mathcal{O}(a,0)))\cup\cup_{j=1}^{m}\operatorname{ev}_{j}^{*}c_{1}(\mathcal{O}(a_{j},a^{\prime}_{j}))^{b_{j}}.

We will do this by an equivariant localization argument as in
[
Kon95
]
.
This bound will be very crude. For instance, it is not good enough to prove the corresponding generating function converges, unlike results in
[
Iri07
]
or
[
Zin14
]
.
On the other hand, this is an explicit bound depending on
d
and the parameters (
8.1
) above.

We write
([X_{0},\cdots,X_{N}],[Y_{0},\cdots,Y_{M}])
for the natural homogenous coordinates on
P
.
We have that
H_{2}^{+}(P)\cong\mathbb{N}^{2}

where the basis elements
(1,0)
and
(0,1)
correspond to lines in
\mathbb{P}^{N}\times[1,0,\cdots,0]
and
[1,0,\cdots,0]\times\mathbb{P}^{M}
respectively.
Let
d_{1},d_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
be such that
d=(d_{1},d_{2})
under this identification.

The group

(8.4)

\mathbb{T}:=(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{N+1+M+1}

acts on
P
via the action:

(8.5)

\mathbb{T}\times P\to P,

(8.6)

\displaystyle(\xi_{0},\cdots,\xi_{N},\nu_{0},\cdots,\nu_{M})\cdot([X_{0},\cdots,X_{N}],[Y_{0},\cdots,Y_{M}])

\displaystyle\to([\xi_{0}X_{0},\cdots,\xi_{N}X_{N}],[\nu_{0}Y_{0},\cdots,\nu_{M}Y_{M}]).

The fixed points of this action are given by
(p_{i},q_{j})
,
i=0,\cdots,N
,
j=0,\cdots,N

where

(8.7)

p_{i}=[\underbrace{0,\cdots,0}_{i},1,\underbrace{0,\cdots,0}_{N-i}]\in\mathbb{P}^{N},\quad q_{j}=[\underbrace{0,\cdots,0}_{j},1,\underbrace{0,\cdots,0}_{M-j}]\in\mathbb{P}^{M}.

We have that
\mathbb{T}
acts on
P_{m,d}
by post-composition.
We define
P^{\mathbb{T}}_{m,d}\subset P_{m,d}
to be the preimage of the fixed point locus of this action on the underlying coarse moduli space of
P_{m,d}
.

Definition 8.1
.

For us a
tree

T
will be a finite one dimensional CW complex with some
0
-cells removed so that the resulting space is contractible with at least one zero cell.
We let
\mathrm{ver}(T)
be its set of vertices (
0
-cells) and
\mathrm{edg}(T)
its set of edges (
1
-cells). We let
\mathrm{lf}(T)\subset\mathrm{edg}(T)
be the subset of edges where one vertex on that edge has been removed. These are called the
leaves
of
T
. Any edge that is not a leaf is called an
internal edge
.
We let
i\mathrm{edg}(\Gamma):=\mathrm{edg}(\Gamma)-\mathrm{lf}(\Gamma)
be the set of internal edges.
An
isomorphism
between two such trees is a homeomorphism sending the vertices of one tree to the vertices of the other.

For each vertex
v\in\mathrm{ver}(T)
, let
\textnormal{val}(v)
be the number of edges with
v
as a vertex.
We define
\textnormal{ival}(v)
to be the number of internal edges with
v
as a vertex.
For each edge
e\in\mathrm{edg}(\Gamma)
, we let
v_{e},v^{\prime}_{e}
be its vertices.

Note that there are no edges with two vertices removed since that would force
T
to be disconnected or have no vertices.
Let
f:\Sigma\to P
represent a
\mathbb{C}
-point of
P^{\mathbb{T}}_{m,d}
.
We construct a labeled tree
\Gamma_{f}
as follows:
We assign one vertex
v
for each connected component of
f^{-1}(\cup_{i=0}^{N}\cup_{j=0}^{M}\{(p_{i},q_{j})\})
. Let us call this connected component
\Sigma_{v}\subset\Sigma
.
We label this vertex by the pair of natural numbers
(i_{v},j_{v})
satisfying
f(\Sigma_{v})=\{(p_{i_{v}},q_{j_{v}})\}
.
Note that this connected component
\Sigma_{v}
could be a union of irreducible components of
\Sigma
, or it could just be a single point.
We assign one internal edge to each non-constant irreducible component of
\Sigma
. Let
\Sigma_{e}\subset\Sigma
be this irreducible component.
We have that
\Sigma_{e}
intersects exactly two connected components
\Sigma_{v}
,
\Sigma_{v^{\prime}}
where
v
,
v^{\prime}
are vertices of
\Gamma_{f}
.
In this case we require the edge
e
to have endpoints
v
and
v^{\prime}
respectively.
We label
e
by a pair of natural numbers
(d_{e,1},d_{e,2})
corresponding to
[f|_{\Sigma_{e}}]\in H_{2}^{+}(P)
.
Finally, we assign one leaf for each marked point and label this leaf by the element of
\{1,\cdots,m\}
corresponding to this marking.
The vertex
v
of this leaf is the connected component
\Sigma_{v}
that the marked point lives in.
Note that if
d_{e,1}\neq 0
then
d_{e,2}=0
and if
d_{e,2}\neq 0
then
d_{e,1}=0
.

Definition 8.2
.

An
(m,d)
-admissible tree
is a labelled tree
\Gamma
where vertices
v
are labelled by pairs of natural numbers
(i_{v},j_{v})\in\{0,\cdots,N\}\times\{0,\cdots,M\}
,
each internal edge
e
is labelled by a pair of natural numbers
(d_{e,1},d_{e,2})\in\mathbb{N}^{2}-\{(0,0)\}
and where there are
m
leaves labelled by
1,\cdots,m
.
We also require that

(1)

\Gamma
has at least one internal edge,

(2)

if
d_{e,1}\neq 0
then
d_{e,2}=0
, if
d_{e,2}\neq 0
then
d_{e,1}=0
,

(3)

if
v,v^{\prime}
are the vertices of an internal edge
e
, then
(i_{v},j_{v})\neq(i^{\prime}_{v},j^{\prime}_{v})
and
i_{v}=i^{\prime}_{v}
if
d_{e,1}=0
and
j_{v}=j^{\prime}_{v}
if
d_{e,2}=0
,

(4)

and
\sum_{e\in i\mathrm{edg}(\Gamma)}d_{e,1}=d_{1}
and
\sum_{e\in i\mathrm{edg}(\Gamma)}d_{e,2}=d_{2}
.

An
isomorphism
between two such
(m,d)
-admissible trees
\Gamma
and
\Gamma^{\prime}
is an isomorphism of the corresponding trees so that
labelings of the vertices, internal edges and leaves are respected.

For each
(m,d)
-admissible tree
\Gamma
, define
P^{\Gamma}\subset P_{m,d}^{\mathbb{T}}

to be the subspace of curves
f
where
\Gamma_{f}
is isomorphic to
\Gamma
.
Also, define
\mathrm{edg}_{1}(\Gamma)\subset\mathrm{edg}(\Gamma)
to be the subset of edges where
d_{2}=0
and
d_{1}\neq 0

and
\mathrm{edg}_{2}(\Gamma)\subset\mathrm{edg}(\Gamma)
the subset of edges where
d_{1}=0
and
d_{2}\neq 0
.

Observe that if the
(m,d)
-admissible trees
\Gamma_{f_{1}}
and
\Gamma_{f_{2}}
are isomorphic for some
\mathbb{C}
-points
f_{1},f_{2}
of
P_{m,d}^{\mathbb{T}}
then
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
lie in the same connected component of
P_{m,d}^{\mathbb{T}}
.

Definition 8.3
.

Let
\Gamma
be a
(m,d)
-admissible tree.
We let
\pi_{\Gamma}:\Sigma_{\Gamma}\to P^{\Gamma}
be the corresponding universal curve.
Let
\operatorname{ev}:\Sigma_{\Gamma}\to P
be the evaluation map.

Let
I(\Gamma)
be the set of irreducible components of
\Sigma_{\Gamma}
and
I_{\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)\subset I(\Gamma)
the subset of non-constant components
and
I_{\mathrm{ver}}(\Gamma)\subset I(\Gamma)
the subset of constant components.
We define
I_{1}(\Gamma)\subset I(\Gamma)
the subspace of irreducible components
\Sigma
so that
\pi_{2}\circ\operatorname{ev}|_{\Sigma}
is constant but
\pi_{1}\circ\operatorname{ev}|_{\Sigma}
is not.
We define
I_{2}(\Gamma)\subset I(\Gamma)
the subspace of irreducible components
\Sigma
so that
\pi_{1}\circ\operatorname{ev}|_{\Sigma}
is constant but
\pi_{2}\circ\operatorname{ev}|_{\Sigma}
is not.

Let
n(\Gamma)
be the set of sections of
\pi_{\Gamma}
whose image is contained in the nodal locus of
\Sigma_{\Gamma}
.
Define
n_{2\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)\subset n(\Gamma)
to be the subspace of sections whose image is contained in two non-constant components,

n_{1\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)\subset n(\Gamma)
to be the subspace of sections whose image is contained in exactly one non-constant component of
\Sigma_{\Gamma}
.
For each
s\in n(\Gamma)
, let
\Sigma_{s}
and
\Sigma^{\prime}_{s}
be the irreducible components of
\Sigma_{\Gamma}
containing the image of
s
.
If
s\in n_{1\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)
, then we will order these components so that
\Sigma_{s}
is the constant component of
\Sigma_{\Gamma}
.

For each irreducible component
\Sigma^{\prime}\in I(\Gamma)
, let
T_{\Sigma^{\prime}/P^{\Gamma}}
be the dual of the relative dualizing sheaf.
For each
s\in n(\Gamma)
, define
T^{\otimes}_{s}
to be
s^{*}(T_{\Sigma_{s}/P^{\Gamma}}\otimes T_{\Sigma^{\prime}_{s}/P^{\Gamma}})

and define
T^{\oplus}_{s}
to be
s^{*}(T_{\Sigma_{s}/P^{\Gamma}}\oplus T_{\Sigma^{\prime}_{s}/P^{\Gamma}})
.
Also, for each
s\in n_{1\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)
, define
T^{\mathrm{ver}}_{s}:=s^{*}T_{\Sigma_{s}/P^{\Gamma}}
and
T^{\mathrm{edg}}_{s}:=s^{*}T_{\Sigma^{\prime}_{s}/P^{\Gamma}}
.

We let
h(\Gamma)
be the set of marked point sections of
\Sigma_{\Gamma}
.
For each
s\in h(\Gamma)
let
\Sigma_{s}
be the irreducible component of
\Sigma_{\Gamma}
containing the image of
s
and define
T_{s}:=s^{*}T_{\Sigma_{s}/P^{\Gamma}}
.

We have that
n(\Gamma)=n_{2\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)\sqcup n_{1\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)
since a generic element of
P^{\Gamma}
has no nodes connecting constant components since any such node can be smoothed and also since
(d_{1},d_{2})\neq(0,0)
.

We wish to compute (
8.2
)
using the Atiyah-Bott localization formula.
As a result, we need to compute the equivariant Euler class of the normal bundle
N_{\Gamma}
to
P^{\Gamma}
inside
P_{m,d}
for each
(m,d)
-admissible tree
\Gamma
.
We will first compute it in the equivariant
K
-theory group
K^{0}_{\mathbb{T}}(P^{\Gamma})\otimes\mathbb{Q}=K^{0}(P^{\Gamma})\otimes\mathbb{Q}[\mathbb{T}^{\vee}]

where
\mathbb{T}^{\vee}
is the group of characters
\mathbb{T}\to\mathbb{C}^{*}
of
\mathbb{T}
.

By reasoning as in
[
Kon95
, Section 3.3.1]
and
[
Kon95
, Section 3.3.5]
, we have
the following identities in
K^{0}_{\mathbb{T}}(P^{\Gamma})\otimes\mathbb{Q}
:

(8.8)

[N_{\Gamma}]=[T(P_{m,d})|_{P^{\Gamma}}]-[TP^{\Gamma}],

(8.9)

[T(P_{m,d})|_{P^{\Gamma}}]=[(TP)_{m,d}|_{P^{\Gamma}}]+\sum_{s\in n(\Gamma)}T^{\otimes}_{s}+T^{\oplus}_{s}-\sum_{\Sigma\in I(\Gamma)}[(\pi_{\Gamma}|_{\Sigma})_{*}T_{\Sigma/P^{\Gamma}}]+\sum_{s\in h(\Gamma)}T_{s}

and

(8.10)

[TP^{\Gamma}]=\sum_{s\in n_{1\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)}[T^{\mathrm{ver}}_{\Gamma}]-\sum_{\Sigma\in I_{\mathrm{ver}}(\Gamma)}[(\pi_{\Gamma}|_{\Sigma})_{*}T_{\Sigma/P^{\Gamma}}]+\sum_{s\in h(\Gamma)}[T_{s}].

Hence:

(8.11)

[N_{\Gamma}]=[(TP)_{m,d}|_{P^{\Gamma}}]+[N^{\prime}_{\Gamma}]

where

(8.12)

[N^{\prime}_{\Gamma}]=\sum_{s\in n(\Gamma)}T^{\otimes}_{s}+T^{\oplus}_{s}-\sum_{\Sigma\in I(\Gamma)}[(\pi_{\Gamma}|_{\Sigma})_{*}T_{\Sigma/P^{\Gamma}}]-\sum_{s\in n_{1\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)}[T^{\mathrm{ver}}_{\Gamma}]+\sum_{\Sigma\in I_{\mathrm{ver}}(\Gamma)}[(\pi_{\Gamma})_{*}T_{\Sigma/P^{\Gamma}}]

(8.13)

=\sum_{s\in n(\Gamma)}[T^{\otimes}_{s}]+\sum_{s\in n_{2\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)}[T^{\oplus s}]+\sum_{s\in n_{1\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)}[T^{\oplus s}]-\sum_{s\in n_{1\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)}[T^{\mathrm{ver}}_{\Gamma}]-\sum_{\Sigma\in I_{\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)}[(\pi_{\Gamma})_{*}T_{\Sigma/P^{\Gamma}}]

(8.14)

=\sum_{s\in n(\Gamma)}[T^{\otimes}_{s}]+\sum_{s\in n_{2\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)}[T^{\oplus s}]+\sum_{s\in n_{1\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)}[T^{\mathrm{edg}}_{s}]-\sum_{\Sigma\in I_{\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)}[(\pi_{\Gamma})_{*}T_{\Sigma/P^{\Gamma}}]

(8.15)

=\sum_{s\in n_{2\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)}[T^{\otimes}_{s}]+\sum_{s\in n_{1\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)}[T^{\otimes}_{s}]+\sum_{s\in n_{2\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)}[T^{\oplus}_{s}]+\sum_{s\in n_{1\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)}[T^{\mathrm{edg}}_{s}]-\sum_{\Sigma\in I_{\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)}[(\pi_{\Gamma})_{*}T_{\Sigma/P^{\Gamma}}]

(8.16)

=\sum_{s\in n_{2\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)}([T^{\otimes}_{s}]+[T^{\oplus}_{s}])+\sum_{s\in n_{1\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)}([T^{\otimes}_{s}]+[T^{\mathrm{edg}}_{s}])-\sum_{\Sigma\in I_{\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)}[(\pi_{\Gamma})_{*}T_{\Sigma/P^{\Gamma}}]

(8.17)

=\sum_{s\in n_{2\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)}([T^{\otimes}_{s}]+[T^{\oplus}_{s}])+\sum_{s\in n_{1\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)}([T^{\mathrm{ver}}_{s}\otimes T^{\mathrm{edg}}_{s}]+[T^{\mathrm{edg}}_{s}])-\sum_{\Sigma\in I_{\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)}[(\pi_{\Gamma})_{*}T_{\Sigma/P^{\Gamma}}].

Combining the calculation above with (
8.11
) gives

(8.18)

[N_{\Gamma}]=[(TP)_{m,d}|_{P^{\Gamma}}]+\sum_{s\in n_{2\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)}([T^{\otimes}_{s}]+[T^{\oplus}_{s}])+\sum_{s\in n_{1\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)}([T^{\mathrm{ver}}_{s}\otimes T^{\mathrm{edg}}_{s}]+[T^{\mathrm{edg}}_{s}])-\sum_{\Sigma\in I_{\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)}[(\pi_{\Gamma})_{*}T_{\Sigma/P^{\Gamma}}].

We now wish to compute the integral of the equivariant Euler class of

(8.19)

\sum_{j=1}^{m}b_{j}[\operatorname{ev}_{j}^{*}\mathcal{O}(a_{j},a^{\prime}_{j})|_{P^{\Gamma}}]-[N_{\Gamma}]\in K^{0}_{\mathbb{T}}(P^{\Gamma})\otimes\mathbb{Q}

plus an additional term coming from summands of
(\pi_{m+1})_{*}(\operatorname{ev}_{m+1}^{*}\mathcal{O}(a,0))|_{P^{\Gamma}}

against
[P^{\Gamma}]
.
By the Atiyah-Bott localization formula the sum of such integrals over all
(m,d)
-admissible trees
\Gamma
will give (
8.2
).
The only vector bundle in the sum (
8.19
) which is non-trivial as a non-equivariant vector bundle is
-[N_{\Gamma}]

and the only sum in (
8.18
) which is non-trivial as a non-equivariant vector bundle is
\sum_{s\in n_{1\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)}[T^{\mathrm{ver}}_{s}\otimes T^{\mathrm{edg}}_{s}]
.
Also,
(\pi_{m+1})_{*}(\operatorname{ev}_{m+1}^{*}\mathcal{O}(a,0))|_{P^{\Gamma}}
is trivial as a non-equivariant vector bundle.
As a result, we need to compute the integral over
[P^{\Gamma}]
of the equivariant Euler class
e_{\mathbb{T}}
of

(8.20)

-\sum_{s\in n_{1\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)}[T^{\mathrm{ver}}_{s}\otimes T^{\mathrm{edg}}_{s}].

and then multiply this by the equivariant Euler classes of the other bundles in (
8.19
), (
8.18
) and summands of
(\pi_{m+1})_{*}(\operatorname{ev}_{m+1}^{*}\mathcal{O}(a,0))
, viewed as Laurent polynomials in the equivariant parameters
since they are pullbacks of
\mathbb{T}
-representations via the map
P^{\Gamma}\to\textnormal{point}
.

We have that
\mathbb{T}^{\vee}
is generated by the characters
\lambda_{0},\cdots,\lambda_{N},\mu_{0},\cdots,\mu_{N}

where
\lambda_{i}
is the projection map to the
(i+1)
st
factor of
\mathbb{T}=(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{N+1+M+1}
for each
i=0,\cdots,N

and
\mu_{i}
is the projection map to the
(i+N+2)
nd
factor for each
i=0,\cdots,M
.
Hence,

(8.21)

H^{*}_{\mathbb{T}}(P^{\Gamma};\mathbb{Q})_{\textnormal{loc}}=H^{*}(P^{\Gamma};\mathbb{Q})(\lambda_{0},\cdots,\lambda_{N},\mu_{0},\cdots,\mu_{N})

where “
loc
” means that we localize with respect to the multiplicative system of polynomials in the equivariant parameters with coefficients in
H^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{\Gamma};\mathbb{Q})\cong\mathbb{Q}
.

Let us start with the term

(8.22)

[N_{\Gamma}]-[(TP)_{m,d}|_{P^{\Gamma}}].

Definition 8.4
.

A
flag
of
\Gamma
is a pair
(v,e)\in\mathrm{ver}(\Gamma)\times i\mathrm{edg}(\Gamma)
where
v
is a vertex of
e
.
For any such flag
F
, we write
v_{F}:=v
and
v^{\prime}_{F}
to be the other vertex of the internal edge
e
.
A
\pi_{1}
-flag
is a flag
(v,e)
where
e\in\mathrm{edg}_{1}(\Gamma)
.
A
\pi_{2}
-flag
is a flag
(v,e)
where
e\in\mathrm{edg}_{2}(\Gamma)
.
For each
\pi_{1}
-flag
F=(v,e)
, we define

(8.23)

w_{F}:=\frac{\lambda_{i_{v_{F}}}-\lambda_{i_{v^{\prime}_{F}}}}{d_{e,1}}.

For each
\pi_{2}
-flag
F=(v,e)
, we define

(8.24)

w_{F}:=\frac{\mu_{j_{v_{F}}}-\mu_{j_{v^{\prime}_{F}}}}{d_{e,2}}.

Also, for each flag
F=(v,e)
we define
s_{F}\in n(\Gamma)
to be the unique element with the property that either
\Sigma_{s_{F}}
or
\Sigma^{\prime}_{s_{F}}
corresponds to
e

and that
(i_{v},j_{v})
is in the image of
\operatorname{ev}\circ s_{F}
.

Here
w_{F}
represents the equivariant Euler class of
T^{\mathrm{edg}}_{s_{F}}
if
s_{F}\in n_{1\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)
.
If
F,F^{\prime}
are distinct flags with the same vertex
v
and
s_{F}\in n_{2\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)
then
w_{F}+w_{F^{\prime}}
represents the equivariant Euler class of
T^{\otimes}_{s_{F}}
.
By reasoning as in
[
Kon95
, Sections 3.3.2 and 3.3.3]
, we get:

(8.25)

\displaystyle\int_{[P^{\Gamma}]}e_{\mathbb{T}}([N_{\Gamma}]-[(TP)_{m,d}|_{P^{\Gamma}}]+\sum_{e\in i\mathrm{edg}(\Gamma)}[\mathbb{C}])=

\displaystyle\prod_{v\in\mathrm{ver}(\Gamma)}\left(\left(\sum_{\textnormal{flags}\ F=(v,e)}w_{F}^{-1}\right)^{\textnormal{val}(v)-3}\prod_{\textnormal{flags}\ F=(v,e)}w_{F}^{-1}\right)

where
[\mathbb{C}]
is the class of the trivial vector bundle of rank
1
where
\mathbb{T}
acts trivially.
Here the term
\sum_{s\in n_{1\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)}[T^{\mathrm{ver}}_{s}\otimes T^{\mathrm{edg}}_{s}]
in
(
8.17
) corresponds to the product over all
v
in (
8.25
) where the valency of
v
is
\geqslant 3
. The term
\sum_{s\in n_{2\mathrm{edg}}(\Gamma)}[T^{\otimes}_{s}]
in (
8.17
) corresponds to the product over all
v
in (
8.25
) where the valency of
v
is
2
.
The remaining terms in (
8.17
) correspond to the product over all
v
in (
8.25
) where the valency of
v
is
1
.

Now let us now compute the equivariant Euler class of the term

(8.26)

[(TP)_{m,d}|_{P^{\Gamma}}]=[(\pi_{1}^{*}T\mathbb{P}^{N})_{m,d}|_{P^{\Gamma}}]+[(\pi_{2}^{*}T\mathbb{P}^{M})_{m,d}|_{P^{\Gamma}}].

Definition 8.5
.

Let
Q_{\Gamma}^{1}
be the set of connected components of
(\pi_{1}\circ\operatorname{ev})^{-1}(\{p_{0},\cdots,p_{N}\})\subset\Sigma_{\Gamma}
.
Similarly, let
Q_{\Gamma}^{2}
be the set of connected components of
(\pi_{2}\circ\operatorname{ev})^{-1}(\{q_{0},\cdots,q_{N}\})\subset\Sigma_{\Gamma}
.
Let
k=0
or
1
.
For each connected component
\Sigma^{\prime}\in Q_{\Gamma}^{k}
, let
\textnormal{ival}(\Sigma^{\prime})\in\mathbb{N}
be the number of
e\in\mathrm{edg}_{k}(\Gamma)

satisfying
\Sigma_{e}\cap Q_{\Gamma}\neq\emptyset
.
Define
\check{Q}_{\Gamma}^{k}\subset Q_{\Gamma}^{k}
to be the subset of
\Sigma^{\prime}
satisfying
\textnormal{ival}(\Sigma^{\prime})>1
.
For each
\Sigma^{\prime}\in Q_{\Gamma}^{1}
, let
i_{\Sigma^{\prime}}\in\{0,\cdots,N\}
be such that
\{p_{i_{\Sigma^{\prime}}}\}=\pi_{1}(\operatorname{ev}(\Sigma^{\prime}))
.
For each
\Sigma^{\prime}\in Q_{\Gamma}^{2}
, let
j_{\Sigma^{\prime}}\in\{0,\cdots,M\}
be such that
\{q_{j_{\Sigma^{\prime}}}\}=\pi_{2}(\operatorname{ev}(\Sigma^{\prime}))
.

By the discussion in
[
Kon95
, 3.3.4]
, we get:

(8.27)

\displaystyle e_{\Gamma}([(\pi_{1}^{*}T\mathbb{P}^{N})_{m,d}|_{P^{\Gamma}}]-\sum_{e\in\mathrm{edg}_{1}(\Gamma)}[\mathbb{C}])=

\displaystyle\prod_{e\in\mathrm{edg}_{1}(\Gamma)}\left(\frac{(-1)^{d_{e,1}}\left(\frac{d_{e,1}}{\lambda_{i_{v_{e}}}-\lambda_{i_{v^{\prime}_{e}}}}\right)^{2d_{e,1}}}{(d_{e,1}!)^{2}}\prod_{k\neq i_{v_{e}},i_{v^{\prime}_{e}}}\prod_{\overset{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}\geqslant 0}{a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}=d_{e,1}}}\frac{1}{\frac{a^{\prime}}{d_{e,1}}\lambda_{i_{v_{e}}}+\frac{b^{\prime}}{d_{e,1}}\lambda_{i_{v^{\prime}_{e}}}-\lambda_{k}}\right)\times

\displaystyle\prod_{\Sigma^{\prime}\in\check{Q}_{\Gamma}^{1}}\left(\prod_{j\neq i_{\Sigma^{\prime}}}\lambda_{i_{\Sigma^{\prime}}}-\lambda_{j}\right)^{\textnormal{ival}(\Sigma^{\prime})-1}

and

(8.28)

\displaystyle e_{\Gamma}(([(\pi_{2}^{*}T\mathbb{P}^{N})_{m,d}|_{P^{\Gamma}}]-\sum_{e\in\mathrm{edg}_{2}(\Gamma)}[\mathbb{C}]))=

\displaystyle\prod_{e\in\mathrm{edg}_{2}(\Gamma)}\left(\frac{(-1)^{d_{e,2}}\left(\frac{d_{e,2}}{\mu_{j_{v_{e}}}-\mu_{j_{v^{\prime}_{e}}}}\right)^{2d_{e,2}}}{(d_{e,2}!)^{2}}\prod_{k\neq j_{v_{e}},j_{v^{\prime}_{e}}}\prod_{\overset{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}\geqslant 0}{a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}=d_{e,2}}}\frac{1}{\frac{a^{\prime}}{d_{e,2}}\mu_{j_{v_{e}}}+\frac{b^{\prime}}{d_{e,2}}\mu_{j_{v^{\prime}_{e}}}-\mu_{k}}\right)\times

\displaystyle\prod_{\Sigma^{\prime}\in\check{Q}_{\Gamma}^{2}}\left(\prod_{j\neq j_{\Sigma^{\prime}}}\lambda_{j_{\Sigma^{\prime}}}-\lambda_{j}\right)^{\textnormal{ival}(\Sigma^{\prime})-1}

So,
e_{\mathbb{T}}([(TP)_{m,d}|_{P^{\Gamma}}])
is the product of (
8.27
) with (
8.28
).

Definition 8.6
.

We define
c_{i}^{\mathbb{T}}(W)
to be the
i
th equivariant Chern class of any
\mathbb{T}
-equivariant vector bundle
W
. We define
c^{\mathbb{T}}(W)=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}c_{i}^{\mathbb{T}}(W)
to be the total equivariant Chern class.

The bundle
(\pi_{m+1})_{*}(\operatorname{ev}_{m+1}^{*}\mathcal{O}(a,0))|_{P^{\Gamma}}
sits in an exact sequence:

(8.29)

\displaystyle 0\to(\pi_{\Gamma})_{*}(\operatorname{ev}^{*}\mathcal{O}(a,0))\to\bigoplus_{e\in\mathrm{edg}_{1}(\Gamma)}(\pi_{\Gamma}|_{\Sigma_{e}})_{*}(\operatorname{ev}^{*}\mathcal{O}(a,0)|_{\Sigma_{e}})

\displaystyle\to\bigoplus_{\Sigma^{\prime}\in\check{Q}_{\Gamma}^{1}}(\pi_{\Gamma}|_{\Sigma^{\prime}})_{*}(\operatorname{ev}^{*}\mathcal{O}(a,0)|_{\Sigma^{\prime}})\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\textnormal{ival}(\Sigma^{\prime})-1}\to 0.

For each
e\in\mathrm{edg}_{1}(\Gamma)
, we get
(\pi_{\Gamma}|_{\Sigma_{e}})_{*}(\operatorname{ev}^{*}\mathcal{O}(a,0)|_{\Sigma_{e}})

splits as a direct sum of line bundles
\oplus_{a^{\prime}=0}^{ad_{e,1}}L_{e,a^{\prime}}

where

(8.30)

c_{1}^{\mathbb{T}}(L_{e,a^{\prime}})=\frac{a^{\prime}\lambda_{i_{v_{e}}}+(ad_{e,1}-a^{\prime})\lambda_{i_{v^{\prime}_{e}}}}{d_{e,1}}.

Also, for
\Sigma^{\prime}\in Q_{\Gamma}^{1}
, we have:

(8.31)

c_{1}^{\mathbb{T}}((\pi_{\Gamma}|_{\Sigma^{\prime}})_{*}(\operatorname{ev}^{*}\mathcal{O}(a,0))|_{\Sigma^{\prime}})=a\lambda_{i_{\Sigma^{\prime}}}.

Definition 8.7
.

We define

(8.32)

\mathrm{edg}(\Gamma,a):=\left\{(e,a^{\prime})\ :\ e\in\mathrm{edg}(\Gamma),\ d_{e,1}\neq 0,\ a^{\prime}\in\{0,\cdots,ad_{e,1}\}\right\}.

By (
8.29
), (
8.30
) and (
8.31
), we have

(8.33)

\displaystyle c^{\mathbb{T}}((\pi_{\Gamma})_{*}(\operatorname{ev}^{*}\mathcal{O}(a,0)))=

\displaystyle\prod_{(e,a)\in\mathrm{edg}(\Gamma,a)}\left(1+\frac{a^{\prime}\lambda_{i_{v_{e}}}+(ad_{e,1}-a^{\prime})\lambda_{i_{v^{\prime}_{e}}}}{d_{e,1}}\right)\prod_{\Sigma^{\prime}\in\check{Q}_{\Gamma}^{1}}\left(1+a\lambda_{i_{\Sigma^{\prime}}}\right)^{1-\textnormal{ival}(\Sigma^{\prime})}=

\displaystyle\prod_{(e,a)\in\mathrm{edg}(\Gamma,a)}\left(1+\frac{a^{\prime}\lambda_{i_{v_{e}}}+(ad_{e,1}-a^{\prime})\lambda_{i_{v^{\prime}_{e}}}}{d_{e,1}}\right)\prod_{\Sigma^{\prime}\in\check{Q}_{\Gamma}^{1}}\left(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}(-1)^{k}{k+\textnormal{ival}(\Sigma^{\prime})-2\choose k}(a\lambda_{i_{\Sigma^{\prime}}})^{k}\right).

Hence:

(8.34)

\displaystyle\prod_{j=1}^{\ell}c_{i_{j}}^{\mathbb{T}}((\pi_{\Gamma})_{*}(\operatorname{ev}^{*}\mathcal{O}(a,0)))=\prod_{j=1}^{\ell}\sum_{\overset{S_{1}\subset\mathrm{edg}(\Gamma,a),\ f:\check{Q}_{\Gamma}^{1}\to\mathbb{N},}{|S_{1}|+\sum_{\Sigma^{\prime}\in\check{Q}_{\Gamma}^{1}}f(\Sigma^{\prime})=i_{j}}}

\displaystyle\left(\prod_{(e,a^{\prime})\in S_{1}}\frac{a^{\prime}\lambda_{i_{v_{e}}}+(ad_{e,1}-a^{\prime})\lambda_{i_{v^{\prime}_{e}}}}{d_{e,1}}\right)\prod_{\Sigma^{\prime}\in\check{Q}_{\Gamma}^{1}}(-1)^{f(\Sigma^{\prime})}{f(\Sigma^{\prime})+\textnormal{ival}(\Sigma^{\prime})-2\choose f(\Sigma^{\prime})}(a\lambda_{i_{\Sigma^{\prime}}})^{f(\Sigma^{\prime})}.

Definition 8.8
.

For each
h\in\mathrm{lf}(\Gamma)
, define
(i_{h},j_{h})\in\{0,\cdots,N\}\times\{0,\cdots,M\}
so that the image of
\operatorname{ev}_{h}|_{\Sigma_{\Gamma}}
is
(p_{i_{h}},q_{j_{h}})
.

We have

(8.35)

e_{\mathbb{T}}([\operatorname{ev}_{h}^{*}\mathcal{O}(a_{h},a^{\prime}_{h})|_{P^{\Gamma}}])=\lambda_{i_{h}}^{a_{h}}\mu_{j_{h}}^{a^{\prime}_{h}}\quad\forall\ h\in\mathrm{lf}(\Gamma).

Hence, by Atiyah-Bott localization we have:

(8.36)

\textnormal{\eqref{eqngromovwitteninvariantbdd}}=\sum_{(m,d)\textnormal{-admissible trees}\ \Gamma}\frac{1}{|\mathrm{Aut}(\Gamma)|\prod_{e\in\mathrm{edg}(\Gamma)}(d_{e,1}+d_{e,2})}\textnormal{\eqref{eqnaefaefaefaefeafeafeafaefee}}\times\textnormal{\eqref{eqnaefaeaefegamma1}}\times\textnormal{\eqref{eqnaefaeaefegamma12}}\times\textnormal{\eqref{eqnawfawfrrr4}}\times\textnormal{\eqref{aef494a0w9kg40w49gkw0g9kdsvsk0}}.

8.2.
Bounds for Gromov-Witten Invariants of Products of Projective Space

We need to find an upper bound for the absolute value of (
8.36
).
The left-hand side of this equation does not depend on the parameters
\lambda_{0},\cdots,\lambda_{N},\mu_{0},\cdots,\mu_{M}
.
As a result, we only need to bound the right-hand side of (
8.36
) for specific values of these parameters.
Let

(8.37)

|d|=\sum_{e\in\mathrm{edg}(\Gamma)}(d_{e,1}+d_{e,2}).

We will now fix the following values for
\lambda_{j}
and
\mu_{j}
for each appropriate
j
:

(8.38)

\lambda_{j}=1+j+\left(\frac{1}{|d|+1}\right)^{N+1-j},\quad\mu_{j}=1+j+\left(\frac{1}{|d|+1}\right)^{M+1-j}.

Cayley’s formula states that the number of trees with
k
vertices, each one uniquely labeled from
1
to
k
, is
k^{k-2}
.
Hence:

(8.39)

\sum_{\textnormal{trees}\ T\ \textnormal{with}\ k\ \textnormal{vertices}}\frac{1}{|\textnormal{Aut}(T)|}\leqslant k^{k-2}/k!.

By Stirling approximation we have

(8.40)

k^{k}/k!\leqslant e^{12k+1}\leqslant 4^{12k+1},\quad\forall\ k\in\mathbb{N}.

Hence,

(8.41)

\sum_{\textnormal{trees}\ T\ \textnormal{with}\ k\ \textnormal{vertices}}\frac{1}{|\textnormal{Aut}(T)|}\leqslant 4^{12k+1}

where
\mathrm{Aut}(T)
is the group of automorphism of the tree
T
.
Hence,

(8.42)

\displaystyle\sum_{(m,d)\textnormal{-admissible trees}\ \Gamma}\frac{1}{|\mathrm{Aut}(\Gamma)|}\leqslant 4^{12(|d|+m+1)+1}\times m!\times|d|^{|d|}

\displaystyle\overset{\textnormal{\eqref{eqnstirlingupperobuund}}}{\leqslant}4^{12(|d|+m+1)+1}m!|d|!4^{12|d|+1}

\displaystyle\leqslant 4^{24(|d|+m+1)}m!|d|!

where
\mathrm{Aut}(\Gamma)
is the group of automorphisms of the
(m,d)
-admissible tree
\Gamma
.

Definition 8.9
.

Recall that a
partition
of a number
k
is a sequence
1\leqslant a_{1}\leqslant\cdots\leqslant a_{\ell}

whose sum is
k
.
The
norm
of such a partition is the product
\prod_{j=1}^{\ell}a_{\ell}
.

Using the fact that the norm of a partition of
k
is at most
4^{k}
(
[
Hal91
, Page 188]
) for each
k\in\mathbb{N}

and the fact that the valency of each vertex of each
(m,d)
-admissible tree
\Gamma
is at most
M+N
, we get that the norm of (
8.25
) with the substitutions (
8.38
)
is less than or equal to:

(8.43)

\prod_{v\in\mathrm{ver}(\Gamma)}\left(\left((N+M)\max_{\textnormal{flags}\ F=(v,e)}(d_{e,1}+d_{e,2})\right)^{M+N}\prod_{\textnormal{flags}\ F=(v,e)}(d_{e,1}+d_{e,2})\right)

(8.44)

\leqslant\prod_{v\in\mathrm{ver}(\Gamma)}\left((M+N)\left(\prod_{\textnormal{flags}\ F=(v,e)}(d_{e,1}+d_{e,2})\right)^{2}\right)^{M+N}

(8.45)

\leqslant\left(\prod_{v\in\mathrm{ver}(\Gamma)}(M+N)^{(M+N)}\right)\left(\prod_{v\in\mathrm{ver}(\Gamma)}\prod_{\textnormal{flags}\ F=(v,e)}(d_{e,1}+d_{e,2})\right)^{2(M+N)}

(8.46)

\leqslant(M+N)^{(M+N)(|d|+1)}\left(\prod_{v\in\mathrm{ver}(\Gamma)}\prod_{\textnormal{flags}\ F=(v,e)}(d_{e,1}+d_{e,2})\right)^{2(M+N)}

(8.47)

\leqslant(M+N)^{(M+N)(|d|+1)}\left((4^{|d|})^{2}\right)^{2(M+N)}

Hence

(8.48)

|\textnormal{\eqref{eqnaefaefaefaefeafeafeafaefee}}|\leqslant(4(M+N))^{5(M+N)(|d|+1)}.

Under the substitutions (
8.38
),
each of the terms

(8.49)

\frac{a^{\prime}}{d_{e,1}}\lambda_{i_{v_{e}}}+\frac{b^{\prime}}{d_{e,1}}\lambda_{i_{v^{\prime}_{e}}}-\lambda_{k},\quad a^{\prime},b^{\prime}\geqslant 0,\ a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}=d_{e,1},\ k\in\{0,\cdots,N\}-\{i_{v_{e}},i_{v^{\prime}_{e}}\},\ e\in\mathrm{edg}_{1}(\Gamma)

has norm at least
\frac{1}{(|d|+1)^{N+2}}
and hence at least
\frac{1}{(|d|+1)^{N+M+2}}
.
Hence:

(8.50)

|\textnormal{\eqref{eqnaefaeaefegamma1}}|\leqslant\prod_{e\in\mathrm{edg}_{1}(\Gamma)}\frac{d_{e,1}^{2d_{e,1}}}{(d_{e,1}!)^{2}}\left(\prod_{k\neq i_{v_{e}},i_{v^{\prime}_{e}}}\prod_{\overset{a^{\prime},b^{\prime}\geqslant 0}{a^{\prime}+b^{\prime}=d_{e,1}}}(|d|+1)^{N+M+2}\right)\prod_{v\in\mathrm{ver}(\Gamma)}(N+M+2)^{(N+M+2)}

(8.51)

\leqslant|d|^{2|d|}\left(\prod_{e\in\mathrm{edg}_{1}(\Gamma)}(|d|+1)^{(N+M+2)d_{e,1}(N+M)}\right)(N+M+2)^{(N+M+2)(|d|+1)}

(8.52)

\leqslant|d|^{2|d|}(|d|+1)^{(N+M+2)|d|(N+M+2)}(N+M+2)^{(N+M+2)(|d|+1)}

(8.53)

\leqslant|d|^{2|d|}(|d|+1)^{|d|(N+M+2)^{2}}(N+M+2)^{(N+M+2)(|d|+1)}

(8.54)

\leqslant|d|^{(N+M+2)|d|}(|d|+1)^{|d|(N+M+2)^{2}}(N+M+2)^{(N+M+2)(|d|+1)}

(8.55)

\leqslant(|d|+1)^{|d|(N+M+2)^{2}}(|d|(N+M+2))^{(N+M+2)^{2}(|d|+1)}.

(8.56)

|\textnormal{\eqref{eqnaefaeaefegamma1}}|\leqslant((N+M+2)(|d|+1))^{2(|d|+1)(N+M+2)^{2}}.

Similarly,

(8.57)

|\textnormal{\eqref{eqnaefaeaefegamma12}}|\leqslant((N+M+2)(|d|+1))^{2(|d|+1)(N+M+2)^{2}}.

We have

(8.58)

\displaystyle|\textnormal{\eqref{eqnawfawfrrr4}}|\leqslant\prod_{j=1}^{\ell}\sum_{\overset{S_{1}\subset\mathrm{edg}(\Gamma,a)\ f:Q^{\prime}_{\Gamma}\to\mathbb{N}}{|S_{1}|+\sum_{\Sigma^{\prime}\in\check{Q}_{\Gamma}^{1}}f(\Sigma^{\prime})=i_{j}}}

\displaystyle\left(\prod_{(e,a^{\prime})\in S_{1}}a(N+2)\right)\prod_{\Sigma^{\prime}\in\check{Q}_{\Gamma}^{1}}{f(\Sigma^{\prime})+\textnormal{ival}(\Sigma^{\prime})-2\choose f(\Sigma^{\prime})}(a(N+2))^{f(\Sigma^{\prime})}.

Again, using the fact that the valency of each vertex of each
(m,d)
-admissible tree
\Gamma
is at most
N+M
, we get (
8.58
) is less than or equal to

(8.59)

\prod_{j=1}^{\ell}\sum_{\overset{S_{1}\subset\mathrm{edg}(\Gamma,a)\ f:\check{Q}_{\Gamma}^{1}\to\mathbb{N}}{|S_{1}|+\sum_{\Sigma^{\prime}\in\check{Q}_{\Gamma}^{1}}f(\Sigma^{\prime})=i_{j}}}\left(a(N+2)\right)^{i_{j}}(a(N+2))^{i_{j}}2^{i_{j}+(N+M)-2}

(8.60)

\leqslant\prod_{j=1}^{\ell}\sum_{\overset{S_{1}\subset\mathrm{edg}(\Gamma,a)\ f:\check{Q}_{\Gamma}^{1}\to\mathbb{N}}{|S_{1}|+\sum_{\Sigma^{\prime}\in\check{Q}_{\Gamma}^{1}}f(\Sigma^{\prime})=i_{j}}}\left(2a(N+2)\right)^{2i_{j}+(N+M)-2}

(8.61)

\leqslant\prod_{j=1}^{\ell}(|d|\times a|d|)^{i_{j}}{|d|+i_{j}\choose i_{j}}\left(2a(N+2)\right)^{2i_{j}+(N+M)-2}

(8.62)

\leqslant\prod_{j=1}^{\ell}(|d|\times a|d|)^{i_{j}}(|d|+i_{j})^{i_{j}}\left(2a(N+2)\right)^{2i_{j}+(N+M)-2}

(8.63)

\leqslant(|d|\times a|d|)^{\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}i_{j}}\left(2a(N+2)\right)^{2\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}i_{j}+\ell(N+M)-2\ell}\prod_{j=1}^{\ell}(|d|+i_{j})^{i_{j}}

(8.64)

\leqslant(a|d|)^{2\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}i_{j}}\left(2a(N+2)\right)^{2\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}i_{j}+\ell(N+M)-2\ell}\prod_{j=1}^{\ell}(|d|+i_{j})^{i_{j}}.

Hence,

(8.65)

|\textnormal{\eqref{eqnawfawfrrr4}}|\leqslant(a|d|)^{2\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}i_{j}}\left(2a(N+2)\right)^{2\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}i_{j}+\ell(N+M)-2\ell}\prod_{j=1}^{\ell}(|d|+i_{j})^{i_{j}}.

We have

(8.66)

|\textnormal{\eqref{aef494a0w9kg40w49gkw0g9kdsvsk0}}|\leqslant(N+2)^{a_{j}}(M+2)^{a^{\prime}_{j}}.

Hence, by (
8.36
), (
8.42
), (
8.48
), (
8.56
), (
8.57
), (
8.65
), (
8.66
) we get

(8.67)

\displaystyle|\textnormal{\eqref{eqngromovwitteninvariantbdd}}|\leqslant

\displaystyle m!4^{24(|d|+m+1)}|d|!\times

\displaystyle(4(M+N))^{5(M+N)(|d|+1)}(((N+M+2)(|d|+1))^{2(|d|+1)(N+M+2)^{2}})^{2}\times

\displaystyle(a|d|)^{2\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}i_{j}}\left(2a(N+2)\right)^{2\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}i_{j}+\ell(N+M)-2\ell}\prod_{j=1}^{\ell}(|d|+i_{j})^{i_{j}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}(N+2)^{a_{j}}(M+2)^{a^{\prime}_{j}}.

Now, (
8.2
) vanishes if

\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}i_{j}>\dim(P_{0,d})
.
Hence, we can assume

(8.68)

\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}i_{j}\leqslant(N+M+2)(|d|+1).

Combining this with (
8.67
) gives:

(8.69)

\displaystyle|\textnormal{\eqref{eqngromovwitteninvariantbdd}}|\leqslant

\displaystyle m!4^{24(|d|+m+1)}|d|!\times

\displaystyle(4(M+N))^{5(M+N)(|d|+1)}(((N+M+2)(|d|+1))^{2(|d|+1)(N+M+2)^{2}})^{2}\times

\displaystyle(a|d|)^{2(M+N+2)(|d|+1)}\left(2a(N+2)\right)^{2(M+N+2)(|d|+1)+(M+N+2)(|d|+1)(N+M)-2(M+N+2)(|d|+1)}

\displaystyle(|d|+(M+N+2)(|d|+1))^{(M+N+2)(|d|+1)}\prod_{j=1}^{m}(N+2)^{a_{j}}(M+2)^{a^{\prime}_{j}}

(8.70)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle m!4^{24(|d|+m+1)}|d|!\times

\displaystyle(4(M+N))^{5(M+N)(|d|+1)}(((N+M+2)(|d|+1))^{2(|d|+1)(N+M+2)^{2}})^{2}\times

\displaystyle(a|d|)^{2(M+N+2)(|d|+1)}\left(2a(N+2)\right)^{2(M+N+2)(|d|+1)+(M+N+2)(|d|+1)(N+M)-2(M+N+2)(|d|+1)}

\displaystyle((M+N+3)(|d|+1))^{(M+N+2)(|d|+1)}\prod_{j=1}^{m}(N+2)^{a_{j}}(M+2)^{a^{\prime}_{j}}.

(8.71)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle m!4^{24(|d|+m+1)}|d|!\times

\displaystyle(4(M+N))^{5(M+N)(|d|+1)}(((N+M+2)(|d|+1))^{2(|d|+1)(N+M+2)^{2}})^{2}\times

\displaystyle(a|d|)^{2(M+N+2)(|d|+1)}\left(2a(N+2)\right)^{3(M+N+2)^{2}(|d|+1)}\times

\displaystyle((M+N+3)(|d|+1))^{(M+N+2)(|d|+1)}\prod_{j=1}^{m}(N+2)^{a_{j}}(M+2)^{a^{\prime}_{j}}.

(8.72)

\displaystyle\overset{\textnormal{\eqref{eqnreallreallyterribleinequality}}}{\leqslant}((m+4+24(|d|+m+1))+|d|+4(M+N)+5(M+N)(|d|+1)

\displaystyle+2((N+M+2)(|d|+1)+2(|d|+1)(N+M+2)^{2})+a|d|+2(M+N+2)(|d|+1)+

\displaystyle(2a(N+1))+3(M+N+2)^{2}(|d|+1)+(M+N+3)(|d|+1)+(M+N+2)(|d|+1))!

\displaystyle\prod_{j=1}^{m}(N+2)^{a_{j}}(M+2)^{a^{\prime}_{j}}

(8.73)

\displaystyle\leqslant(25m+4+(24+1+4+5+2+4+a+2+2a+3+1+1)(M+N+3)^{2}(|d|+1))!

\displaystyle\times\prod_{j=1}^{m}(N+2)^{a_{j}}(M+2)^{a^{\prime}_{j}}.

(8.74)

\displaystyle\leqslant(25m+(51+3a)(M+N+3)^{2}(|d|+1))!\prod_{j=1}^{m}(N+2)^{a_{j}}(M+2)^{a^{\prime}_{j}}.

Hence, we get the following proposition:

Proposition 8.10
.

(8.75)

\displaystyle|\textnormal{\eqref{eqngromovwitteninvariantbdd}}|\leqslant(25m+(51+3a)(M+N+3)^{2}(|d|+1))!\prod_{j=1}^{m}(N+2)^{a_{j}}(M+2)^{a^{\prime}_{j}}.

8.3.
D
-Volume Bounds in Products of Projective Space

Let us fix the notation from the last subsection.

Proposition 8.11
.

Let
h^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
satisfy
h^{\prime}\geqslant a+2(N+1)(M+1)
and suppose
D
is a very general divisor representing
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}^{M}}(h^{\prime},h^{\prime})
.
Recall that
T=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}^{M}}(a,0)^{b}
for some
b\in\mathbb{N}
.
Then

(8.76)

\displaystyle D\textnormal{-vol}(T_{m,d})\leqslant(b+(52+3a)(M+N+3)^{2}(|d|+1)+(N+M+2h^{\prime}+30)m)!/(abd_{1}+b)!

Proof.

The dimension of
P_{0,d}
is at most
\ell:=(N+M+2)(|d|+1)
.
We have for each
i_{1},\cdots,i_{m}\in\mathbb{N}
that

(8.77)

\langle D^{i_{1}},\cdots,D^{i_{m}}\rangle_{m,d}^{s(T_{\bullet})}=

(8.78)

\langle D^{i_{1}},\cdots,D^{i_{m}};c(\mathcal{O}(a,0)^{b})^{-1}\rangle^{\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}^{M}}_{m,d}

(8.79)

=\langle D^{i_{1}},\cdots,D^{i_{m}};(1+c_{1}(\mathcal{O}(a,0)))^{-b}\rangle^{\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}^{M}}_{m,d}

(8.80)

=\sum_{k=0}^{\ell}(-1)^{k}{k+b-1\choose k}\langle D^{i_{1}},\cdots,D^{i_{m}};c_{1}(\mathcal{O}(a,0))^{k}\rangle^{\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}^{M}}_{m,d}.

Hence, by Proposition
8.10
,

(8.81)

\sum_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{m}=0}^{NM}\left|\langle D^{i_{1}},\cdots,D^{i_{m}}\rangle_{m,d}^{s(T_{\bullet})}\right|

(8.82)

\displaystyle\leqslant(NM+1)^{m}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\ell}{k+b-1\choose k}\right)

\displaystyle\times(25m+(51+3a)(M+N+3)^{2}(|d|+1))!\prod_{j=1}^{m}(N+2)^{h^{\prime}}(M+2)^{h^{\prime}}

(8.83)

\displaystyle\leqslant(NM+1)^{m}2^{\ell+b-1}\times(25m+(51+3a)(M+N+3)^{2}(|d|+1))!\prod_{j=1}^{m}(N+2)^{h^{\prime}}(M+2)^{h^{\prime}}

(8.84)

\displaystyle\overset{\textnormal{\eqref{eqnreallreallyterribleinequality}}}{\leqslant}(NM+1+m+2+\ell+b-1+

\displaystyle 25m+(51+3a)(M+N+3)^{2}(|d|+1)+(N+2+h^{\prime}+M+2+h^{\prime})m)!

(8.85)

\leqslant(b+\ell+26m+NM+2+(51+3a)(M+N+3)^{2}(|d|+1)+(N+M+2h^{\prime}+4)m)!

(8.86)

\displaystyle=(b+(N+M+2)(|d|+1)+26m+NM+2+

\displaystyle(51+3a)(M+N+3)^{2}(|d|+1)+(N+M+2h^{\prime}+4)m)!

(8.87)

\leqslant(b+(52+3a)(M+N+3)^{2}(|d|+1)+(N+M+2h^{\prime}+30)m)!

Since
D
is the restriction of a very general divisor of degree
h^{\prime}
pulled back via the Segre embedding

(8.88)

\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}^{M}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{(N+1)(N+1)-1},

we have that
D
is
T
-large by Lemma
4.15
.
Our result now follows from Proposition
5.14
.
∎

9.
Main Argument

In this section we prove the main theorem of this paper, Theorem
1.1
. We will first construct bounded partial resolutions of the normal bundle of our variety in projective space by sums of twists of the structure sheaf. Such bounds depend on the Castelnuovo-Mumford regularity of the defining ideal sheaf and its square. We use such bounds to embed the projective compactification of the normal bundle into products of projective space. After that we assemble all the ingredients together to prove the main theorem.
Throughout this section we will fix the following notation:

(1)

We let
X\subset\mathbb{P}^{N}
be a smooth projective subvariety with defining ideal sheaf
\mathfrak{I}_{X}
.

(2)

We let
\iota_{X}:X\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{N}
be the corresponding closed immersion.

(3)

We let
\omega
be the restriction of the Fubini Study form on
\mathbb{P}^{N}
to
X
.

Definition 9.1
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a sheaf on
\mathbb{P}^{N}
.
We say that
\mathcal{F}
is
r
-regular
or
r
-regular in the sense of Castelnuovo-Mumford
if

(9.1)

H^{i}(\mathcal{F}(r-i))=0

for each
i\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}
.
Define
\textnormal{reg}(\mathcal{F})\in\mathbb{N}
to be the infimum over all
r\in\mathbb{N}
so that
\mathcal{F}
is
r
-regular.

Proposition 9.2
.

(Castelnuovo)
[
Mum16
, Lecture 14]
.
If a sheaf
\mathcal{F}
is
r
-regular then it is
k
-regular for any
k\geqslant r
.

Proposition 9.3
.

(Castelnuovo)
[
Mum16
, Lecture 14]
.
If a coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}
is
r
-regular then
\mathcal{F}(k)
is globally generated for each
k\geqslant r
.

Lemma 9.4
.

Let
0\to\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{H}\to 0

be a short exact sequence of sheaves on
\mathbb{P}^{N}
.
Then

(9.2)

\textnormal{reg}(\mathcal{H})\leqslant\max(\textnormal{reg}(\mathcal{G}),\textnormal{reg}(\mathcal{F})-1),

(9.3)

\textnormal{reg}(\mathcal{G})\leqslant\max(\textnormal{reg}(\mathcal{F}),\textnormal{reg}(\mathcal{H})).

Also, if the induced map
H^{0}(\mathcal{G}(r-1))\to H^{0}(\mathcal{H}(r-1))
is surjective for some
r\geqslant\max(\textnormal{reg}(\mathcal{H})+1,\textnormal{reg}(\mathcal{G}))
then

(9.4)

\textnormal{reg}(\mathcal{F})\leqslant r.

Proof.

Let
r_{\mathcal{H}}=\max(\textnormal{reg}(\mathcal{G}),\textnormal{reg}(\mathcal{F})-1)
,
r_{\mathcal{G}}=\max(\textnormal{reg}(\mathcal{F}),\textnormal{reg}(\mathcal{H}))

and
r_{\mathcal{F}}=\max(\textnormal{reg}(\mathcal{H})+1,\textnormal{reg}(\mathcal{G}))
.
We have an exact sequence:

(9.5)

H^{i}(\mathcal{F}(r_{\mathcal{G}}-i))\to H^{i}(\mathcal{G}(r_{\mathcal{G}}-i))\to H^{i}(\mathcal{H}(r_{\mathcal{G}}-i)).

The outer terms vanish for all
i>0
by Proposition
9.2
and hence the middle term does. This proves (
9.3
).

We have an exact sequence:

(9.6)

H^{i}(\mathcal{G}(r_{\mathcal{H}}-i))\to H^{i}(\mathcal{H}(r_{\mathcal{H}}-i))\to H^{i+1}(\mathcal{F}(r_{\mathcal{H}}+1-(i+1))).

Again, the outer terms vanish for each
i>0
and hence middle term does. This proves (
9.2
).

Let
r
be as in the statement of this lemma.
We have an exact sequence

(9.7)

H^{i-1}(\mathcal{H}(r-1-(i-1)))\to H^{i}(\mathcal{F}(r-i))\to H^{i}(\mathcal{G}(r-i)).

The outer terms vanish for each
i>1
since
r\geqslant r_{\mathcal{F}}
and hence so does the middle term. We only need to consider the case
i=1
. In this case we have an exact sequence:

(9.8)

H^{0}(\mathcal{G}(r-1))\overset{f}{\longrightarrow}H^{0}(\mathcal{H}(r-1))\to H^{1}(\mathcal{F}(r-1))\to H^{1}(\mathcal{G}(r-1)).

Since
f
is surjective by assumption, we get
H^{1}(\mathcal{F}(r-1))=0
since
r\geqslant\textnormal{reg}(\mathcal{G})
.
∎

Lemma 9.5
.

Let
K
be an
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}
-submodule of
(\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\ell})^{\oplus k}
containing
(\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\ell+1})^{\oplus k}
for some
k,\ell\in\mathbb{N}
. Suppose that
K
and
\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\ell+1}
are
r
-regular for some
r\in\mathbb{N}
.
Then
K/(\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\ell+1})^{\oplus k}
is
r
-regular and

(9.9)

\dim H^{0}((K/(\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\ell+1})^{\oplus k})(r))\leqslant k{N+r\choose N}.

Proof.

We have a short exact sequence

(9.10)

0\to(\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\ell+1})^{\oplus k}\to K\to K/(\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\ell+1})^{\oplus k}\to 0.

Then by Lemma
9.4
we get that
K/(\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\ell+1})^{\oplus k}
is
r
-regular.
We have that
H^{0}(K(r))\subset H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}^{\oplus k}(r))
.
Hence,

(9.11)

\dim H^{0}(K(r))\leqslant\dim H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}^{\oplus k}(r))=k{N+r\choose r}.

Since
H^{1}((\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\ell+1})^{\oplus k}(r))=0
by Proposition
9.2
we get
H^{0}(K(r))\to H^{0}((K/(\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\ell+1})^{\oplus k})(r))
is surjective.
Hence,

(9.12)

\dim H^{0}((K/(\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\ell+1})^{\oplus k})(r))\leqslant\dim H^{0}(K(r))\overset{\textnormal{\eqref{eqndimnplusrbounds}}}{\leqslant}k{N+r\choose r}.

∎

Lemma 9.6
.

Let
K
be a coherent subsheaf of
(\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\ell})^{\oplus k}
containing
(\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\ell+1})^{\oplus k}
for some
k,\ell\in\mathbb{N}
. Suppose that
K
and
\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\ell+1}
are
r
-regular for some
r\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
h\geqslant k{N+r\choose N}
.
Then there is a coherent subsheaf
K^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}^{\oplus h}
containing
\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\oplus h}
which is
1
-regular together with a short exact sequence:

(9.13)

0\to K^{\prime}/\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\oplus h}\to(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}/\mathfrak{I}_{X})^{\oplus h}\to(K/(\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\ell+1})^{\oplus k})(r)\to 0.

Proof.

By Lemma
9.5
we have
K/(\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\ell+1})^{\oplus k}
is
r
-regular and Equation (
9.9
) holds.
Therefore, by Proposition
9.3
there is a surjection

(9.14)

f:\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}^{\oplus h}\to(K/(\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\ell+1})^{\oplus k})(r)

which is also a surjection on
H^{0}
. Let
K^{\prime}
be in the kernel of
f
.
Since
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}^{\oplus h}
is
0
-regular by
[
Gro61
, Proposition 2.1.12]
,
(K/(\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\ell+1})^{\oplus k})(r)
is
0
-regular
and
H^{0}(f)
is surjective, we have by Lemma
9.4

that
K^{\prime}
is
1
-regular.
Since
K
is a subsheaf of
(\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\ell})^{\oplus k}
, we get that
\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\oplus h}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}^{\oplus h}
gets sent to
0
under
f
.
Therefore, we have an induced surjection

(9.15)

(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}/\mathfrak{I}_{X})^{\oplus h}\to(K/(\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\ell+1})^{\oplus k})(r).

The kernel of this map is
K^{\prime}/\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\oplus h}
.
Hence, we have an exact sequence (
9.13
).
∎

Lemma 9.7
.

Suppose that
\mathfrak{I}_{X}
,
\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\ell}
and
\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\ell+1}
are
r
-regular for some
r\in\mathbb{N}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
r_{1}\geqslant r
and
r_{2},r_{3}\geqslant 1
.
Let
a_{1},a_{2},a_{3}\in\mathbb{N}
satisfy

(9.16)

a_{1}\geqslant{N+r_{1}\choose N},\ a_{2}\geqslant a_{1}{N+r_{2}\choose N},\ a_{3}\geqslant a_{2}{N+r_{3}\choose N}.

Then there is an exact sequence of sheaves:

(9.17)

\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\oplus a_{3}}(-r_{1}-r_{2}-r_{3})\to\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\oplus a_{2}}(-r_{1}-r_{2})\to\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\oplus a_{1}}(-r_{1})\to\iota_{X}^{*}(\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\ell})\to 0.

Proof.

By Lemma
9.6
, there exists a coherent subsheaf
K_{1}
of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}^{\oplus a_{1}}
containing
\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{a_{1}}
which is
1
-regular
together with a short exact sequence:

(9.18)

0\to K_{1}/\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\oplus a_{1}}\to(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}/\mathfrak{I}_{X})^{\oplus a_{1}}\to(\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\ell}/\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\ell+1})(r_{1})\to 0.

By Proposition
9.2
,
K_{1}
and
\mathfrak{I}_{X}
are
r_{2}
-regular. Hence, by Lemma
9.6
there exists a coherent subsheaf
K_{2}
of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}^{\oplus a_{2}}
containing
\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{a_{2}}
which is
1
-regular together with a short exact sequence:

(9.19)

0\to K_{2}/\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\oplus a_{2}}\to(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}/\mathfrak{I}_{X})^{\oplus a_{2}}\to\left(K_{1}/\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\oplus a_{1}}\right)(r_{2})\to 0.

By Proposition
9.2
,
K_{2}
and
\mathfrak{I}_{X}
are
r_{3}
-regular. Hence, by Lemma
9.6
there exists a coherent subsheaf
K_{3}
of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}^{\oplus a_{3}}
containing
\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{a_{3}}
which is
1
-regular together with a short exact sequence:

(9.20)

0\to K_{3}/\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\oplus a_{3}}\to(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}/\mathfrak{I}_{X})^{\oplus a_{3}}\to\left(K_{2}/\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\oplus a_{2}}\right)(r_{3})\to 0.

By combining (
9.18
),(
9.19
) and (
9.20
) we get a long exact sequence:

(9.21)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}/\mathfrak{I}_{X})^{\oplus a_{3}}\to(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}/\mathfrak{I}_{X})^{\oplus a_{2}}(r_{3})\to

\displaystyle(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}/\mathfrak{I}_{X})^{\oplus a_{1}}(r_{3}+r_{2})\to\left(\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\ell}/\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\ell+1}\right)(r_{3}+r_{2}+r_{1})\to 0.

Hence, we get a long exact sequence:

(9.22)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}/\mathfrak{I}_{X})^{\oplus a_{3}}(-r_{1}-r_{2}-r_{3})\to(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}/\mathfrak{I}_{X})^{\oplus a_{2}}(-r_{1}-r_{2})

\displaystyle\to(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}/\mathfrak{I}_{X})^{\oplus a_{1}}(-r_{1})\to\left(\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\ell}/\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{\ell+1}\right)\to 0.

By
[
Sta18
, Lemma 04CJ]
this restricts to an exact sequence (
9.17
).
∎

Lemma 9.8
.

Suppose that
\mathfrak{I}_{X}
and
\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{2}
are
r
-regular for some
r>0
.
Then there is an exact sequence of sheaves

(9.23)

0\to V\overset{e_{V}}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{O}_{X}(r)^{{N+r\choose N}}\to\mathcal{O}_{X}(2r)^{{N+r\choose N}^{2}}\to\mathcal{O}_{X}(3r)^{{N+r\choose N}^{3}}

on
X

where
V
is the normal bundle of
X
.

Proof.

By Lemma
9.7
we get an exact sequence:

(9.24)

\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\oplus{N+r\choose N}^{3}}(-3r)\to\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\oplus{N+r\choose N}^{2}}(-2r)\to\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\oplus{N+r\choose N}}(-r)\to V^{*}\to 0.

We then have that (
9.23
) is the dual of the sequence above.
∎

Lemma 9.9
.

Suppose that
\mathfrak{I}_{X}
is
r
-regular for some
r>0
.
Then there is an exact sequence

(9.25)

0\to\mathcal{O}_{X}\overset{}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{O}_{X}(2r)^{{N+r\choose N}^{2}}\overset{}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{O}_{X}(3r)^{{N+r\choose N}^{3}}

Proof.

We have
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}
is
r
-regular by
[
Gro61
, Proposition 2.1.12]
combined with Proposition
9.2
.
Hence, by Lemma
9.7
we get an exact sequence:

(9.26)

\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\oplus{N+r\choose N}^{3}}(-3r)\to\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\oplus{N+r\choose N}^{2}}(-2r)\to\mathcal{O}_{X}\to 0.

We then have that (
9.25
) is the dual of the sequence above.
∎

Let us now fix
r\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}
so that
\mathfrak{I}_{X}
and
\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{2}
are
r
-regular.
Let
W
be the cokernel of the map
e_{V}
from the exact sequence (
9.23
).
This is a vector bundle since
e_{V}
is injective and its domain and codomain are vector bundles.
Define

(9.27)

\mu:={N+r\choose N}.

By (
9.23
) and (
9.25
), we have an exact sequence:

(9.28)

0\to W\oplus\mathcal{O}_{X}\overset{e_{W}}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{O}_{X}(2r)^{2\mu^{2}}\to\mathcal{O}_{X}(3r)^{2\mu^{3}}.

The map
e_{W}
in (
9.28
) induces the following closed immersion:

(9.29)

\iota_{P_{W}}:P_{W}\overset{}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{O}_{X}(2r)^{2\mu^{2}})\cong X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}

where
P_{W}
is as in Definition
4.7
.
As a result we have a closed immersion

(9.30)

f:P_{W}\overset{\iota_{P_{W}}}{\longrightarrow}X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}\overset{\iota_{X}\times\mathrm{id}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}.

We let

(9.31)

\widetilde{V}\to X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}

be the normal bundle of
X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}
inside
\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}
.
This is the pullback of
V
to
X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}
via the natural projection map to
X
.
Let

(9.32)

\widetilde{Q}\to P_{W}

be the normal bundle of
\iota_{P_{W}}(P_{W})
inside
X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}
.
Let us now compute this bundle.
The map
\iota_{P_{W}}
corresponds to the exact sequence:

(9.33)

0\to W(-2r)\oplus\mathcal{O}_{X}(-2r)\overset{e_{W}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(-2r)}}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{O}_{X}^{2\mu^{2}}.

Let
Q^{\prime}
be the cokernel of
e_{W}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(-2r)}
.
Then

(9.34)

\widetilde{Q}\cong\pi_{P_{W}}^{*}Q^{\prime}\otimes f^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}}(0,1).

We also have that:

(9.35)

Q^{\prime}\cong Q\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(-2r)

where
Q
is the cokernel of the map
e_{W}
.
Hence,

(9.36)

\widetilde{Q}\cong\pi_{P_{W}}^{*}(Q\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(-2r))\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}}(0,1)|_{P_{W}}=\pi_{P_{W}}^{*}Q\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}}(-2r,1)|_{P_{W}}.

Definition 9.10
.

Define

\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}}(a,b):=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}}(a,b)

and
\mathcal{O}_{P_{W}}(a,b):=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}}(a,b)|_{P_{W}}

for each
a,b\in\mathbb{Z}
.
We let
\pi_{X}:X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}\to X
be the natural projection map.
Define
e_{X}
to be the natural composition:

(9.37)

e_{X}:X\hookrightarrow W\hookrightarrow P_{W}.

Define
d_{P_{W}}=(e_{X})_{*}d
.
Also, we define
T:=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}}(r,0)^{\mu}
.
We let

d_{X\times\mathbb{P}}
be the image of
d
in
H_{2}^{+}(X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1})

and
d_{\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}}
its image in
H_{2}^{+}(\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1})
.

Lemma 9.11
.

We have

(9.38)

f^{*}(\mathcal{O}_{P_{W}})(a,b)=\pi_{P_{W}}^{*}(\mathcal{O}_{X}(a+2br))\otimes\mathcal{O}(\infty_{W})^{\otimes b}

and

(9.39)

e_{X}^{*}f^{*}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}})(a,b)=\mathcal{O}_{X}(a+2br)

for each
a,b\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Proof.

Because
\pi_{X}\circ f=\pi_{P_{W}}
, we have

(9.40)

f^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}}(1,0)=\pi_{P_{W}}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}(1).

Also, since Equation (
9.33
) is the map inducing the inclusion map
\iota_{P_{W}}
, we have

(9.41)

f^{*}\mathcal{O}_{P_{W}}(0,1)=\pi_{P_{W}}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}(2r)\otimes\mathcal{O}(\infty_{W}).

Equation (
9.38
) now follows from (
9.40
), (
9.41
) and linearity.
Equation (
9.39
) also holds because
e_{X}^{*}\mathcal{O}(\infty_{W})=\mathcal{O}_{X}
and
\pi_{P_{W}}\circ e_{X}=\mathrm{id}_{X}
.
∎

Lemma 9.12
.

We have the following identifications:

(9.42)

f^{*}T\cong\pi_{P_{W}}^{*}(f|_{X}^{*}T)

(9.43)

\widetilde{V}\cong\pi_{X}^{*}(e_{X}^{*}\iota_{P_{W}}^{*}\widetilde{V})

(9.44)

\widetilde{Q}|_{W}\cong\pi_{P_{W}}^{*}(e_{X}^{*}\widetilde{Q})|_{W}\cong(\pi_{P_{W}}^{*}Q)|_{W}.

Also, for each
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
,
m\in\mathbb{N}
, we have an identification

(9.45)

C^{X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}/\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}}_{m,d_{X\times\mathbb{P}}}|_{W}\cong(\pi_{P_{W}})_{m,d}^{*}C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}}_{m,d}|_{W}

induced by the natural identification

(9.46)

(\widetilde{V}|_{P_{W}})_{m,d_{P_{W}}}\cong(\pi_{P_{W}})_{m,d}^{*}V_{m,d}

where

(9.47)

(\pi_{P_{W}})_{m,d}:(\widetilde{V}|_{P_{W}})_{m,d_{P_{W}}}\to V_{m,d}

is the map of moduli spaces induced by
\pi_{P_{W}}
.
We also have a natural identification

(9.48)

C^{X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}/\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}}_{m,d_{X\times\mathbb{P}}}|_{X}\cong C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}}_{m,d}.

Proof.

Equation (
9.42
) follows from Lemma
9.11
.
We have that Equation (
9.43
) holds because
V=e_{X}^{*}\iota_{P_{W}}^{*}\widetilde{V}
.
We have

(9.49)

\mathrm{id}_{X}=\pi_{P_{W}}\circ e_{X}.

Equation (
9.44
) holds by Lemma
9.11
combined with Equations (
9.36
) and (
9.49
) and the fact that
\infty_{W}
has support disjoint from
W
.

Let

(9.50)

(\pi_{X})_{m,d}:\widetilde{V}_{m,d_{X\times\mathbb{P}}}\to V_{m,d}

be the map of moduli spaces induced by
\pi_{X}
.
Let
U\subset(X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1})_{m,d_{X\times\mathbb{P}}}
be the subspace of curves which are still stable after post composing them with
\pi_{X}
.
Recall that
\mathfrak{M}_{m}
is the moduli stack of genus zero pre-stable curves with
m
marked points.
Because the moduli space of prestable curves on a product is the fiber product over
\mathfrak{M}_{m}
of the respective moduli spaces on each factor, we have an identification

(9.51)

C^{X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}/\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}}_{m,d_{X\times\mathbb{P}}}|_{U}\cong(\pi_{X})_{m,d}^{*}C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}}_{m,d}|_{U}.

Equation (
9.45
)
now holds since
\pi_{X}\circ\iota_{P_{W}}=\pi_{P_{W}}
.
Also Equation (
9.48
) holds by (
9.51
) combined with
\pi_{X}\circ\iota_{P_{W}}\circ e_{X}=\mathrm{id}_{X}
.
∎

Lemma 9.13
.

We have that
V
,
W
,
Q
and
\widetilde{Q}|_{W}
are strongly convex (Definition
4.16
).
Also, we have
c_{1}(\widetilde{Q})(d_{p_{W}})=c_{1}(Q)(d)
and

(9.52)

f_{*}(d_{P_{W}})=(\deg(d),2r\deg(d))\in\mathbb{N}^{2}\cong H_{2}^{+}(\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1})

for each
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)

where

\deg(d)
is the degree of the image of
d
in
H_{2}^{+}(\mathbb{P}^{N})\cong\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

We have surjections
T\mathbb{P}^{N}|_{X}\twoheadrightarrow V
,

\mathcal{O}_{X}(r)^{\mu}\twoheadrightarrow W
and
\mathcal{O}_{X}(2r)^{2\mu^{2}}\to Q
.
Hence, by Lemma
4.17
, we get that
V
,
W
and
Q
are strongly convex.
Lemma
9.12
then tells us that
\widetilde{Q}|_{W}
is also strongly convex.
By Equation (
9.49
), we have
(\pi_{P_{W}})_{*}(d_{P_{W}})=d
.
Hence, by Lemma
9.12
, we have

c_{1}(\widetilde{Q})(d_{p_{W}})=c_{1}(Q)(d)
.
Equation (
9.52
)
follows from Lemma
9.11
.

∎

Lemma 9.14
.

Let
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
.
We have that

C^{P_{W}/X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}}_{m,d_{P_{W}}}|_{W}
is equal to

\widetilde{Q}_{m,d_{P_{W}}}|_{W}
(See Definition
4.20
).

Proof.

Let
T^{\mathrm{ver}}P_{W}=\mathrm{ker}(d\pi_{P_{W}})
and
T^{\mathrm{ver}}(X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1})=X\times T\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}
be the vertical tangent bundles of
P_{W}
and
X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}
with respect to the natural projection maps to
X
.
Let
\mathcal{M}\subset(X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1})_{m,d_{P_{W}}}
be the open subspace consisting of those curves
u
that are lifts of ones
v
from
X_{m,d}
and with the additional property that non-constant components of
u
are lifts of non-constant components of
v
.
We have

(9.53)

T^{\mathrm{ver}}P_{W}|_{W}\cong\pi_{P_{W}}^{*}W|_{W}.

Hence,
T^{\mathrm{ver}}P_{W}|_{W}
is convex by Lemma
9.13
. Also,
T^{\mathrm{ver}}(X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1})
is convex.

Hence,
W_{m,d}
and
\mathcal{M}

are smooth over
X_{m,d}
.
This implies that the natural inclusion map

(9.54)

W_{m,d}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}\subset(X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1})_{m,d_{P_{W}}}

is a regular immersion.
Hence,
C^{P_{W}/X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}}_{m,d_{P_{W}}}|_{W}
is equal to the normal bundle of
W_{m,d}
inside
(X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1})_{m,d_{P_{W}}}
.
Since
\widetilde{Q}|_{W}
is convex as well by Lemma
9.13
, we have that this normal bundle is also identified with
\widetilde{Q}_{m,d_{P_{W}}}|_{W}
giving our result.
∎

Lemma 9.15
.

For each irreducible curve
C
in
X
we have

(9.55)

c_{1}(V)(C)\leqslant\mu r\deg(C),

(9.56)

c_{1}(W)(C)\leqslant\mu r\deg(C)

(9.57)

c_{1}(Q)(C)\leqslant 4r\mu^{2}\deg(C).

Proof.

We have a short exact sequence:

(9.58)

0\to V\to\mathcal{O}_{X}(r)^{\mu}\to W\to 0.

Hence,

(9.59)

c_{1}(V)(C)+c_{1}(W)(C)=\mu r\deg(C).

Since
V
and
W
are strongly convex by Lemma
9.13
,
we get that
c_{1}(V)(C)\geqslant 0
and
c_{1}(W)(C)\geqslant 0
.
Hence, by Equation (
9.59
), we get
Equations (
9.55
) and (
9.56
) hold.

We have a short exact sequence
0\to W\oplus\mathcal{O}_{X}\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}(2r)^{2\mu^{2}}\to Q\to 0
and so

(9.60)

c_{1}(W)(C)+c_{1}(Q)(C)=4r\mu^{2}\deg(C).

Since
W
and
Q
are strongly convex by Lemma
9.13
, we have
c_{1}(W)(C)\geqslant 0

and
c_{1}(W)(Q)\geqslant 0
.
Hence, by Equation (
9.60
), we get
Equation (
9.57
) holds.
∎

Lemma 9.16
.

Suppose
N>0
.
Let
D^{\prime}_{T}
,
D^{\prime}_{\widetilde{V}}
,
D^{\prime}_{W}
,
D^{\prime}_{\widetilde{Q}}
,
D
,
E
,
F
,
F^{\prime}
be very general divisors corresponding to the line bundles:

(9.61)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}}(r,1),\ \mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}}(r,1),\ \mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}}(2r,0),\ \mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}}(r,1),

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}}(72\mu^{4},72\mu^{4}),\ \mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}}(36\mu^{4},36\mu^{4}),\ \mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}}(12\mu^{3},12\mu^{3}),\ \mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}}(3\mu^{3},3\mu^{3})

respectively.
Then

(1)

T^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(D^{\prime}_{T})
,
\widetilde{V}^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(D^{\prime}_{\widetilde{V}}|_{X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}})
,
W^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(D^{\prime}_{W}|_{X})
,
\widetilde{Q}^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(D^{\prime}_{\widetilde{Q}}|_{P_{W}})
,
V^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(F^{\prime}|_{X})
,
T^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(F^{\prime})
and
W^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(F^{\prime}|_{X})
are globally generated,

(2)

D-D^{\prime}_{T}
is
\mathrm{Bl}_{X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}}(\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1})
-compatible and its tensor product with the ideal sheaf defining
X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}
in
\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}
is globally generated,

(3)

(D-D^{\prime}_{T}-D^{\prime}_{\widetilde{V}})|_{X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}}
is
\mathrm{Bl}_{P_{W}}(X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1})
-compatible and also its tensor product with the ideal sheaf defining
P_{W}
in
X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}
is globally generated,

(4)

the following divisors are nef:

(9.62)

\displaystyle D-D^{\prime}_{T},\quad D-D^{\prime}_{T}-D^{\prime}_{\widetilde{V}},\quad D-E-D^{\prime}_{\widetilde{Q}},

\displaystyle E-D^{\prime}_{T}-D^{\prime}_{\widetilde{V}}-D^{\prime}_{\widetilde{Q}},\quad F-D^{\prime}_{T}-D^{\prime}_{\widetilde{V}}-D^{\prime}_{W},\quad E|_{P_{W}}-\pi_{W}^{*}(F|_{X})-\infty_{W},

(5)

D|_{P_{W}}
is
\widetilde{Q}
-large and

(6)

F|_{X}
is
V,T,W
-large.

Proof.

We have
T^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(D^{\prime}_{T})=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}\otimes\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}}(0,1)^{\mu}
and so is globally generated.
By Equation (
9.23
)
combined with the fact that
\widetilde{V}
is the pullback of
V
via the projection map to
X
, we have an exact sequence:

(9.63)

\mathcal{O}_{X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}}^{\mu}\to\widetilde{V}^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}}(r,0)\to 0.

Hence,
\widetilde{V}^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(D^{\prime}_{\widetilde{V}}|_{X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}})
is globally generated.
Since
W
is the cokernel of the map
e_{V}
from (
9.8
), we get an exact sequence:

(9.64)

\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\mu^{2}}\to W^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(2r)\to 0

and hence
W^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(D^{\prime}_{W}|_{X})
is globally generated since
D^{\prime}_{W}|_{X}=\mathcal{O}_{X}(2r)
by Lemma
9.11
.

By (
9.28
), we have an exact sequence:

(9.65)

0\to Q\to\mathcal{O}_{X}(3r)^{2\mu^{3}}.

Taking duals and tensoring with
\mathcal{O}_{X}(3r)
gives an exact sequence:

(9.66)

\mathcal{O}_{X}^{2\mu^{3}}\to Q^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(3r)\to 0.

Hence, we have an exact sequence:

(9.67)

\mathcal{O}_{P_{W}}^{2\mu^{3}}\to\pi_{P_{W}}^{*}Q^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{P_{W}}(3r,0)\to 0.

By (
9.36
) we get:

(9.68)

\widetilde{Q}^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{P_{W}}(-2r,1)=\pi_{P_{W}}^{*}Q^{*}.

Combining (
9.67
) with (
9.68
),
we get the exact sequence

(9.69)

\mathcal{O}_{P_{W}}^{2\mu^{3}}\to\widetilde{Q}^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{P_{W}}(r,1)\to 0.

Hence,
\widetilde{Q}^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(D^{\prime}_{\widetilde{Q}}|_{P_{W}})
is globally generated.

Since
N>0
, we have

(9.70)

\mu\geqslant\max(N,r).

Equations (
9.70
) and (
9.23
) and Lemma
9.11
tell us that
V^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(F^{\prime}|_{X})
is globally generated.
We have
T^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(F^{\prime}|_{X})=\mathcal{O}_{X}(4\mu^{3}-r)^{\mu}
is globally generated by Equation (
9.70
) too.
Equations (
9.70
) and (
9.64
) tell us that
W^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}(F^{\prime}|_{X})
is globally generated.

Because
\mathfrak{I}_{X}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(r)
is globally generated by Proposition
9.3

we get that
\mathcal{O}_{X}(r)
is
\mathrm{Bl}_{X}\mathbb{P}^{N}
-compatible by Lemma
6.3
.
Hence,
D-D^{\prime}_{T}
is
\mathrm{Bl}_{X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}}(\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1})
-compatible and also this divisor and its tensor product with the ideal sheaf defining
X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}
in
\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}
is globally generated.

Let
\mathfrak{I}_{P_{W}}
be the ideal sheaf defining
P_{W}
inside
X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}
.
Let
\mathcal{F}=\mathfrak{I}_{P_{W}}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}}(r,1)
.
Since
\mathcal{F}
is the kernel of the natural map

(9.71)

\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}(W(-3r)\oplus\mathcal{O}_{X}(-3r))}(1)\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{O}_{X}^{2\mu^{2}}(-r))}(1),

induced by
e_{W}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(-3r)}
, we get
that

(9.72)

(\pi_{X})_{*}(\mathcal{F})=\mathrm{ker}(e_{W}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(-3r)})=Q^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(3r)

since pushforward is left exact.
Therefore, since
Q^{*}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(3r)
is globally generated by (
9.66
),
we get by (
9.72
) that

(\pi_{X})_{*}(\mathcal{F})
is globally generated.
Also,

(9.73)

\pi_{X}^{*}(\pi_{X})_{*}\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{F}

is surjective since it is surjective at the level of fibers and by Nakayama’s lemma.
Combining the previous two facts
gives us
that
\mathcal{F}
is globally generated.
Hence,

(D-D^{\prime}_{T}-D^{\prime}_{\widetilde{V}})|_{X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}}

is
\mathrm{Bl}_{P_{W}}(X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1})
-compatible
by Lemma
6.3
. Also, its tensor product with
\mathfrak{I}_{P_{W}}
is globally generated.

We have
\mathcal{O}(\infty_{W})=\mathcal{O}_{P_{W}}(-2r,1)
by Lemma
9.11
.
We have
F|_{X}=\mathcal{O}_{X}(12\mu^{3}+24\mu^{3}r)
by Lemma
9.11
too.
Since
\pi_{P_{W}}=\pi_{X}\circ\iota_{P_{W}}
, this implies
\pi_{P_{W}}^{*}(F|_{X})=\mathcal{O}_{P_{W}}(12\mu^{3}+24\mu^{3}r,0)
.
Combining this with Equation (
9.70
), we get that the divisors in (
9.62
) are all nef.

Let
C
be an irreducible curve in
X
whose normalization has genus zero.
Since
N>0
and by (
9.70
), we have

(9.74)

8\mu^{3}\geqslant 1+2(2(N+1)\mu^{2}-1).

Hence, by Lemma
9.15
, we have

(9.75)

(12\mu^{3}-1-2(2(N+1)\mu^{2}-1))\deg(C)\geqslant 2+c_{1}(V)(C),

(9.76)

(12\mu^{3}-1-2(2(N+1)\mu^{2}-1))\deg(C)\geqslant 2+c_{1}(W)(C)

and

(9.77)

(72\mu^{3}-1-2(2(N+1)\mu^{2}-1))\deg(C)\geqslant 2+c_{1}(Q)(C).

Therefore, by Equation (
9.36
)

(9.78)

(72\mu^{3}-1-2(2(N+1)\mu^{2}-1))\deg(C)\geqslant 2+c_{1}(\widetilde{Q})(C).

Also,

(9.79)

(12\mu^{3}-1-2(2(N+1)\mu^{2}-1))\deg(C)\geqslant 2+r\deg(C)=2+c_{1}(T)(C).

Let
\phi:\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{2(N+1)\mu^{2}-1}

be the Segre embedding.
Since
D
is the restriction of a very general divisor in
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{2(N+1)\mu^{2}-1}}(72\mu^{4})
,
we have by Equation (
9.78
) combined with Lemma
4.15
,
that
D|_{P_{W}}
is
\widetilde{Q}
-large.

We have
F|_{X}
is
V,T,W
-large by Equations (
9.75
),(
9.79
) and (
9.76
)
combined with Lemmas
9.13
and
4.15
.

∎

Lemma 9.17
.

Let
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)-0
.
Suppose that there exists
C>0
so that

(9.80)

\left|\langle[\alpha_{1}],\cdots,[\alpha_{m}]\rangle^{X}_{m,d}\right|\leqslant C\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{i}|_{C^{0},\omega}

for each
m\leqslant 2((N+1)\deg(d)+N-3)
and each collection of closed homogenous differential forms
\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{m}
on
X
.
Then

(9.81)

\left|\langle[\alpha_{1}],\cdots,[\alpha_{m}]\rangle^{X}_{m,d}\right|\leqslant m!Ce^{\omega\cdot d}\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{i}|_{C^{0},\omega}

for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
and each collection of closed homogenous differential forms
\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{m}
on
X
.

Proof.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}

and
\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{m}
a collection of closed homogenous differential forms.
Let
\delta:=2((N+1)\deg(d)+N-3)
.
If the degree of
\alpha_{j}
is equal to one or zero for some
j\in\{1,\cdots,m\}
then

(9.82)

\langle[\alpha_{1}],\cdots,[\alpha_{m}]\rangle^{X}_{m,d}

vanishes
and so our inequality is satisfied.
As a result we can assume that the degree of
\alpha_{j}
is
\geqslant 2
for each
j=1,\cdots,m
.
After permuting the
\alpha_{j}
’s we can assume that there exists
k\in\{1,\cdots,m\}
so that
the degree of
\alpha_{j}
is equal to
2
for each
j=1,\cdots,k
and is greater than
2
for each
j\in\{k+1,\cdots,m\}
.
Also, since the dimension of
X_{m,d}
is bounded above by
\delta/2+m
by Lemma
5.2
,
we get (
9.82
) vanishes if
3(m-k)+2k>\delta+2m
.
Hence, we can assume that
m-k\leqslant\delta
.
We have

(9.83)

\left|\langle[\alpha_{1}],\cdots,[\alpha_{m}]\rangle_{m,d}^{X}\right|=

(9.84)

\left|\langle[\alpha_{k+1}],\cdots,[\alpha_{m}]\rangle_{m,d}^{X}\prod_{j=1}^{k}\alpha_{j}\cdot d\right|

(9.85)

\leqslant\left|\langle[\alpha_{k+1}],\cdots,[\alpha_{m}]\rangle_{m,d}^{X}\right|\prod_{j=1}^{k}(|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}\omega\cdot d)

(9.86)

=\left|\langle[\alpha_{k+1}],\cdots,[\alpha_{m}]\rangle_{m,d}^{X}\right|k!\frac{(\omega\cdot d)^{k}}{k!}\prod_{j=1}^{k}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(9.87)

\leqslant\left|\langle[\alpha_{k+1}],\cdots,[\alpha_{m}]\rangle_{m,d}^{X}\right|m!e^{\omega\cdot d}\prod_{j=1}^{k}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}.

Since
m-k\leqslant\delta
we have

(9.88)

\left|\langle[\alpha_{k+1}],\cdots,[\alpha_{m}]\rangle_{m,d}^{X}\right|\leqslant C\prod_{j={k+1}}^{m}|\alpha_{i}|_{C^{0},\omega}

by assumption, and hence (
9.87
) is less than or equal to:

(9.89)

C\prod_{j=k+1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}m!e^{\omega\cdot d}\prod_{j=1}^{k}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(9.90)

=m!Ce^{\omega\cdot d}\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}.

∎

Theorem 9.1
.

Let
r>0
be so that
\mathfrak{I}_{X}
and
\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{2}
are
r
-regular.
Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
d\in H_{2}^{+}(X)
and
\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{m}
closed homogenous differential forms on
X
.
Also, let
\deg(d)\in\mathbb{N}

be the degree of the image of
d
in
H_{2}^{+}(\mathbb{P}^{N})\cong\mathbb{N}
.
Then

(9.91)

\left|\langle[\alpha_{1}],\cdots,[\alpha_{m}]\rangle_{m,d}\right|\leqslant m!\left(4952{N+r\choose N}^{6}(\deg(d)+3)\right)!\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}.

Proof.

We can assume that
d\neq 0
since the
d=0
case is straightforward.
This also implies
N>0
too.

We write
d_{\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}}=(d_{1},d_{2})\in\mathbb{N}^{2}\cong H_{2}^{+}(\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1})
.
We have that
d_{1}
is the degree of the image of
d
in
\mathbb{P}^{N}
and
d_{2}=2rd_{1}
by Lemma
9.13
.
Let
T
be equal to
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}}(r,0)^{\mu}
.
Let
D
,
E
and
F
be as in Lemma
9.16
.
By Proposition
8.11
we have:

(9.92)

\displaystyle D\textnormal{-vol}((T^{k})_{m,(d_{1},d_{2})})\leqslant

\displaystyle(k\mu+(52+3r)(N+2\mu^{2}-1+3)^{2}(d_{1}+d_{2}+1)+(N+2\mu^{2}-1+2\times 72\mu^{4}+30)m)!

\displaystyle/(rk\mu d_{1}+k\mu)!

(9.93)

=(k\mu+(52+3r)(N+2\mu^{2}+2)^{2}((1+2r)d_{1}+1)+(N+2\mu^{2}+144\mu^{4}+29)m)!/(rk\mu d_{1}+k\mu)!

(9.94)

\displaystyle\overset{\textnormal{\eqref{eqnmuinequality}}}{\leqslant}(k\mu+(52+3\mu)(\mu+2\mu^{2}+2)^{2}((1+2\mu)d_{1}+1)+

\displaystyle(\mu+2\mu^{2}+144\mu^{4}+29)m)!/(rk\mu d_{1}+k\mu)!

(9.95)

\displaystyle\leqslant(k\mu+(52\mu+3\mu)(\mu^{2}+2\mu^{2}+2\mu^{2})^{2}((\mu+2\mu)d_{1}+3\mu)+

\displaystyle(\mu^{4}+2\mu^{4}+144\mu^{4}+29\mu^{4})m)!/(rk\mu d_{1}+k\mu)!

(9.96)

\displaystyle\leqslant(k\mu+(55\mu)(5\mu^{2})^{2}(3\mu)(d_{1}+1))+144\mu^{4}m)!/(rk\mu d_{1}+k\mu)!

(9.97)

\displaystyle\leqslant((k\mu+4125\mu^{6}(d_{1}+1))+144\mu^{4}m)!/(rk\mu d_{1}+k\mu)!

and so

(9.98)

\displaystyle D\textnormal{-vol}((T^{k})_{m,(d_{1},d_{2})})\leqslant\eta_{k}:=(k\mu+4125\mu^{6}(d_{1}+1)+144\mu^{4}m)!/(rk\mu d_{1}+k\mu)!.

Let

(9.99)

\overline{C}_{m,d}=C^{X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}/\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}}_{m,d_{X\times\mathbb{P}}}\subset\widetilde{V}_{m,d_{X\times\mathbb{P}}}

be as in Definition
6.4
.
By Proposition
6.5
combined with Lemma
9.16
,
we have

(9.100)

D\textnormal{-vol}((T^{k})_{m,(d_{1},d_{2})})\geqslant(D|_{X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}})\textnormal{-vol}((T^{k}|_{X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}})_{m,d_{X\times\mathbb{P}}},\overline{C}_{m,d})

and hence by (
9.98
) we have

(9.101)

(D|_{X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}})\textnormal{-vol}((T^{k}|_{X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}})_{m,d_{X\times\mathbb{P}}},\overline{C}_{m,d})\leqslant\eta_{k}.

By Proposition
6.5
combined with Lemma
9.16
,
we have

(9.102)

(D|_{X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}})\textnormal{-vol}((T^{k})_{m,d}|_{X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}},\overline{C}_{m,d})\geqslant(D|_{P_{W}})\textnormal{-vol}((T^{k})_{m,d}|_{W},\overline{C}_{m,d}|_{W},C^{P_{W}/X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}}_{m,d_{P_{W}}}|_{W})

(Definition
4.20
).

Also, by Lemma
9.14
, we have that

C^{P_{W}/X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}}_{m,d_{P_{W}}}|_{W}
is

\widetilde{Q}_{m,d_{P_{W}}}|_{W}
.
Hence, by Proposition
5.17
and
Lemmas
9.13
and
9.16
, we have

(9.103)

(E|_{P_{W}})\textnormal{-vol}((T^{k})_{m,d}|_{W},\overline{C}_{m,d}|_{W})\leqslant g_{d}!(D|_{P_{W}})\textnormal{-vol}((T^{k})_{m,d}|_{W},\overline{C}_{m,d}|_{W},C^{P_{W}/X\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}}_{m,d_{P_{W}}}|_{W})

where

(9.104)

\displaystyle g_{d}=c_{1}(\widetilde{Q})\cdot d_{P_{W}}+\dim(\widetilde{Q})

\displaystyle\overset{\textnormal{Lemma \ref{lemmaconvexityofbundles}}}{=}c_{1}(Q)(d)+\dim(Q).

Combining equations (
9.102
), (
9.103
) and (
9.101
), gives

(9.105)

(E|_{P_{W}})\textnormal{-vol}((T^{k})_{m,d}|_{W},\overline{C}_{m,d}|_{W})\leqslant g_{d}!\eta_{k}.

By Proposition
5.21
combined with Lemmas
9.16
and
9.12

we have

(9.106)

(E|_{P_{W}})\textnormal{-vol}((T^{k}|_{W})_{m,d_{P_{W}}},\overline{C}_{m,d}|_{W})\geqslant(F|_{X})\textnormal{-vol}((T^{k}|_{X})_{m,d},\overline{C}_{m,d}|_{X},W_{m,d})

and hence

(9.107)

(F|_{X})\textnormal{-vol}((T^{k}|_{X})_{m,d},\overline{C}_{m,d}|_{X},W_{m,d})\leqslant g_{d}!\eta_{k}.

By Lemma
9.15
combined with the fact that

(9.108)

\dim(Q)\leqslant\dim(\mathcal{O}_{X}(2r)^{2\mu^{2}})=2\mu^{2}

and by Equation (
9.104
)
we get

(9.109)

g_{d}\leqslant 4r\mu^{2}d_{1}+2\mu^{2}

(9.110)

\overset{\textnormal{\eqref{eqnmuinequality}}}{\leqslant}4\mu^{3}(d_{1}+1).

Hence, by Equation (
9.107
), we get

(9.111)

(F|_{X})\textnormal{-vol}((T^{k}|_{X})_{m,d},\overline{C}_{m,d}|_{X},W_{m,d})\leqslant(4\mu^{3}(d_{1}+1))!\eta_{k}

(9.112)

\overset{\textnormal{\eqref{eqnreallreallyterribleinequality}}}{\leqslant}(4\mu^{3}(d_{1}+1)+k\mu+4125\mu^{6}(d_{1}+1)+144\mu^{4}m)!/(rk\mu d_{1}+k\mu)!

(9.113)

\leqslant(k\mu+4129\mu^{6}(d_{1}+1)+144\mu^{4}m)!/(rk\mu d_{1}+k\mu)!.

Hence,

(9.114)

(F|_{X})\textnormal{-vol}((T^{k}|_{X})_{m,d},\overline{C}_{m,d}|_{X},W_{m,d})\leqslant(k\mu+4129\mu^{6}(d_{1}+1)+144\mu^{4}m)!/(rk\mu d_{1}+k\mu)!.

Since
\overline{C}_{m,d}|_{X}=C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}}_{m,d}
by Lemma
9.12
, we have

(9.115)

(F|_{X})\textnormal{-vol}((T^{k}|_{X})_{m,d},C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}}_{m,d},W_{m,d})\leqslant(k\mu+4129\mu^{6}(d_{1}+1)+144\mu^{4}m)!/(rk\mu d_{1}+k\mu)!.

Let
r_{d}
be as in Equation (
7.35
) and
\rho_{d,T}
is as in Equation (
7.36
).
We have
c_{1}(T)d=r\mu d_{1}
and
\dim(T)=\mu
and so

(9.116)

\rho_{d,T}=r\mu d_{1}+\mu+2\overset{\textnormal{\eqref{eqnmuinequality}}}{\leqslant}\mu^{2}d_{1}+\mu+2\mu\leqslant\mu^{2}(d_{1}+3).

Let
\pi_{X}:\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}\to\mathbb{P}^{N}
be the natural projection map as in Definition
9.10
.
Choose a Kähler form
\omega^{\prime}
on
\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}^{2\mu^{2}-1}
Poincaré dual to
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}\times\mathbb{P}}(3\mu^{3}-1,3\mu^{3})
.
Then
\omega+\omega^{\prime}|_{X}
is Poincaré dual to
\frac{1}{4}F|_{X}=F^{\prime}|_{X}
where
F^{\prime}
is as in Lemma
9.16
.
Therefore, since we have a short exact sequence of convex vector bundles

(9.117)

0\to V\to T|_{X}\to W\to 0,

we get by Lemmas
7.7

and

9.16

that

(9.118)

\left|\langle[\alpha_{1}],\cdots,[\alpha_{m}]\rangle_{m,d}^{X}\right|\leqslant

(9.119)

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=0}^{r_{d}}\sum_{k=0}^{r_{d}-\ell}{k+\ell\choose\ell}(23r_{d}+2mN+18m+\rho_{d,T}+23)!(\ell\rho_{d,T})!

\displaystyle(F|_{X})\textnormal{-vol}(C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}}_{m,d},T_{m,d}^{\ell},W_{m,d})\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega+\omega^{\prime}|_{X}}

(9.120)

\displaystyle\overset{\textnormal{Lemma \ref{lemmaaboutpositivedefiniteforms}}}{\leqslant}\sum_{\ell=0}^{r_{d}}\sum_{k=0}^{r_{d}-\ell}{k+\ell\choose\ell}(23r_{d}+2mN+18m+\rho_{d,T}+23)!(\ell\rho_{d,T})!

\displaystyle(F|_{X})\textnormal{-vol}(C^{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}}_{m,d},T_{m,d}^{\ell},W_{m,d})\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(9.121)

\displaystyle\overset{\textnormal{\eqref{eqnefe854}}}{\leqslant}\sum_{\ell=0}^{r_{d}}\sum_{k=0}^{r_{d}-\ell}{k+\ell\choose\ell}(23r_{d}+2mN+18m+\rho_{d,T}+23)!(\ell\rho_{d,T})!

\displaystyle(\ell\mu+4129\mu^{6}(d_{1}+1)+144\mu^{4}m)!/(rl\mu d_{1}+\ell\mu)!\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(9.122)

\displaystyle\overset{\textnormal{\eqref{eqnrehodty}}}{=}\sum_{\ell=0}^{r_{d}}\sum_{k=0}^{r_{d}-\ell}{k+\ell\choose\ell}(23r_{d}+2mN+18m+\mu^{2}(d_{1}+3)+23)!(lr\mu d_{1}+\ell\mu+2\ell)!

\displaystyle(\ell\mu+4129\mu^{6}(d_{1}+1)+144\mu^{4}m)!/(rl\mu d_{1}+\ell\mu)!\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(9.123)

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{\ell=0}^{r_{d}}\sum_{k=0}^{r_{d}-\ell}{k+\ell\choose\ell}(23r_{d}+2mN+18m+\mu^{2}(d_{1}+3)+23)!(\ell\mu+4129\mu^{6}(d_{1}+1)+144\mu^{4}m)!

\displaystyle\times(lrd_{1}+\ell\mu+2\ell)^{2\ell}\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(9.124)

\displaystyle\leqslant(r_{d}+1)^{2}2^{2r_{d}}(23r_{d}+2mN+18m+\mu^{2}(d_{1}+3)+23)!

\displaystyle\times(r_{d}\mu+4129\mu^{6}(d_{1}+1)+144\mu^{4}m)!(r_{d}rd_{1}+r_{d}\mu+2r_{d})^{2r_{d}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(9.125)

\displaystyle\leqslant(r_{d}+1)^{2}2^{2r_{d}}(23r_{d}+2mN+18m+\mu^{2}(d_{1}+3)+23)!

\displaystyle\times(r_{d}\mu+4129\mu^{6}(d_{1}+1)+144\mu^{4}m)!(r_{d}\mu d_{1}+r_{d}\mu+2r_{d})^{2r_{d}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(9.126)

\displaystyle\leqslant(r_{d}+1)^{2}2^{2r_{d}}(23r_{d}+2mN+18m+\mu^{2}(d_{1}+3)+23)!

\displaystyle\times(r_{d}\mu+4129\mu^{6}(d_{1}+1)+144\mu^{4}m)!(r_{d}\mu(d_{1}+3))^{2r_{d}}\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

Now by Equation (
7.35
),

(9.127)

r_{d}=(N+1)d_{1}+N+c_{1}(V)(d)+c_{1}(W)(d)+\dim(V)+\dim(W)

(9.128)

\overset{\textnormal{Lemma \ref{lemmaboundsforfirstchernclasses}}}{\leqslant}(N+1)d_{1}+N+\mu rd_{1}+\mu rd_{1}+\dim(V)+\dim(W).

Now

(9.129)

\dim(V)+\dim(W)\leqslant\dim(\mathcal{O}_{X}(r)^{\mu})=\mu.

Hence, (
9.128
) is less than or equal to:

(9.130)

(N+1)d_{1}+N+\mu rd_{1}+\mu rd_{1}+\mu

(9.131)

=((N+1)+2\mu r)d_{1}+N+\mu

(9.132)

\overset{\textnormal{\eqref{eqnmuinequality}}}{\leqslant}((\mu+1)+2\mu^{2})d_{1}+\mu+\mu

(9.133)

\leqslant 4\mu^{2}d_{1}+2\mu^{2}

(9.134)

\leqslant 4\mu^{2}(d_{1}+1).

Combining this with (
9.116
) we get (
9.126
) is less than or equal to:

(9.135)

\displaystyle\leqslant(4\mu^{2}(d_{1}+1)+1)^{2}2^{2(4\mu^{2}(d_{1}+1))}(23\times 4\mu^{2}(d_{1}+1)+2mN+18m+\mu^{2}(d_{1}+3)+23)!

\displaystyle\times((4\mu^{2}(d_{1}+1))\mu+4129\mu^{6}(d_{1}+1)+144\mu^{4}m)!((4\mu^{2}(d_{1}+1))\mu(d_{1}+3))^{8\mu^{2}(d_{1}+1)}\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(9.136)

\displaystyle\overset{\textnormal{\eqref{eqnmuinequality}}}{\leqslant}(4\mu^{2}(d_{1}+1)+1)^{2}2^{8\mu^{2}(d_{1}+1)}(92\mu^{2}(d_{1}+1)+2m\mu+18m+3\mu^{2}(d_{1}+1)+23)!

\displaystyle\times((4\mu^{2}(d_{1}+1))\mu+4129\mu^{6}(d_{1}+1)+144\mu^{4}m)!((12\mu^{3}(d_{1}+1)^{2}))^{8\mu^{2}(d_{1}+1)}\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(9.137)

\displaystyle\leqslant(4\mu^{2}(d_{1}+1)+1)^{2}2^{8\mu^{2}(d_{1}+1)}(92\mu^{2}(d_{1}+1)+2m\mu+18m+3\mu^{2}(d_{1}+1)+23)!

\displaystyle\times((4\mu^{3}(d_{1}+1))+4129\mu^{6}(d_{1}+1)+144\mu^{4}m)!((4\mu^{2}(d_{1}+1)))^{16\mu^{2}(d_{1}+1)}\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(9.138)

\displaystyle\overset{\textnormal{\eqref{eqnreallreallyterribleinequality}}}{\leqslant}(4\mu^{2}(d_{1}+1)+1+2+2+8\mu^{2}(d_{1}+1)+92\mu^{2}(d_{1}+1)+2m\mu+18m+3\mu^{2}(d_{1}+1)+23+

\displaystyle 4\mu^{3}(d_{1}+1)+4129\mu^{6}(d_{1}+1)+144\mu^{4}m+4\mu^{2}(d_{1}+1)+16\mu^{2}(d_{1}+1))!\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(9.139)

\displaystyle=(28+2m\mu+131\mu^{2}(d_{1}+1)+18m+

\displaystyle 4\mu^{3}(d_{1}+1)+4129\mu^{6}(d_{1}+1)+144\mu^{4}m)!\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(9.140)

\displaystyle\leqslant(4292\mu^{6}(d_{1}+1)+164\mu^{4}m)!\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}.

So, putting everything together so far we get:

(9.141)

\displaystyle\left|\langle[\alpha_{1}],\cdots,[\alpha_{m}]\rangle_{m,d}^{X}\right|\leqslant(4292\mu^{6}(d_{1}+1)+164\mu^{4}m)!\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}.

Therefore, if

(9.142)

m\leqslant 2((N+1)d_{1}+N-3),

we get

(9.143)

\left|\langle[\alpha_{1}],\cdots,[\alpha_{m}]\rangle_{m,d}^{X}\right|\leqslant

(9.144)

\displaystyle(4292\mu^{6}(d_{1}+1)+164\mu^{4}(2((N+1)d_{1}+N-3)))!\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(9.145)

\displaystyle\overset{\textnormal{\eqref{eqnmuinequality}}}{\leqslant}(4292\mu^{6}(d_{1}+1)+164\mu^{4}(2((\mu+1)d_{1}+\mu-3)))!\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(9.146)

\displaystyle\leqslant(4292\mu^{6}(d_{1}+1)+164\mu^{4}(2((\mu+\mu)d_{1}+\mu)))!\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(9.147)

\displaystyle\leqslant(4292\mu^{6}(d_{1}+1)+164\mu^{4}(4\mu d_{1}+4\mu))!\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(9.148)

\leqslant(4948\mu^{6}(d_{1}+1))!\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}.

Combining this with Lemma
9.17
,
we get:

(9.149)

\left|\langle[\alpha_{1}],\cdots,[\alpha_{m}]\rangle_{m,d}^{X}\right|\leqslant m!e^{\omega\cdot d}(4948\mu^{6}(d_{1}+1))!\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
.
Hence, for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
,

(9.150)

\left|\langle[\alpha_{1}],\cdots,[\alpha_{m}]\rangle_{m,d}^{X}\right|\leqslant m!4^{d_{1}}(4948\mu^{6}(d_{1}+1))!\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(9.151)

\overset{\textnormal{\eqref{eqnreallreallyterribleinequality}}}{\leqslant}m!(4+d_{1}+4948\mu^{6}(d_{1}+1))!\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(9.152)

\leqslant m!(4952\mu^{6}(d_{1}+1))!\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

(9.153)

\overset{\textnormal{\eqref{eqndefnofmu}}}{=}m!\left(4952{N+r\choose N}^{6}(d_{1}+1)\right)!\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}.

∎

We will now prove the main theorem
1.1
.
Let us recall its statement:

Theorem
.

Suppose
X\subset\mathbb{P}^{N}
is cut out by polynomials of degree at most
\delta_{X}\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
d\in H_{2}(X;\mathbb{Z})
be a curve class and let
\deg(d)\in\mathbb{N}
be its image in
H_{2}(\mathbb{P}^{N};\mathbb{Z})\cong\mathbb{Z}
.
Then for each tuple
\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{m}
of homogenous closed differential forms on
X
we have

(9.154)

\left|\langle[\alpha_{1}],\cdots,[\alpha_{m}]\rangle^{X}_{m,d}\right|\leqslant m!\left(4952{N+(4\delta_{X})^{2^{N-1}}+1\choose N}^{6}(\deg(d)+1)\right)!\prod_{j=1}^{m}|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}

where
|\alpha_{j}|_{C^{0},\omega}
is the
C^{0}
norm of
\alpha_{j}
with respect to the induced Fubini Study metric on
X
for each
j
.

Proof.

Recall that
r>0
is such that
\mathfrak{I}_{X}
and
\mathfrak{I}_{X}^{2}
are
r
-regular.
By
[
BM93
, Theorem 3.7]
, we can assume that
r\leqslant(4\delta_{X})^{2^{N-1}}
.
Hence, our result follows from Theorem
9.1
above.
∎
